> **Lossless anchor manifests, 23 November 2026:** packet transport
> `claims_property_columns_v6` additionally encodes consecutive anchor IDs with
> exact `[prefix, first, stop, suffix, width]` ranges. Expansion preserves every
> literal ID, order, duplicate and numeric zero padding. It does not reduce the
> source scope or omit scientific information. All canonical packet fields remain
> unchanged and replay checks exact reconstruction. Earlier v5 receipts remain
> decodable. Source-card provider schemas explicitly enforce the existing READY,
> NEED_EVIDENCE and unresolved-outcome rules for original and corrected batches.
> New runtime hashes require a separate frozen policy; old clocks and scores stay
> in their original generation.

> **Complete source batching, 23 November 2026:** source and structured-packet
> allowances are per request, not per paper. Source size alone triggers complete
> independent requirement/archival-row/assertion-group partitions, retaining shared
> definitions, qualifications and joint estimate/uncertainty dependencies. For a
> remaining oversized source scope, both PRIMARY and SECONDARY independently
> inspect every source batch for every complete assertion query. Queries retain
> exact original asserted properties, relations, identity/context and ownership;
> final scientific packets retain their complete closure ledgers and dependencies.
> Retrieval returns only original anchor IDs for support, contradictions, definitions,
> conditions and provenance, never summaries or verdicts. Join both roles' selected
> anchors and all mandatory source witnesses before judging the complete case.
> Empty individual batches do not prove absence. Complete independent inspection
> receipts can establish that no support was found; document-identity anchors alone
> do not positively support any scientific assertion. Failed or incomplete scans
> hold rather than certifying absence. Replay verifies every role/query has exact,
> disjoint, exhaustive original-source coverage, the retained provider responses,
> independent model/runtime identities and unchanged original assertions. No batch
> earns an additional scientific bit: each original parent requires every necessary
> shard. An inseparable assertion or mandatory joint witness set still exceeding
> the per-call allowance retains HOLD without truncation. This implementation change
> requires a new frozen policy generation; prior requests and clocks stay immutable.

> **Source disagreement routing:** a secondary `SOURCE_UNRESOLVED` interpretation
> of a source proposal goes to the independent source arbiter, just like a
> substantive `REVISE`. The arbiter may accept, correct, request captured context,
> or retain HOLD. This is not an automatic pass or an additional technical retry.

> **Source-fidelity clarification, 23 November 2026:** qualitative findings remain
> valid at the reported level. Unreported effect sizes, exact p-values or statistic
> types are not automatically unresolved source meaning. Independent source review
> can correct an unsupported draft assertion and remove its draft-only citations,
> with concrete source issues and arbiter confirmation. It must retain every
> readable source-supported finding; candidate outputs never enter this decision.

> **Owner-approved testnet recovery — 22 November 2026:** the sealed scientific
> judging deadline can be frozen at 7,200 seconds instead of the legacy 1,800
> seconds. The current recovery uses 7,200 seconds for all entrants. Miner
> execution, cost and token limits remain governed by the unchanged worker policy.
> Existing generation clocks are never reset; this change requires a new audited
> scoring-policy generation. Recovery/corrections within a generation keep its
> original start/deadline. Successful exact-request cache reads remain permitted
> after expiry, while new dispatch remains prohibited.
>
> Lossless source transport `claims_source_documents_v2` stores each literal
> document UID/hash once and uses `document_ref` on anchors; decoding reconstructs
> every original text, identifier and locator exactly. Packet transport
> `claims_property_columns_v5` additionally interns repeated exact output refs.
> Returned scientific decisions must still use literal original pointers/IDs.
> Independently confirmed source-card inventory defects create common frame
> corrections, retain source-only review evidence, and rerank with the same secret.

> Source requirement identity explicitly records analysis_scope and adjustment_scope
> from the source, including which analysis a qualitative restatement refers to.
> NOT_APPLICABLE and SOURCE_UNSPECIFIED preserve absent source information; these
> fields do not add miner output requirements. Independent review verifies the scope.

> **Bounded independent match identity — 21 November 2026:** when a scientific
> CONTENT packet cannot fit, two independent roles (SECONDARY and ARBITER) review
> each complete original owner against supplied-source evidence. Small owner batches
> keep the existing 120-second timeout, four contexts per case and 16 global requests.
> Every owner needs an explicit decision. Exclusion requires positive source evidence
> and matching non-KEEP dispositions from both roles; uncertainty or disagreement
> retains it. Scientific fields remain scientific in every storage section and may
> not be excluded as context/format. Wrong numbers/qualifiers and equivalent encodings
> at the same referent stay. The target, original output and C/T/P/Q rules do not change.
> An immutable identity receipt preserves the complete initial lookup and owner groups,
> original scope headers, source witnesses and both review journals. Replay reconstructs
> initial proposals, checks full disjoint batch coverage and reproduces the exact
> conservative exclusions. Excluded assertions remain in the original P population.

> Oversized CONTENT cards may assess complete requirements separately, repeating
> all their full shared scientific objects, endpoints, closures and contradictory
> views. A requirement is never split into partial candidate views. The same parent
> bit requires every mandatory shard; weights and scientific source targets remain
> unchanged. A single complete requirement that cannot fit still holds.

> **Property-scoped context and lossless transport clarification — 21 November 2026:**
> SOURCE_CONTEXT correspondence cites original property pointers. Non-value metadata
> matches render those exact properties with original identity/scope headers, rather
> than unrelated numerical outcome properties. A matched input value remains a full
> original field; focal scientific findings keep their necessary full assertions.
> Replay verifies the exact matched properties and rejects a partial focal quantity.
> Transport v3 may factor repeated ordered property names into property_name_sets;
> property_names_ref plus the same-length literal-value vector reconstructs the exact
> original property pairs. Unknown templates or mismatched lengths are invalid.
> Provider matches use an object keyed by every source requirement ID exactly once,
> with an authored reference array (possibly empty) per ID. The strict adapter only
> decodes that complete map into the unchanged canonical RequirementMatch list.
> Missing/unknown keys fail; no match, value or scientific decision is synthesized.

> Independent THIS_BATCH_ONLY matching scans may run in parallel, up to four
> request contexts per case, under the existing 16-request global gate and four
> case-worker limit. Aggregate matches in their original batch order and retain
> every successful/failed attempt ledger. All original components still require
> complete inspection; failed batches cannot certify absence. Scientific generation
> deadlines, request timeouts, retries, evidence limits and sampling are unchanged.

> Lossless transport v2 additionally interns repeated long original JSON literal
> strings in packet.literal_pool. A property slot {"literal_ref":"L0"} expands to
> that exact retained string, including original number spellings and whitespace.
> All scientific assertions remain present; decoding must round-trip exactly to
> the full audit packet. Literal pooling changes transport size, not evidence.

> Family membership context uses explicit original identity/scope headers for
> otherwise unrelated co-members, in all review kinds. The full family declaration,
> matched results and their necessary scientific endpoints, closures and boundary
> definitions remain intact. No unseen numeric assertions receive credit from a
> header. Replay rejects replacing focal scientific assertions with such headers.

> **Source requirement granularity clarification — 21 November 2026:**
> Keep the coherent parent source card and all required information. Independently
> reported condition-specific quantities/tests and independently checkable context
> definitions/conventions use separate complete requirements within that card.
> Each estimate retains its uncertainty/test; intervals and genuinely joint
> multivariate findings stay joint. Independent source review checks granularity
> before any output is inspected. All necessary shards must pass for the same
> parent bit; sampling weights and scientific credit rules are unchanged.

> Provider schemas may bind immutable request IDs, applicable dimensions and
> available witness/pointer domains, and express the existing outcome/witness rules
> using supported grammar constructs. Canonical response contracts and scientific
> decisions remain unchanged. Exact distinct inspection/dimension sets are checked
> in code; unsupported provider grammar keywords are not relied on for validation.

> SOURCE_CONTEXT association endpoints may use explicit partial identity/scope
> context_headers with original property pointers; unrendered numeric values may
> not receive credit from these headers. Focused objects and numeric findings keep
> their required complete assertions. Replay validates header properties against
> the immutable original index.

> **Source-context and audit ownership clarification — 21 November 2026:**
> Source-only drafts declare SCIENTIFIC_RESULT or SOURCE_CONTEXT, independently
> checked in source review. Findings retain joint dependencies; context covers only
> source background, protocol inputs/conditions, definitions or shared conventions.
> Complete context requirements may be separate mandatory shards with common source
> identity; every representation of each requirement remains in its shard. No
> within-requirement truncation or selective favorable-view credit is allowed.
> P groups original assertions and their incident associations by scientific owner
> without merging all consumers through shared context. Each shard owns exactly its
> packet.match.candidate_refs. Replay checks their disjoint exhaustive union against
> the retained parent ownership ledger. Unowned rendered definitions are context.

> **Typed correspondence and provider-routing amendment — 21 November 2026:**
> MatchResponse returns only requirement-scoped matches; the controller derives
> MatchResult.candidate_refs from their union. This removes redundant model-authored
> lists without dropping any returned requirement match. DeepSeek keeps the same
> approved model ID and is pinned to the advertised DeepInfra strict-output endpoint
> family after Baidu repeatedly returned fenced responses to strict-JSON requests.
> Original and filtered endpoint snapshots are retained in the frozen descriptor.

> **Packet and table-matching implementation clarification — 21 November 2026:**
> Every content candidate must be assigned to a source requirement; `candidate_refs`
> equals the union of `matches[].output_refs`. Context-only neighbors are retained
> through explicit packet dependencies, not unassigned candidate hits. Local closure
> expansion starts from original candidate owners once; a shared member does not
> activate every other consumer's closure. Required-table identity compares all
> original TableObject declarations directly, with blind primary/secondary review
> and arbitration for disagreement, ambiguity or multiple proposed archives. T uses
> that retained correspondence and checks selected original CSV rows and their direct
> links, without repeating scientific-content lookup or importing every consumer's
> complete closure. C still completes its exhaustive original-index search.
> Judge transport is lossless: properties are ordered `[name, original_json_literal]`
> pairs; a null literal slot marks absence, while the string `"null"` is a present
> JSON null. Original property pointers derive from the object/relation pointer and
> escaped name, with explicit exceptions. Source SHA-256 is factored once per
> document; full source document and anchor IDs remain literal. Rich audit packets
> and original bytes remain retained. The 32k caps count the actual lossless judge
> transport; replay verifies the transported assertions against the retained packet.

> **Owner-approved validator budget amendment — 21 November 2026:** source
> evidence and each complete structured review packet now have a 32,000-token
> allowance from the first request. Up to two source-context retrievals remain
> permitted within that allowance (`source_token_budgets=[32000,32000,32000]`).
> This supersedes earlier 8k/16k/24k review budgets. Judge output stays 16,384;
> miner models, cost and execution limits are unchanged. Legacy visual table-layout
> subdivision instructions are inactive for the independently parsed Docling PDF
> path. Retained earlier runs keep their original policy and results.

# Claims validator implementation guide

> **Owner source-evidence decision, 21 November 2026 — `docling_pdf_derivatives_v1`:**
> The validator independently runs Docling, with OCR and table structure, on the
> actual supplied PDFs. It may reuse a controller-owned cache bound to identical
> PDF bytes and retained parser settings. Retained Docling text, OCR, equations,
> captions and native table cells are the authoritative evidence for judging.
> This supersedes mandatory page-image inventory/review, model-authored rectangles,
> image cropping and image-capable reviewer requirements in the original guide.
> Native Docling layout/row-span proofs replace the independent visual table-layout
> receipts for this mode. Prose eligibility and scientific result judgments remain
> source-only, with independent review. Parser omissions/OCR errors are a disclosed
> limit; there is no claim of independent visual verification or recovery of plot
> values absent from Docling. Additional submitted table exports do not fail merely
> because Docling detected fewer tables: every qualifying Docling source table must
> still be delivered, and submitted assertions remain subject to source review.
> Source data-row counts exclude parser-marked header/section-only rows. Additional
> label-only CSV context rows retain their original physical coordinates and bytes.
> The extended extraction schema, scientific dimensions and C/T/P/Q weights remain
> unchanged. Miner models and the $0.10 miner inference cap remain unchanged.


> **Owner row-count clarification, 23 November 2026 — `source_row_count_review_v1`:**
> CSV file shape, declared dimensions and original coordinates remain mechanically
> enforced. A disagreement with Docling's source data-row count is not, by itself,
> evidence of a miner error: source rows may have been merged, split or classified
> as headings. Before that disagreement can set G=0, PRIMARY and SECONDARY blindly
> inspect the original source rows and header/context anchors in complete bounded
> batches. ARBITER resolves any disagreement. Verified counts require witnesses to
> every owned source row and complete, nonoverlapping table coverage. The review
> contains no candidate CSV, candidate row count or candidate scientific assertions.
> If the captured source layout cannot establish the count, record a common source
> hold and apply the same recovery/exclusion to every entrant. Do not assign zero
> to a miner on the basis of an unresolved parser layout. Additional CSV rows that
> exactly retain the CSV header or source-supported declared header levels are
> context; preserve their bytes and physical coordinates. Scientific values are
> never dropped, repaired or changed by this formatting map. Strict source-count
> failures carry independently replayable review receipts. Earlier frozen receipts
> retain their original policy; this clarification applies to newly frozen policies.


> **Judge-route amendment, 21 November 2026:** use DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731
> (`deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731`) for SECONDARY in place of GLM. Increase
> the frozen per-call judge output allowance to 16,384 tokens. Source reading
> and PRIMARY remain Qwen3.8 Flash; ARBITER remains Kimi K2.5. These are
> validator settings; miner execution limits and the $0.10 miner cost cap are
> unchanged. DeepSeek is text-only: image-bearing evidence must never be
> silently omitted or treated as inspected by this route.

> **Deployment decision, 21 November 2026:** the deployment owner has waived the
> unavailable signed scientific truth corpus and its pre-activation accuracy
> thresholds. Implementation and rollout may proceed without it. This is an
> explicit exception to §7.3 items 2–3, not evidence that judge accuracy is qualified.
> Freeze `scientific_accuracy_policy="unmeasured_without_truth_corpus"` in the round
> configuration. Retain conformance, source-grounded review, operational testing,
> recovery and publication checks. Never report unmeasured error rates as passing.


> Round policy amendment, 20 November 2026: use the extended repository schema
> including optional data/code availability and V28–V30. Evaluate only sources
> supplied to every miner. Unsupplied supplements/external tables are outside
> scope and cause neither miner penalties nor common source holds. Supplied
> supplements remain required. This policy overrides contrary acquisition/scope
> language below. C/T/P weights and the Q formula remain unchanged.


> **Retrospective PDF verification profile, 21 November 2026:** image-bearing
> verification may explicitly freeze approved MiniMax M3.1 Flash as SECONDARY,
> with its mandatory reasoning set to `minimal`, while retaining Qwen source/primary
> and Kimi arbitration. The default text/table SECONDARY remains DeepSeek. All
> source pixels remain in scope. Docling remains the OCR/layout parser. This profile
> does not activate or change a live round. Inventory validation permits a null
> request-local discovery key when an independently verified table UID is reused;
> the controller must still validate that UID and the nonempty source regions.

Protocol: `claims-source-task-v1`  
Extraction format: Claims Extraction Schema `1.1`  
Integration target: `DeSciClaims/Claims`, commit `0ba3f4bada37fdef54a8845ca6ef1ee2596c023f`  
Extraction-schema SHA-256: `78f5b279dfbf4f2fec5fc83b2219350e43e3fece5c58e94f81fb5d146d10a5e2`

2. [Validation workflow](#2-validation-workflow)
3. [Validation rules](#3-validation-rules)

5. [Judge decisions and failure handling](#5-judge-decisions-and-failure-handling)

Scientific criteria and prompts](#appendix-c-scientific-criteria-and-judge-instructions) · [D. Helpers, fixtures, and tests](#appendix-d-executable-helpers-fixtures-and-tests) · [E. Engineering completeness map](#appendix-e-engineering-completeness-map) · [F. Extraction schema](#appendix-f-authoritative-extraction-schema)

## 1. Task, inputs, and outputs

Implement protocol `claims-source-task-v1` for Claims Extraction Schema `1.1`. Execute each committed extraction pipeline on a common paper, check its original JSON/CSV package against authoritative source material, and produce one common immutable Silver evaluation receipt with per-pipeline quality scores. The required behavior, wire meanings, algorithms, and policies in this guide are mandatory; internal classes, modules, and storage layout are implementation choices. Appendix B separates required wire invariants from optional Python representations. Appendix F is the complete extraction schema; Appendix A fixes configuration and resource limits.

Accept every source-supported, schema-compliant organization. Preserve each pipeline's submitted objects and graph; do not select an ideal organization, require matching family partitions or IDs, or combine fields from different pipelines. Package, schema, reference, and graph checks are exhaustive. Scientific fidelity and scientific-result coverage use the prescribed samples; required source/table delivery is checked exhaustively. Field findings identify errors but do not create independently weighted field rewards.

| Term/component | Required meaning |
|---|---|
| Submitted package | Original extraction JSON and all delivered CSV bytes, bound by immutable hashes. |
| Bronze | The reference pipeline's original output; available for retrieval and verified discoveries, never ground truth. |
| Source task | A source-defined coherent scientific result or archival table block, independent of submitted family IDs. |
| Local closure | Reverse reachability from a submitted eligible core through reviewed subordinate-to-core edges. |
| Result family | Submitted grouping by substantive question and generating investigation. |
| Silver | Shared source/tasks/evidence, original-output findings, sampling weights, and scores for one frozen compared-input pool. |
| `G`, `C`, `T`, `P` | Mandatory package/mechanical gate; common content-task performance; sampled archival fidelity; output-assertion audit. Scoring and empty cases are specified in §§5–7. There is no organization score. |
| Adjudication | Independent resolution of failed, disputed, or unresolved source-verification cases; ordinary primary verification is not a vote between miners. |

### 1.1 Run inputs and source scope

Require frozen settings; immutable round, stage, paper, and pipeline IDs; the complete compared pipeline set; committed executable bytes and hashes; a trusted `SourcePacket`; worker policy; and approved model-route credentials. Credentials remain outside receipts and hashes. The compared set includes the reference, the incumbent when present, and every scheduled challenger. Do not add or replace packages after sampling begins.

The source packet contains the actual main-source version, supplied supplements, associated supplements identified by that source, and explicitly in-scope cited sources. For each source retain ID, media type, byte length, SHA-256, immutable location, version, and acquisition/review status. Supported material includes PDF, captured UTF-8 text/HTML, and source-provided tabular files. URLs are provenance; judges inspect captured bytes and do not follow links. Resolve required supplementary material before scheduling. Unavailable, unreadable, or version-unresolved controller input causes common `HOLD_SOURCE`; an available required source omitted by a pipeline is a pipeline coverage failure.

Give all pipelines identical source bytes, schema, required scope, and worker policy. Request a complete extraction once; never request a Bronze-named family, disclose selected tasks, or rerun after scientific findings. If Bronze and incumbent are the same committed pipeline, retain one package with both roles. A Bronze-only helper supplies retrieval/nominations, not a score row or its own C/T/P evaluation; its failure is diagnostic. A purported source defect still requires independent source confirmation. All incumbent/challenger roles receive complete evaluation.

### 1.2 Worker request and package collection

Retain existing execution identity, gateway configuration, and main-PDF argument; add:

```json
{
  "output_format": "claims-extraction-1.1",
  "validation_protocol": "claims-source-task-v1",
  "source_manifest_file": "sources.json",
  "extraction_schema_file": "claims_extraction.schema.json",
  "output_json_file": "output.json",
  "output_directory": "results",
  "required_table_scope": "main_and_associated_supplements"
}
```

`sources.json` is the credential-free manifest with paths relative to read-only input. Supply assigned `paper.id` and extraction prefix; independent pipelines receive distinct extraction IDs. Matching never requires equal IDs across pipelines. Require `results/output.json` and exactly named `<TableObject.id>.csv` files beside it.

Apply Appendix A's execution, bundle, file-count, byte, object, relationship, memory, log, and network limits. Stop all guest processes before collection. Read regular files without following symlinks; verify owner, byte length, inode/stat stability, and hash during collection. Reject duplicate archive names, absolute/traversal paths, devices, links, unexpected subdirectories, undeclared CSVs, and missing declared files. Transport actual JSON/CSV bytes and manifest together; retain logs separately, not as extraction evidence.

A miner timeout, resource breach, invalid extraction, or missing output is `PIPELINE_INVALID`, scoring zero on an otherwise eligible paper. Controller/source/provider failures use common holds. A demonstrated controller transfer failure permits one retry with identical package/source/policy: retain both receipts and use the first complete successful result. Scientific failures never authorize extraction retries. Preflight common bundle size; a source bundle exceeding the worker profile is ineligible.

### 1.3 Required retained products

Persist source bytes/manifest/frame, seed commitment, execution receipts, original packages, complete compared-input-pool hash, mechanical findings, selections and source cards, matching traces, rendered packets, raw and validated judge responses with usage, final verdicts, common Silver receipt, exact score vectors, pairwise rows, and audit/publication receipts. Every decision must be replayable from these artifacts. Holds retain diagnostics but have no measured quality score. Terminal exclusions use only the flagged transport placeholders specified in §5.

## 2. Validation workflow

### 2.1 Controller sequence

| State | Required action and transition |
|---|---|
| `CONFIGURED` | Validate Appendix A settings, approved route descriptors, schema/runtime hashes, dependencies, and worker capabilities. Configuration failure stops intake. |
| `SOURCE_PREPARED` | Acquire sources; prepare and independently verify the source/table inventory below before viewing pipeline organizations. Unavailable source → `HOLD_SOURCE`; no eligible content/tables → `INELIGIBLE`. |
| `EXECUTING` | Commit the paper secret before execution; run every pipeline once under §1. |
| `INPUTS_SEALED` | Require original output or terminal package-failure receipt for every pipeline. Hash the complete pool, reveal the secret only to the trusted evaluator, and select common samples. |
| `CHECKING` | Run exhaustive checks and source mapping. Invalid pipelines receive `G=0`; valid pipelines continue. |
| `VERIFYING` | Build source cards, match original output, render complete cases, and run scientific verification/escalation under §§5–7 and Appendix C. Apply common corrections when required. |
| `CONSISTENT` | Check receipt references, decision coherence, and complete common weights; inconsistency requires correction or hold. |
| `FROZEN` | Freeze the common revision; compute exact scores, pair rows, and audit receipts. |
| `PUBLISHABLE` | Publish only after required audits and common-grid checks. A correction creates another revision and invalidates derived publication artifacts. |

Eligibility, required scope, corrections, and source/evaluator holds apply to the complete compared set. An invalid pipeline does not prevent evaluating valid packages. Reference omissions or failures do not invalidate source-supported challenger results.

Assign one controller owner to each exact shared preparation operation. Source parsing, inventory/layout generation and review, common sampling, and selected-card generation/review produce shared immutable artifacts at their prescribed stages and revisions. Pipeline and pair workers await those artifacts instead of independently recreating them. Compute each scored pipeline’s Q once per frozen paper revision and derive every pair row from those scores. Distinct candidate packets retain their required separate verification.

### 2.2 Prepare the independent source frame

Extract text, reading order, headings, equations, table/figure bounds, and images from frozen source bytes. Retain parser/OCR versions/options and text/image/page hashes and anchors. Parsed text locates evidence; readable source images override OCR substitutions. Keep both with a transcription note.

Cache deterministic source derivatives using source-byte hashes, scope, parser/OCR/rendering/tokenizer identities and options, and every other input affecting their bytes. Reuse identical page images, text indexes, evidence fragments and token counts; retain their derivation hashes. This cache does not replace independent source review or authorize reuse of a model response under changed request bindings.

Use Appendix C's source-inventory prompt in source order, one page per call, with its image and at most 8,000 source tokens. Subdivide dense pages into disjoint anchored regions; retain headings/captions as context. Account for every region as prose/equation, numerical table, figure, bibliography/administrative, or unresolved, including supplements and explicit continuation requests. Independently review the whole inventory and table identities against source, then reconcile substantive disagreements through the source arbiter. Uncovered pages or unresolved continuations block sealing. Source-only proposals and reviews must not expose candidate identities or organizations. Apply Appendix C's exact review contracts: acceptance requires faithful complete content; revision requires a concrete source defect and complete corrected artifact, not wording preference.

Discover tables through source labels, grouping, and geometry; existing parser/table IDs are not prerequisites, and one image can contain several tables. Reconcile physical panels and continuations across requests before assigning controller-owned table identities. If inventory discovers a required companion source, complete the common packet before execution. Adding required source bytes later requires a new common packet and fresh executions for the whole compared set.

Frame identities use source locators. Anchor identity binds source-byte hash, zero-based internal page/record index, region ordinal, and captured text/image-fragment hash. Display pages and CSV coordinates are one-based; use Appendix B's internal half-open coordinates. A source table combines its panels/continuations; distinct source tables remain distinct even if they report the same estimate.

Eligible content includes numerical/descriptive findings, comparisons, diagnostic/statistical results, substantive mathematical results/equations, and necessary definitions/conditions, including null and negative findings. Exclude bibliography numbers, administrative dates/affiliations, page furniture, and unsupported boilerplate. Optional JSON properties do not make necessary source qualifications optional.

Build prose/equation tiles in source order within each section, greedily appending complete parser-recorded sentences up to 400 whitespace-delimited words. Freeze sentence boundaries. Keep equations with defining sentences when possible; split an oversized sentence at the last whitespace before word 400. Own disjoint spans; adjacent evidence contributes context without owning another result. Represent substantive figures through caption/result-text tiles with image evidence, including visually accessible results without adequate text. Numeric plots are not source tables; image cases require approved image-capable routes.

For every numerical table independently establish all ordered data rows, panel/row labels, full logical column hierarchy, and header/note anchors. Include source records with blank or missing entries; exclude captions, separators, repeated headers, footnotes, and panel-label-only rows. Do not require transcription of every numerical cell. Apply Appendix C's complete layout pagination, row ownership, independent review, and assembly procedure. Every physical part/final owned scope must have a complete accepted layout; retain layout receipts and their frame binding. Derive `data_row_count` from verified rows, never a candidate declaration or parser guess. Unrecoverable row identity/order or incomplete layout causes common hold.

Use source-ordered blocks of five data rows, with a shorter final block. Preserve row/panel anchors and union-of-columns interpretation for incompatible panels. Freeze `SourceFrame` separately from the source-packet hash. Reinterpreting OCR/layout does not change source bytes or authorize re-extraction.

### 2.3 Generate selected source tasks and map inventories

Generate cards only for selected content tiles. A card is one independently assessable coherent result with source-present outcome/quantity, counted set/population, intervention/comparator, time/model/adjustment, role, values/operators/uncertainty, and essential conditions. Anchor every requirement. Keep an estimate with its uncertainty/test/contrast, one joint test as one test, and an expression with its necessary locally bound definitions. Do not infer absent details.

Cards specify required information and applicable encoding constraints, never canonical IDs, record counts, unique labels where alternatives are allowed, or family partitions. Permit faithful `other`, contextual qualifiers, parent/child intervals, and shared same-ID objects under the schema. Table scientific cards concern results and interpretation; archival tasks concern complete selected rows with headings, panels, units, notes, and display cells. Do not require an F field per CSV cell.

Independently review every new/changed card under Appendix C. An eligible selected tile yielding no cards is `INVENTORY_DEFECT`, not automatic credit: correct the common frame and rerank using the same secret. No content and no numerical tables after correction means common `INELIGIBLE`.

Map each submitted source/table inventory to the common inventory, first using source byte/version identity, then exact title/locator/page agreement; independently source-review ambiguous identities. Confirmed missing/duplicated required tables, unreviewed required sources, missing CSVs, or `table_coverage.status="incomplete"` fail `G`. Inventory defects require common correction/hold. Scientific fidelity of delivered table contents remains sampled through `T`.

## 3. Validation rules

### 3.1 Exhaustive and sampled obligations

`D` means exhaustive deterministic checks on every object/file, `I` independent common source inventory verification, and `S` scientific checking within selected C/T/P cases. Appendix C supplies exact scientific property, classification, relationship, annotation, family, and judge rules. Every submitted property receives schema checking; scientific claims receive the declared sampled treatment. Common C tasks also check omissions nobody submitted.

Strictly parse UTF-8 JSON, rejecting duplicate keys, non-finite numbers, and trailing non-whitespace. Preserve original bytes and numeric lexemes. Validate Draft 2020-12 against all of Appendix F, including conditionals and `additionalProperties`. Use decimal/rational comparisons without binary-float round trips. Findings retain rule ID, stable failure code, and original pointer or file/parsed-record coordinate. Never repair a value, ID, relation, core, family, or CSV; computed alternatives are diagnostic only.

Parse each immutable package once per validated byte identity. Retain exact literal spans and original pointers alongside parsed values, object-ID definitions, typed-reference indexes, and parsed parent-field paths. Reject invalid bytes or duplicate keys before a permissive parser can erase them. Compile extraction and response validators once per frozen schema/runtime. Reuse these immutable indexes across checks, matching, rendering, and replay; still verify identity and required boundary contracts when loading artifacts.

| Rule | Required coverage |
|---|---|
| V01 | D: unique physical definitions, valid prefixes/namespaces; shared views retain one ID. Ordinary and cross-group R share one namespace. |
| V02 | D: typed resolution of every source, parent, group, table, named-reference, and relationship endpoint. |
| V03 | D: each ordinary R has exactly one primary family; declared main results belong to it; summary groups resolve and cross-group summaries have no primary family. S: family assertions. |
| V04 | D: allocation prefixes remain valid after storage movement; IDs do not determine current ownership. |
| V05 | D: valid parent/path, exact addressed value, acyclicity, implicit child→parent. S: scientific membership. |
| V06 | D: enums. S: label meaning; typical `field_group` vocabularies are advisory, and `other` must be intelligible. |
| V07 | D: conditional roles/notes. S: scientific roles separately from closure orientation. |
| V08 | S: correct uncertainty/test/count targets, joint versus diagnostic tests, and result-specific counting sets. |
| V09 | D: established exact internal arithmetic. S: source magnitude, signs, units, operators, contrast, approximation, and calculation origin. |
| V10 | S: source-supported question/investigation and family boundaries after local closure; shared data alone does not merge families. |
| V11 | D: source resolution/provenance presence. S: actual quote, locator, scope, origin, calculation inputs/basis. |
| V12 | D: exact reverse-reachable members including cores. |
| V13 | D: eligible types, terminal-SCC shape, required explanation. S: meaningful core and source-supported compound anchor/cycle. |
| V14 | D: omitted direction remains unreviewed; closed records have no unresolved incident edge on members or owner R. S: reviewed direction/boundary meaning. |
| V15 | D: exact nonrecursive boundary union, including `table_for` to owner R outside members. |
| V16 | D: one physical shared definition. S: scientific identity, shared views, and duplicate-evidence treatment; equal numbers do not establish identity. |
| V17 | D: disposition of every F/R/C as member, boundary, shared-context/cross-group content, or explicit `closure_review`. I/S: required content and honest unresolved dispositions. |
| V18 | D/S: storage/grouping does not create closure membership; preserve comparison anchors, links, and unresolved information. |
| V19 | S: scoped equation symbols and true dependencies through appropriate `derived_from` or explicit contextual reference; equal printed symbols do not establish identity. |
| V20 | I/D: full main/required-supplement inventory, reviewed sources, complete actual delivery. S: source fidelity. |
| V21 | I/D: one T/CSV per source table, panels/continuations combined, distinct tables distinct, exact filename. |
| V22 | D: actual safe files, encoding/dialect, exact headers, rectangular records, declared and source data-row counts. |
| V23 | S in T: all selected entries and interpretation, order, display precision, units, markers, missing values, and notes. |
| V24 | D: at least one `table_for` T→ordinary-R per T with permitted role and `none`; every T incident edge has `none`. S: actual role and source-used reported results. |
| V25 | D: cell bounds. S: correct direct components/provenance; header/caption/footnote quantities use precise snippets and named table references, never row zero; calculated outputs identify inputs rather than claim input cells as directly reported outputs. |
| V26 | D: annotation scopes/bounds and merged-span mechanics. S: actual source scope and no duplicated joint test. |
| V27 | D: tables/CSV coordinates excluded from local graph, parents, cores, and members. S: layout does not establish scientific dependence, merging, or additional evidence. |

Gate failure codes are `PACKAGE_SCHEMA` (shape/version/request identity), `OBJECT_IDENTITY` or `REFERENCE_UNRESOLVED` (identity/resolution), `FAMILY_INTEGRITY`, `PARENT_PATH`, `ENUM_OR_CONDITIONAL`, `CLOSURE_INCONSISTENT`, `OBJECT_UNACCOUNTED`, `TABLE_PACKAGE_OR_COVERAGE`, `CSV_FORMAT_OR_DIMENSIONS`, and `TABLE_REFERENCE`. Direct deterministic failures set `G=0`; judges cannot waive them. Exact incompatible assertions about the same cell/component without disclosed source discrepancy produce `INTERNAL_SCIENTIFIC_CONFLICT` for joint scientific review, not automatic semantic inference from equal numbers.

Do not put unresolved scientific judgments into the mechanical gate. Schema-valid `review_required` needs explanatory notes but is not globally rejected for that status. A selected task fails if required interpretation remains unresolved by the output; a faithful representation of genuine source ambiguity can pass.


### Extended availability checks for this round

The optional `data_code_availability` extension is checked under V28–V30. It does
not add a score dimension, an object-count reward, or an internet-fetch requirement.
Source-supported availability assertions and artifact links enter the applicable
scientific and submitted-assertion reviews. Absent optional metadata is not itself
a mechanical failure. All new IDs/references still receive exhaustive structural
checks. Unsupported assertions remain subject to P.

- **V28**: Every ArtifactLink url or identifier appears verbatim in at least one of its source_snippets, and each snippet traces to a listed source and locator. Links that the source does not print are fabricated and invalid.
- **V29**: Statuses agree with the source: a data or code statement marked available, available_on_request, restricted, or not_available quotes the statement; not_stated is used only when the reviewed sources contain none; an artifact of type dataset or code is consistent with the corresponding statement (for example, a printed GitHub link for the analysis code is not compatible with code not_applicable).
- **V30**: Artifacts are not duplicated: the same URL or identifier repeated across the article and supplements is one entry with several snippets. related_ids resolve to existing objects and never enter core_ids, member_ids, or local edges.

### 3.2 Complete property treatment

| Property family | Additional treatment beyond schema validation |
|---|---|
| Root version/arrays/properties | Exact supported version, required arrays, additional-property restrictions. |
| Paper ID/title/authors/year/journal/DOI/URL/citation/notes | Requested ID and source/version binding. Material wrong source/version is an identity/provenance defect; cosmetic bibliography receives no penalty. Do not require absent optional bibliography. |
| Extraction ID/revision/date/scope/notes/table coverage | Assigned ID, actual complete-package obligation, reviewed-source resolution, truthful scope. Scope text cannot reduce required table coverage. |
| Source ID/title/kind/DOI/URL/version/notes | Bind actual authoritative bytes through `DocumentBinding`; check material source/version claims. Free kind/notes remain free. |
| Snippet source/locator/text/note | Resolve source and verify location/quotation; keep note separate from quote. Adequate inherited record support is allowed. |
| Every FieldEntry property | ID/path/reference checks, complete literal ledger, Appendix C property and 28-label criteria. Optional properties become necessary only when required meaning has no permitted representation elsewhere. |
| Reference field/target/note | Resolve target and scientific named scope/role; never create a closing edge. |
| Every Relationship property | Typed endpoints/conditionals plus all ten predicates, roles, context, basis, note, and direction meanings; no default direction. |
| Result title/summary/context/references/notes/snippets | Matching and applicable context; audit scientific sidecars. Summaries do not replace missing fields. |
| Closure cores/members/references/status/notes | Exact graph computation/status/explanation, then meaningful core/boundary/compound interpretation. |
| Group title/question/investigation/grouping reason/main results/summary/context/references/related groups/snippets/notes | Integrity, scoped scientific assertions, and bounded grouping-witness review. Related groups do not merge families. |
| SharedContext fields/context/references/summary/snippets/notes | Unique definitions, accounting, scoped applicability and truth; no automatic paper-wide N or condition inheritance. |
| CrossGroupSummary groups/fields/summary/context/references/snippets/notes | At least two resolved groups, no primary family, truthful synthesis/scope, no duplicated independent evidence. |
| closure_review object/reason/disposition | Resolved object/nonempty explanation, honest context/unresolved disposition, selected required-information check. |
| Every TableObject property | Required files/identity/header/row count/links/bounds, full inventory, sampled caption/title/display/column/annotation fidelity. |
| Column name/header path/role/units/description | Exact names/order/enum, source hierarchy, original versus added descriptors, units and data/label/context meaning. |
| Annotation kind/text/marker/rows/columns/source locator | Bounds/conditional spans, all nine kinds and source meaning. |
| Cell reference table/row/column/component/note | Bounds, direct reported origin, component/scope; no input-cell provenance masquerading as calculated output provenance. |

### 3.3 CSV, paths, and numeric comparisons

Parse CSV as UTF-8 without BOM, comma-delimited, double-quoted with doubled-quote escaping and no type inference. Require LF separators outside quoted fields and a final LF. Preserve CR/LF inside correctly quoted cells; escaped `""` does not toggle quote state. Validate parsed records, not physical lines. Require one nonempty unique lower_snake_case header equal to `columns[].name` in exact order, rectangular records, and data-row count equal to both declaration and verified source count.

Complete one full validation pass per CSV byte identity, retaining the header map, parsed-record byte offsets, record count, and dimension findings. Quoted line breaks remain inside their record. Resolve later cell references and sampled blocks through that index, with a bounded parsed-row cache. Every row still receives format validation; every annotation and cell reference still receives its required checks. Never infer types or normalize stored cell strings.

Annotation rows are one-based; columns name existing keys. Both dimensions mean their Cartesian product; one means all of the other dimension; neither means table-wide. A merged numeric span stores content once in its first covered row and leftmost covered exported column; other covered cells are blank. Sampled verification establishes whether that merge/scientific meaning is source-supported. Every cell reference has row `1..row_count` and an existing column; retain component text. Header/caption/footnote quantities require precise snippets and named table references rather than row zero.

Parse `field_path`; never evaluate it. Require one or more steps: initial unquoted key or bracket, then `.key` or brackets. Unquoted keys are nonempty and exclude dots, brackets, and whitespace. Brackets contain a nonnegative integer without leading zeros except `0`, or a double-quoted JSON string key decoded by the JSON parser. Reject wildcards, slices, signed indices, calls, expressions, empty paths, and trailing separators. Integer steps require lists; key steps require objects with that exact key. Missing keys, invalid containers, and out-of-range indices fail `PARENT_PATH`. A parent must be an existing nonself F, parent graphs are acyclic, and parented paths are mandatory, relative to parent `value`, and yield the exact child value under JSON numeric equality. Unparented paths receive syntax checking only and create no edge.

| Operation | Exact rule |
|---|---|
| Evidence hashes | Hash unchanged bytes; never normalize before hashing. |
| Retrieval | NFKC, casefold, Unicode whitespace collapse; typographic-minus mapping only in a separate search key. Retain originals; retrieval equality is not acceptance. |
| Quotes | Locate exact text, then whitespace/line-wrap joins; accept documented OCR alternatives only with image corroboration. Paraphrase is not quotation. |
| Numbers | Exact decimal/rational equality, including `1` = `1.0`; percent/fraction conversion only with both scales explicit. No generic epsilon or discarded signs/operators. |
| Units | Normalized spelling or explicit SI-prefix conversion with recorded rational multiplier, matching dimensions/definitions. Never infer missing units or reverse contrasts. |
| Intervals | Preserve endpoints, reported level, inclusivity/operator, and interpretation. CI, SE, SD, range, and prediction interval are distinct. |
| Archival displays | Preserve printed precision, markers, missing-value strings, and composite cells; only confirmed transcription/OCR corrections with notes. |
| Calculations | Recompute only a declared expression with identified inputs using decimal/rational arithmetic; preserve origin and declared rounding. Never invent a formula. |

For explicitly rounded calculations displayed to `d` places, require `Decimal(calculated).quantize(Decimal(1).scaleb(-d), rounding=ROUND_HALF_UP)` to equal the declared decimal, including negative ties. Otherwise there is no automatic approximate/digitized tolerance beyond source-declared precision or documented rounding; verify preserved approximation against source. Archival CSV remains display-exact.

### 3.4 Local graph and exact closure

Include all defined eligible R/C/F nodes, including cross-group R; exclude G/S/T. Storage, family membership, source co-location, table coordinates, and named references create no edges. For relationships with eligible endpoints, `source_to_target` adds source→target, `target_to_source` adds target→source, `none` adds no closing edge, and omitted direction remains unresolved with no reviewed edge. Add each valid child F→parent F.

Compute strongly connected components. A terminal SCC has no outgoing edge to another SCC. All submitted `core_ids` must lie in one terminal SCC; distinct terminal results require a source-supported compound anchor already submitted, not one invented by the validator. Require applicable cycle/compound explanations. Scientific core eligibility remains a source check; a support-only sink is not automatically a result.

For each owner result R:

1. `members` is exactly its `core_ids` plus every reverse-reachable ancestor through projected edges.
2. Initialize `boundary` with outside endpoints of reviewed `none` relationships incident on members.
3. Add targets of named references attached to members or owner R, sources T of `table_for`→owner R even when R is not a member, and table IDs from member-field `table_references`.
4. Remove members from boundary and deduplicate. Compare both sets with submitted sets ignoring order, while rejecting schema-forbidden duplicates.
5. An unresolved relationship incident on members or owner R prevents `closed`. Preserve group context separately.

Boundary objects do not extend closure through their own parents, descendants, table cells, siblings, unrelated edges, or families. Do not traverse them recursively to compute membership.

Build the projected graph, forward/reverse adjacency, SCCs, incident relationships, owner references, and `table_for` target index once per immutable package. Cache reverse-reachable membership by validated terminal SCC within that package; cores in the same SCC have identical ancestors. Bound this cache and evict/recompute without changing results; do not construct an all-pairs reachability matrix. Compute boundaries separately for each owner because owner-level references and table links can differ despite identical membership. Keep parent acyclicity and unresolved incident-edge checks separate from SCC membership.

### 3.5 Family acceptance

Attach family review to selected content/audit cases; do not add a family quota or organization penalty. Check question, generating investigation, grouping reason, main results, and relevant co-members after local closures. Apply Appendix C's `SOURCE_SEPARATE` and `MUST_TOGETHER` witness rules. Outcomes/times/subgroups/adjustments/doses/parameters alone do not create a boundary within a proved common question/investigation; shared data/table/equation alone does not merge different investigations. Multiple schema-admissible organizations pass.

Inspect compact same-family co-member descriptors, their source anchors, explicitly related results, and source-descriptor neighbors across families. When evidence identifies a required missing connection, use §4's complete fallback even without a submitted link. Expand only witness-relevant records/evidence. Failure requires original result/group pointers, the governing rule, and a concrete source-supported incompatible pair or mandatory connection—not preferred granularity or family count. A scientific grouping error affects its task/bucket; mechanical family corruption fails `G`. Evidence insufficient to resolve a material boundary is unresolved, while mere choice between admissible alternatives passes.

## 4. Sampling, matching, and reconstruction

### 4.1 Reproducible sample identities

Generate a cryptographically random 32-byte paper secret and commit before pipeline execution. Keep it outside guests; seal all outputs/failures and the complete pool before trusted sampling. Publish it only with the frozen receipt after all compared outputs are immutable; never expose a reusable master secret during other executions.

Define `frame(parts)` by UTF-8 encoding each string, prefixing its byte length as an unsigned eight-byte big-endian integer, and concatenating. Scope is `(protocol_id, round_id, stage, paper_id)`. Rank an item using `HMAC-SHA256(secret, frame(scope..., stream, item_id))`; order by raw digest bytes, then UTF-8 ID bytes. Streams are `C_PROSE`, `C_TABLE`, `C_BLOCK`, `T_TABLE`, `T_BLOCK`, `P_BUCKET`, and `PASS_AUDIT`. Do not include pipeline ID in `P_BUCKET`. Commit as `SHA256(frame(scope..., source_packet_sha256, policy_sha256) + secret)`, using the complete source-packet hash, not the runner's main-PDF hash; represent hashes as lowercase hex. Record selected IDs, populations, conditional draw sizes, and ranks.

Case fingerprints bind task, original-output bytes, assertion-packet hash, evidence hash, and policy hash. Identical case bytes share cache eligibility and audit draw. Audit a primary PASS exactly when `int_big_endian(rank(PASS_AUDIT, fingerprint)) * 10 < 2**256`. Corrections retain the same secret and stream names and rerank the corrected complete population; never substitute easier tasks.

### 4.2 Content and archival sampling

Allocate six C draws: three prose/equation tiles and three eligible table blocks when both exist, otherwise six to the sole stratum. If a stratum is smaller than allocation, take its census and transfer unused draws until six or exhaustion. Applicability comes from the common frame. Transfers do not alter weights: `1/2` each if both strata exist, otherwise `1` for the sole stratum.

Select prose tiles by lowest `C_PROSE` rank without replacement. For table allocation `k`, the C population includes only tables with content-eligible blocks and only those blocks; T includes every qualifying numerical table and data block. Select `min(k, table_count)` tables by table rank. Take the lowest block rank per selected table; include source table ID in block IDs. Cycle selected tables in table-rank order, adding each next unselected block and skipping exhausted tables until `k` or exhaustion. When `k < table_count`, take one block per selected table; otherwise all tables participate. T independently applies this procedure with `k=4` and its own streams.

Use exact fractions. C averages cards within tile; prose tiles equally; table blocks within table, then tables equally; finally applicable strata using fixed weights. T averages block bits within table then table means. A T block covers every selected row/data column and all necessary headers/notes/merged scopes. Record table inclusion `min(1,k/table_count)` and conditional block inclusion `1/blocks_in_table` when `k<table_count`, otherwise `draws_for_table/blocks_in_table`. These diagnostics do not replace the prescribed hierarchical mean with inverse-probability weighting.

### 4.3 Output-assertion audit and discoveries

Inventory every scientific assertion, including summaries, descriptions, contexts, notes, and scientific table metadata/links. Administrative metadata, filenames, and nonassertive review notes add no assertions. Raw archival cells belong to T; parent/member views or repeated rendering of one physical assertion add no audit units.

Assign ownership using strongest supplied provenance: resolved direct field-cell reference, then field snippet, then applicable record/shared-context snippet. Resolve cells to independent source tables/blocks, not submitted row groupings. For genuinely repeated valid source anchors use the lexically first source tile ID; retain others as evidence. Group assertions use the earliest supporting member anchor with full scope. Source co-location allows retrieval, not acceptance; preserve locator/quote mismatch flags. Adequate inherited evidence can support a field without its own snippet.

Select the lowest-ranked `min(4, occupied_bucket_count)` distinct occupied `SOURCE_TILE` buckets by common `P_BUCKET` rank restricted to each pipeline's occupancy. Take a census when fewer than four exist. Identical occupied sets get identical samples regardless of pipeline ID, organization, object count, or order. Check every owned scientific assertion and relevant relations/context/family witnesses; every coherent shard must pass for a bucket to pass. Keep the sample size fixed during a scored run; observed failures, apparent ties, or resource pressure do not authorize extra or fewer draws.

Put unresolved supplied provenance in one mandatory additional `UNANCHORED` bucket. Exhaustively search and source-verify it. Established support plus compliant original provenance permits reassignment and occupancy recomputation before sealing. Demonstrably unsupported/untraceable assertions on readable source, or demonstrably false supplied quotes/locators, force `P=0`. Unavailable evidence or failed lookup causes common hold. Finding a claim elsewhere does not repair false submitted provenance; retain the original error. Search limits alone never establish falsehood. Empty assertion sets have `P=1`; required-content omissions and gates still apply. Otherwise P is the sampled-bucket mean unless the unanchored failure forces zero.

Any pipeline can identify required information omitted from a selected task. Independently confirm against source with producer identity hidden, correct the common task, and reassess every original output. Supported discoveries receive credit through corrected common requirements and output audit, not a pipeline-specific bonus. Outside-sample discoveries are future nominations unless they prove omitted eligible frame regions/tables; then correct the frame and regenerate affected samples using the same secret for all pipelines. Allow at most two common correction revisions; unresolved defects thereafter hold the paper.

### 4.4 Matching and certified absence

Index every ordinary result, F, C, cross-group summary, table, reference, relation, computed closure, and original pointer. Scientific descriptors retain source anchors, original value/operator/unit tokens, label/measure, outcome, counted set/population, intervention/comparator, time, model/adjustment, scoped equation symbols, context, and local linked IDs. Missing values stay missing. Match source identity and scientific referent; wrong values/labels can still be the right result. Do not require equal family IDs, values, counts, or organizations. Match tables by source identity and rows by source order, panel, label, occurrence, and surrounding headings.

Candidate order is: exact table/cell/row/quote-span hits; their necessary explicit parent/reference/incident-relation/computed-closure neighbors; then scientific descriptor overlap. Retain every exact hit and the top 12 additional descriptor candidates; split oversized exact-hit sets into ordered batches without dropping hits. Tokenize retrieval-normalized text into maximal Unicode letter/number strings, removing no domain words. With `N` index descriptors and token document frequency `df`, weight tokens by `1 + log((N+1)/(df+1))`; score distinct query-token weights present in each descriptor. Break ties by original-pointer UTF-8 order; exact source hits precede lexical ranks. Numeric tokens aid retrieval but never exclude candidates. Embedding thresholds/confidence scores do not determine acceptance.

Build direct source-location indexes and a token-to-descriptor inverted index once per immutable descriptor corpus. Compute `N`, `df`, and weights over that complete corpus. Sum distinct present query-token weights in UTF-8 token order using the frozen numeric implementation. Indexed and full-scan retrieval must yield identical scores and ordering, including zero-score candidates when needed to fill the top 12. Index absence never certifies scientific absence.

Use deterministic correspondence when verified source/table bindings and indexed coordinates establish the complete mapping without an unresolved identity or scope choice; retain all required neighbors and contradictory representations. A fully resolved archival table/block-to-CSV mapping can use this path. A quote hit, numeric equality, or lexical rank alone cannot. Otherwise use Appendix C's matching prompt and required response. Neither path establishes scientific correctness; every selected case still receives its required verification. Preserve many-to-many matches and all qualifying alternatives. One card may span records; one result may serve several cards when genuinely reused. Do not choose a correct duplicate and hide a conflicting one.

For any inconclusive result or qualifier, scan all remaining descriptors in original-pointer order in batches of 32, recording complete ID manifests, examined relationships, and outcomes. Fetch plausible full objects. Include source hits/needed graph neighbors even with zero lexical score; inspect shared/related context, references, parent members, and alternative representations. `ABSENT` requires complete applicable-index coverage and a valid response certifying no representation of that referent/requirement. Failed requests, truncation, deadline, or incomplete scans yield `LOOKUP_UNRESOLVED` and common evaluator hold, never omission. `AMBIGUOUS` retains candidates for joint review; admissible alternatives pass, while unresolved material scientific differences follow the bounded evidence procedure.

Return task/output alias, status, candidate original pointers, requirement mapping, source hits, scan coverage, unresolved questions, and expansion requests. Matching emits neither a score nor repaired extraction and performs no canonical graph/family alignment.

### 4.5 Lossless deterministic assertion packets

Rendering is a pure function of immutable JSON/CSV bytes, source alignment, graph calculations, task scope, and renderer version. Use stable pointer order and literal templates, never model-written candidate descriptions. Every ledger item has an `OutputRef` resolving to original bytes. Encode each property as its exact original JSON literal in `PropertyAssertion.value_json`, preserving numeric lexemes and all array/object components. Explicit null is the string `"null"`; absence is `present=false`, `value_json=null`, `original_ref=null`. The extraction property remains `value`; the envelope does not change its meaning.

Reuse byte-identical property, relationship, cell, and scoped-context fragments under their complete input hashes and renderer identity. Assemble packets in the prescribed order; cached and uncached rendering must produce identical bytes, references, completeness flags, and shard boundaries. Shared definitions must still appear wherever a request requires them. Fragment reuse never removes contradictory content, replaces evidence with an inaccessible cache reference, or authorizes judgment reuse under a different binding.

Emit these seven sections in order:

1. **Identity and declared scope:** original record ID/type/title, family ID, and submitted closure. Titles aid identity/matching, not missing numerical content.
2. **Scientific fields:** every selected field's `field`, `field_group`, `label`, `value`, `units`, `value_operator`, `measure`, `confidence_level`, `description`, `context`, `origin`, `value_status`, `notes`, `parent_field_id`, and `field_path`, preserving presence and literal values.
3. **Context with explicit scope:** applicable record/group context and notes and each field's physical-owner context, labeled by owner and original pointer. Preserve conflicting assertions side by side; compatible local narrowing is allowed. Context does not create an edge.
4. **Recorded associations:** named references with field/target/note; relations with endpoints/predicate/role/context/basis/note/direction; implicit child→parent edges. Distinguish association types and mark computed-closing participation. Show submitted and computed closures separately. Never infer edges from storage/order/title/group/cell/source quotation or translate scientific direction into closure direction.
5. **Resolved boundary objects:** original directly referenced definitions and allowed context, labeled as boundary. Do not import their ancestors, descendants, siblings, or table cells into closure. Needed contextual reference chains retain their explicit path/provenance without becoming closing edges.
6. **Table provenance:** field table references, parsed cells/components, applicable row/column annotations, header paths, caption, and locator; show `table_for` separately. Available cells do not instantiate missing fields.
7. **Boundary evidence:** neighboring result IDs/actual memberships, relevant group question/investigation/grouping reason, declared main results/co-member descriptors, and submitted boundary explanations for the specific grouping check.

Keep summaries and submitted snippets in separate sidecars; keep independently fetched authoritative evidence in a different payload field. Summaries cannot satisfy a field, classification, relation, or closure requirement. Submitted snippets are claimed provenance: use their locators to fetch evidence and check quotation fidelity, including permitted inherited support, but do not populate missing structured assertions from their text. Source text never supplies missing candidate values, classifications, relations, conditions, qualifiers, or provenance declarations. Permitted field descriptions/context/notes remain available to express schema-allowed meaning. Check scientific sidecars in P and for contradictions within selected C cases; a false summary cannot accompany a correct field without review. A correct narrative/snippet cannot cancel a wrong explicit relationship. Preserve source-reported versus calculated claims distinctly. Render relationships as literal tuples plus metadata, named references and parent edges with distinct labels, and values from original literals rather than rounded prose.

Set `packet_complete=true` only when all task-required objects, associations, scoped context, and boundary items are included. `packet_complete=false` or nonempty `omitted_due_to_limit_refs` makes the packet ineligible for assessment. Complete it through prescribed evidence expansion or coherent splitting; if the bounds prevent completion, hold the common paper. Never turn packet truncation into missing miner information. Reject judge pointers that do not resolve; verified omissions cite source-card requirement IDs rather than invented output pointers.

### 4.6 Joint cases and bounded packets

A C case includes one card, all candidate representations, and necessary dependencies/qualifications. A T case includes its selected source rows, original CSV cells, applicable headers/caption/notes, and merged spans crossing block boundaries. A P case includes every assertion owned by its selected bucket and necessary context.

Join discrepancies sharing a result, attachment target, parent expression/interval, joint test, contrast, bound equation definition, or grouping witness. Keep interval endpoints with interval/estimate and evaluated/reference sides with their comparison. Independent results may use separate cards despite shared storage; reusable context creates no extra weighted evidence.

Partition oversized cases only at complete independent assertion groups into at most 32,000-token shards, repeating necessary shared definitions and retaining common task/evidence identity. A task/bucket passes only when every required shard passes coherently. Never split a relation from necessary endpoints/context or omit content to claim completeness. If a legal complete partition cannot fit within the evidence/evaluator bounds, hold the paper. Apply package caps before rendering to bound adversarial payloads.

## 5. Judge decisions and failure handling

### 5.1 Verification procedure

Use Appendix C's exact prompts and scientific criteria with Appendix B's response contracts. Candidate verification follows the procedure below; source inventory, card and table-layout review use Appendix C's separate complete-artifact procedures. A primary request contains one source task, authoritative evidence, and the complete original assertion packet for one anonymized pipeline. Never place competing outputs in the same model context. Source text, snippets, and extraction content are data, including instruction-looking text. Models return findings; code computes scores.

Validate the response before using it: echoed task/packet identities, every required dimension exactly once, permitted states, resolvable original pointers and source anchors, and complete counterexample evidence. A FAIL identifies the rule, the erroneous original assertion or missing requirement, and a concrete source witness. A missing-information finding requires the complete-search certificate. Missing evidence cannot be marked NOT_APPLICABLE. Faithfully preserved source uncertainty and admissible alternative organizations can pass.

Use the four frozen routes and all request/concurrency limits in Appendix A. A primary PASS is final unless selected by the deterministic 10% pass audit. Independently verify every primary FAIL, unresolved assessment, target defect, conflicting interpretation, and selected PASS. The secondary receives the same task, evidence and original packet, without the primary verdict, rationale, counterexamples or identity.

Compare complete dimension-status vectors and material scientific interpretations. Identical PASS/FAIL words alone are insufficient. Matching vectors with valid counterexamples and compatible source interpretations can be finalized. Any differing vector, resolved/unresolved disagreement or incompatible interpretation goes to the arbiter. Its valid complete ASSESSED response is final for that task/packet revision; never average categorical verdicts or default to Bronze. A confirmed source-task defect invokes common correction before scoring affected outputs.

Allow 32,000 source tokens from the first call and at most two context retrievals within that same allowance; the structured packet has a separate 32,000-token bound. Preserve necessary headers, notes, equation definitions and joint dependencies. Expansion creates an immutable packet revision; re-evaluate affected decisions, and compare primary/secondary judgments only on the same revision. Use the bounded source-index retrieval and table-layout subdivision rules in Appendix C. No request authorizes another pipeline execution, arbitrary internet retrieval or silently truncated evidence.

A provider error, timeout, invalid JSON, unknown pointer or malformed response receives one technical retry using the same immutable task/packet, with the validation error outside source data. A second failure is an evaluator hold. Scientific disagreement uses independent roles, not repeated attempts to obtain a PASS. Explicit table-layout output-length truncation follows the prescribed scope subdivision rule instead of accepting a partial layout. Model confidence never supplies an acceptance threshold.

Complete every required case, shard and independent-review route for gate-passing pipelines. A failed shard or P=0 does not authorize stopping remaining checks: they can establish a common source defect or hold. The mechanical G=0 skip in §5.2 remains the prescribed scientific-work exclusion.

### 5.2 Outcome routing

The following table governs ownership and action. Exact error codes, stage names and response schemas are in Appendix B. Only trusted controller evidence can classify a source/infrastructure fault; a miner cannot assign that status to its own failure.

| Condition | Required typed representation | Action |
|---|---|---|
| Configuration, schema/prompt/runtime identity or required route capability is invalid | `ErrorRecord`: category `EVALUATOR`, stage `CONFIGURATION`, code `CONFIG_INVALID`. | Stop execution/publication; do not emit a frozen Silver generation, substitute a model or loosen a contract. |
| Pipeline build/execution/resource failure, invalid package/schema/graph/file, or confirmed required-table omission | `ErrorRecord.category=PIPELINE`; failed delivery uses `SubmittedPackage.PACKAGE_FAILURE`. Delivered invalid data retains `SubmittedPackage.COMPLETED` and has `GateReceipt.PIPELINE_FAILURE`, `gate_bit=0`. Eligible `PaperScoreVector.state=PIPELINE_FAILURE`, Q=`0/1`. | Controller disposition `PIPELINE_INVALID`; retain findings and skip expensive scientific verification for that pipeline. No extraction retry. |
| Valid assessed response, all applicable dimensions pass | `JudgeResponse.outcome=ASSESSED`; final `ScientificVerdict.state=PASS`. | PASS, subject to independent audit. An optional absent property is not an error unless schema/source meaning requires it. |
| Wrong value, classification, attachment, scope, required information, provenance or demonstrated grouping boundary | `JudgeResponse.outcome=ASSESSED` with failing dimensions; final `ScientificVerdict.state=FAIL`. | Task bit 0 after required independent checking. Continue other cases; do not repair output or add per-field penalties. |
| NEED_EVIDENCE with an available expansion | `JudgeResponse.outcome=NEED_EVIDENCE` with authorized requests; immutable packet revision. | Retrieve evidence, preserve prior evidence and re-evaluate affected judgments. |
| Materially defective source task/frame | `JudgeResponse.TARGET_DEFECT` or `SourceTaskBatch.INVENTORY_DEFECT`; controller correction record. | Confirm against source and create a common correction. Reassess all affected outputs; assign no failure bit against the defective target. |
| Required controller source unavailable/unreadable, unresolved source interpretation, or mandatory source-layout context exceeding its prescribed chunk limit | `ErrorRecord.category=SOURCE`; `SourcePacket.HOLD` for absent required material, or `SourceFrame.SOURCE_UNRESOLVED` for interpretation/inventory. `SampledSilverReceipt.HOLD`; affected scores `HOLD`, Q=null. | `HOLD_SOURCE` after the prescribed procedure. Faithful representation of an ambiguity can instead PASS. |
| Incomplete lookup, evaluator limits, execution infrastructure fault, or exhausted provider/response retry | `ErrorRecord.category=EVALUATOR` for controller limits or `MODEL` for provider/response errors. Execution infrastructure failure uses `SubmittedPackage.INFRASTRUCTURE_FAILURE`; a gate unable to run uses `GateReceipt.EVALUATOR_HOLD`. Silver/scores `HOLD`, Q=null. | `HOLD_EVALUATOR` for everyone. Search, evidence, packet or deadline exhaustion cannot prove omission or become a pipeline failure. |
| No source-eligible content and no qualifying numerical table | `SampledSilverReceipt` and `PaperScoreVector` state `INELIGIBLE`, Q=null. | No denominator or quality score; use common replacement. |
| Evidence/retry/correction work remains pending | Retained HOLD receipt; no `CompetitionPairRow` records. | Block paper/stage finalization pending the prescribed work. |
| Common HOLD is terminal, or source is verified INELIGIBLE | `CommonExclusionReceipt`; underlying HOLD/INELIGIBLE scores remain null. | Emit skipped transport rows and follow §5.3. |
| Scientific correction after publication | Immutable common revision with predecessor hash and retained correction evidence. | Regenerate affected scores, pairs, audits and publication evidence. |

Each hold records the failed step, affected evidence, completed attempts and common outcome. Resume only outstanding prescribed work with sealed pipeline bytes; successful judgments remain reusable only under the exact cache conditions in §6.4. A hold never authorizes a new extraction of the revealed paper.

Recovering a claim's true source location does not repair a false submitted quote or locator. Retain its PROVENANCE finding. An unanchored assertion is never dropped: establish compliant original provenance and reassign ownership, establish an unsupported/untraceable assertion or false provenance in that mandatory review and set P=0, or hold for evaluator uncertainty.

### 5.3 Common exclusion and replacement

After all applicable evidence, retry and correction bounds are exhausted, persist `CommonExclusionReceipt` with the terminal Silver HOLD and null Q values. Verified INELIGIBLE sources require no pointless retries. Emit one `CompetitionPairRow` per challenger with `infrastructure_fault=true`, both numeric score fields `0.0`, and `evaluation_status=COMMON_EXCLUDED`. These zeros are compatibility placeholders, not measurements; ranking skips the row before reading them. An eligible package failure instead has measured Q=0 and `infrastructure_fault=false`, retaining its competitor's actual score.

For a new percentage-minimum policy, first complete the scheduled common grid. If it meets its frozen eligible-paper minimum, exclusions do not require additional draws. Otherwise replace an excluded selected paper for the entire compared set with the next unused eligible paper in the precommitted order. Historical policies retain their existing replacement procedure. Preflight exclusions before selection are source-selection diagnostics. Post-selection exclusions consume an allowance of `max(1, floor(N/20))` replacement papers for a stage requiring N eligible papers. `CommonExclusionReceipt.replacement_ordinal` counts replacements from 1 within the stage; the replacement entry's ordinal identifies its position, also from 1, in the full precommitted order. Exceeding the allowance, or lacking frozen budget to meet the stage minimum, blocks stage finalization and produces a failure report. Do not select easier tasks, unequal paper sets, or additional draws until a desired winner appears.

Replacement scheduling is atomic: validate the exclusion, current sample revision, committed order, complete entrant set, allowance and budget; append the replacement/exclusion history and schedule all entrants together. A stale predecessor schedules nothing; reload authoritative state without duplicating the replacement or choosing a different reserve paper. Preserve excluded rows and prior grid revisions.
