All three reconciliation prompts use `arbiter`. Its valid `READY` artifact is the final source decision for that packet/revision. A `NEED_EVIDENCE` response uses the typed evidence-request resolver. For an initial or arbiter inventory `SOURCE_UNRESOLVED`, whose artifact DTO has no request list, the controller uses the next bounded source expansion: add the immediately preceding and following source pages to the existing packet in document order, one additional adjacent-page hop per expansion, including trusted figure/table captions and images. A missing required companion is handled through the companion-manifest rule, not invented through adjacent pages.

The two-expansion allowance is shared by drafting, review, and arbitration for this source request. After expansion, regenerate the source proposal from the expanded packet and review it against that same packet; any retained arbiter input must refer to the current proposals. A schema error, invalid pointer, incomplete required checked set, or technical failure gets one retry at the same route/packet. Exhausted evidence expansion, a second technical failure, or a final unresolved arbiter result holds the common evaluation. No source-only uncertainty becomes a miner failure.

For a reconciled `INVENTORY_DEFECT`, apply the common source-frame revision and same-seed resampling procedure, at most two frame revisions. Any final source artifact that changes a previously sealed inventory, table layout, or card invalidates all affected candidate matches, packets, and verdicts. Recompute them for every pipeline under the same common revision before publishing scores.

### Candidate matching and certified absence

Match source tasks to candidate scientific content, allowing one source task to map to several original fields/records and several source statements to occur within one candidate record. Identity cues are source location, outcome, comparison, group, investigation, time, model/adjustment, parameter setting, equation scope, and quantity role. Candidate family IDs and record counts are not matching keys. Numerical equality is an aid, not a requirement: the wrong number for an otherwise identified source quantity must be matched and assessed as wrong.

Use deterministic retrieval first. An unresolved match invokes the source-grounded matching role. A top-k retrieval miss is never a certified omission. Before declaring absence, scan the full eligible output ledger or every block of its lossless index, then inspect every plausible retrieved object and named association. Code records inspected block IDs. If any required block could not be examined because of an output limit, parsing problem, or evaluator limit, absence is unproven and the common task is held.

Do not query the submitted pipeline to extract a validator-selected family. All matches refer to the already committed full-paper run. A model may search that committed output; it cannot ask the miner to produce a new answer to the selected task.

Source recovery may find the correct support location for an otherwise unanchored assertion and thereby correct its validator-owned source-bucket assignment. It must retain the original submitted quotation, source ID, and locator. A demonstrably false quotation or specific locator remains a `PROVENANCE` counterexample and follows the usual verification/secondary route. An adjudicated false-citation failure remains part of the submitted-output audit after re-anchoring or resampling; do not repair or redraw away the failure because a different source passage supports the number. A broad but valid locator or a permissible inherited record excerpt must not be treated as false merely because recovery finds a more precise anchor.

```text
ROLE: SOURCE_MATCH
INPUT: source task card; source anchors; immutable candidate ledger/index blocks;
original output references; inspected-block manifest; response schema.

Find candidate representations of the same scientific result, allowing one-to-many
and many-to-one mappings. Use scope, outcome, comparison, group, time, model, quantity
role, and source identity. Do not require the same family or local-record boundaries.
Do not require numerical agreement. Return an identified result even when its value,
classification, relation, or qualifier is wrong; the verifier will assess the error.

Inspect all plausible candidates. Distinguish a genuine omission from an incorrect
representation and from uncertainty about correspondence. A quotation or summary
without extracted content does not satisfy a scientific field requirement. A full
CSV is not automatically an independently extracted scientific result.

This is one batch of an exhaustive controller-managed search, not the whole output.
Inspect every supplied component. State ABSENT means no relevant representation
was found in THIS BATCH; it is not a scientific omission verdict or a certificate
that the entire extraction lacks the result. Return MATCHED for identified relevant
candidates, even when their scientific contents are wrong. Return AMBIGUOUS only
for a concrete plausible correspondence in this batch whose identity is ambiguous.
LOOKUP_UNRESOLVED means this supplied batch itself cannot be inspected or its
available evidence cannot support correspondence. Do not use LOOKUP_UNRESOLVED
merely because other batches have not been shown. The controller visits all batches
and alone adds the global exhaustive-search certificate before any omission credit.

Original context_headers identify relation endpoints, physical result owners and
family scope. They are partial original-property views, not additional inspected
blocks and not rewritten values. Their original_ref pointers may identify candidate
representations; complete original blocks are retained and inspected separately.
Never fabricate a missing output pointer. Return only the supplied
MatchResponse JSON contract: task_id, output_alias, state, inspected_component_ids,
matches, candidate_refs, and explanation. state is MATCHED, ABSENT, AMBIGUOUS, or LOOKUP_UNRESOLVED. Each match
contains requirement_id and output_refs. References must resolve to supplied original
objects or CSV cells. The controller adds the exhaustive-search coverage certificate;
inspected_component_ids must contain exactly the distinct component IDs supplied in
this successful scan request that you actually inspected. The controller checks exact
set equality and stores the hashed available/inspected ID-list files and counts.
Use the explicit inspection_component_ids input list as the exact set of IDs to
return after inspecting every corresponding component. These IDs are the outer
components dictionary keys (original JSON pointers), not nested object IDs,
requirement IDs, or only the matched components. Return one matches entry for every
required_requirement_ids input ID, with output_refs=[] when no correspondence is
found in this batch.
You must not author those hashes or claim that uninspected blocks were examined.
```

### Primary scientific verifier

The verifier receives one pipeline at a time under an opaque output alias. Provide the shared task card, authoritative source packet, deterministic candidate ledger, original output refs, candidate match or certified-absence record, applicable rule text, and strict `JudgeResponse` JSON Schema. Do not provide the pipeline author, rank, reward, reference/challenger designation, peer output, or another verifier's response.

Use Appendix A's concurrency limits and §6.4's exact cache eligibility. Each request contains one pipeline's assertion packet; no request includes a peer pipeline's assertions.

For content and submitted-output tasks, the check dimensions are `NUMERIC_FIDELITY`, `CLASSIFICATION`, `SCIENTIFIC_ATTACHMENT`, `SCOPE_AND_ORIGIN`, `REQUIRED_INFORMATION`, `REQUIRED_ENCODING`, `GROUPING_CONFORMANCE`, and `PROVENANCE`. For archive tasks they are `TABLE_FIDELITY`, `TABLE_INTERPRETATION`, and `TABLE_LINK_CONFORMANCE`. Return every dimension for the task kind, with `NOT_APPLICABLE` only when the source task does not invoke it. `NOT_APPLICABLE` is not a way to skip an uncertain or failed check.

```text
ROLE: PRIMARY_VERIFY
INPUT: task card; source evidence packet; candidate ledger and sidecars; match or
absence record; applicable rule text; JudgeResponse JSON Schema.

First check that the source card describes the actual supplied source and task scope.
If the card is materially wrong or incomplete, return TARGET_DEFECT with source
anchors and an exact target_issue. Do not penalize the candidate for an evaluator
card defect and do not silently repair the card inside your judgment.

For each required check dimension, compare the candidate's actual encoded meaning
with the authoritative source. Read values, labels, units, operators, measures,
context, scoped references, recorded scientific relations, and origin together.
Check the actual targets of uncertainty, spread, tests, counts, contrasts, derivations,
and conditions. Check compound-result/core eligibility when relevant. Preserve all
candidate contradictions; do not select a convenient context or import source prose
to fill a missing candidate assertion.

Check scientific statements in the selected summary/note/snippet sidecars for false
claims and contradictions. In OUTPUT_AUDIT tasks these assertions are part of the
audited submission. They cannot supply credit for an omitted structured scientific
entry. Permitted field and record context/notes can preserve their documented scope,
origin, uncertainty, and qualifications.

For REQUIRED_INFORMATION, assess every required-information group in the task card.
For REQUIRED_ENCODING, apply only schema-mandated encoding. A correct quote or
summary does not supply a missing scientific field. Permitted field/record/group
context can carry qualifiers without a newly invented taxonomy. Missing information
can fail only after a complete candidate search or when the controller certifies that
the mapped packet contains the complete relevant semantic component and it lacks the
required information. Search related original fields and referenced context before
declaring an apparent one-record omission; the candidate may use a valid one-to-many
representation. Use its requirement ID for an omission.

For GROUPING_CONFORMANCE, accept admissible organization. Report only a supported
SOURCE_SEPARATE or MUST_TOGETHER counterexample; cite the relevant memberships and
source question/investigation boundary. Never compare against a preferred partition.

For archive tasks, compare all selected row-block source values and their row/group
associations, headings, units, caption, applicable notes, missing-value meanings,
merged spans, and source-display conventions. Check the applicable table_for roles.
Coordinate validity is supplied by code; you decide source meaning, not row indexing.

Use outcome ASSESSED only when all applicable dimensions are decidable. Return PASS,
FAIL, or NOT_APPLICABLE per dimension. Every FAIL requires at least one counterexample
with code, schema rule IDs, source-anchor IDs, and original output_refs, or a verified
missing_requirement_id. A numeric mismatch must retain both the source-supported
meaning and the candidate's actual assertion in the description.

If the packet lacks necessary evidence, return NEED_EVIDENCE with the smallest
permitted evidence request. If the task card is defective, return TARGET_DEFECT.
If sufficient available evidence still cannot determine the scientific question,
return SOURCE_UNRESOLVED with the specific unresolved_reason. An admissible ambiguous
source classification preserved in the candidate is not SOURCE_UNRESOLVED: assess it
as correct. If you cannot execute the task, return TECHNICAL_FAILURE. Never use FAIL
for a timeout, truncation, unreadable source, missing evaluator context, or model error.

For ASSESSED, checks must contain every dimension named in the required_dimensions
input exactly once, including NOT_APPLICABLE checks. Do not add other dimensions.
CONTENT and OUTPUT_AUDIT use the eight scientific dimensions; ARCHIVE uses only
TABLE_FIDELITY, TABLE_INTERPRETATION, and TABLE_LINK_CONFORMANCE. OUTPUT_AUDIT
REQUIRED_INFORMATION must be NOT_APPLICABLE: auditing submitted assertions does
not measure omissions.

For PASS and NOT_APPLICABLE, counterexamples must be []. A PASS cites inspected
source evidence in evidence_anchor_ids; do not put supporting examples into the
counterexamples array. Only FAIL carries a nonempty counterexamples array.
For ASSESSED use evidence_requests=[], target_issue=null, unresolved_reason=null,
and technical_error=null. Other outcomes have checks=[] and only the matching
outcome-specific request/issue/reason/error field populated.

Return only the JudgeResponse object. Do not emit an aggregate score, a rewritten
extraction, a new organization, a preferred pipeline, or model confidence.
```

#### Judge response requirements

The controller validates these fields against the strict contract before accepting a response:

```json
{
  "task_id": "task-id-from-request",
  "packet_id": "packet-id-from-request",
  "outcome": "ASSESSED",
  "checks": [
    {
      "dimension": "SCIENTIFIC_ATTACHMENT",
      "status": "FAIL",
      "evidence_anchor_ids": ["source-anchor-from-packet"],
      "counterexamples": [
        {
          "code": "TEST_TARGET",
          "rule_ids": ["V08"],
          "description": "The source identifies this p-value as a heterogeneity test; the submitted relation targets the pooled treatment-effect estimate.",
          "evidence_anchor_ids": ["source-anchor-from-packet"],
          "output_refs": [
            {
              "kind": "json",
              "output_alias": "opaque-output-alias",
              "pointer": "/relationships/7"
            }
          ],
          "missing_requirement_id": null
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "evidence_requests": [],
  "target_issue": null,
  "unresolved_reason": null,
  "technical_error": null
}
```

This is a response-shape example, not a complete task response: actual `checks` must include every dimension required for its task kind. For a non-`ASSESSED` outcome, `checks` is empty and only the corresponding request/issue/reason/error field is populated. Code derives the task verdict: any applicable failed dimension means `FAIL`; otherwise every applicable dimension must be `PASS`. Failures from unsupported rule IDs, nonexistent anchors, unresolved pointers, or an incomplete omission search are invalid evaluator responses, not candidate failures.

### Evidence expansion, independent checking, and arbitration

Use the approved routes, fixed provider parameters, capacity checks, and bounds in Appendix A. The operation-to-route and response mapping is:

| Prompt operation | Frozen model route | Strict response |
|---|---|---|
| `SOURCE_INVENTORY` | `source_reader` | `InventoryResponse` |
| `SOURCE_INVENTORY_REVIEW` | `secondary` | `SourceReviewResponse` |
| `SOURCE_INVENTORY_RECONCILE` | `arbiter` | `InventoryResponse` |
| `SOURCE_TABLE_LAYOUT` | `source_reader` | `SourceTableLayoutResponse` |
| `SOURCE_TABLE_LAYOUT_REVIEW` | `secondary` | `SourceReviewResponse` |
| `SOURCE_TABLE_LAYOUT_RECONCILE` | `arbiter` | `SourceTableLayoutResponse` |
| `SOURCE_CARD` | `source_reader` | `SourceTaskBatch` |
| `SOURCE_CARD_REVIEW` | `secondary` | `SourceReviewResponse` |
| `SOURCE_CARD_RECONCILE` | `arbiter` | `SourceTaskBatch` |
| `SOURCE_MATCH` | `source_reader` | `MatchResponse` |
| `PRIMARY_VERIFY` | `primary` | `JudgeResponse` |
| `INDEPENDENT_VERIFY` | `secondary` | `JudgeResponse` |
| `ARBITRATE` | `arbiter` | `JudgeResponse` |

Allow 32,000 source tokens from the first call. A task has at most two context retrievals within that same allowance, retaining the original evidence and adding requested source context. The complete structured candidate packet has a separate maximum of 32,000 tokens. Neither source nor candidate content may be silently truncated. If a required source region or candidate component cannot fit the applicable limit, hold the common task. Resolve requests by the prescribed deterministic source indexes. Include necessary table headings/notes and equation scope before unrelated surrounding prose. If a requested source or required output ledger cannot be retrieved, return a common hold rather than silently dropping evidence.

Source-table layout generation, review, and reconciliation use the stricter chunk input bound above. After retrieving additional layout evidence, subdivide the owned scope as needed to keep each such call within 8,000 source tokens while preserving mandatory context. Do not expand a layout call to an unbounded whole-table request. If the mandatory context alone cannot fit, use `HOLD_SOURCE`; do not omit headers/notes to make the call fit. The two authorized evidence-expansion limit still applies to evidence retrieval for that layout request.

An `EvidenceRequest` has `request_id`, `kind`, `anchor_ids`, `document_uid` (or null), and `query`. Allowed kinds are `ADJACENT_TEXT`, `DEFINITION`, `TABLE_HEADERS`, `TABLE_NOTES`, `METHOD_CONTEXT`, `SUPPLEMENT`, and `INVESTIGATION_SCOPE`. Requests select trusted evidence within the frozen supplied-source scope. They do not authorize internet browsing, new sources, or another execution of a submitted pipeline.

Evidence expansion creates a new immutable packet ID. Re-run every affected decision on that packet. Primary and secondary comparisons must use the same packet revision. Reuse a completed valid model response only when the complete role, exact model identity, prompt, schema, source evidence, task, candidate packet, configuration, and request bytes match the cache key. Shared source artifacts can be reused under that exact binding. Semantically equivalent content, renamed IDs, matching numbers, and agreement with Bronze do not authorize verdict reuse. Acceptance may recognize equivalent representations while each distinct complete request receives its own assessment. Do not cache a technical failure as the result of its retry.

A technical error, invalid JSON, unknown pointer, or malformed response gets one retry with the same immutable task and packet and the validation error supplied outside source data. A second technical failure is an evaluator hold. Do not retry until a passing answer appears. Scientific disagreement is handled by the independent roles below, not by repeated calls to the same role.

Accept a primary `PASS` unless selected in the deterministic 10% pass audit. Send every primary scientific `FAIL` and unresolved scientific assessment, and every audited pass, to the independent secondary role. The secondary receives the same source task and packet plus the same original candidate ledger, without the primary's verdict, rationale, counterexamples, or identity. An evidence request is serviced before comparing assessments. A source-card defect invokes shared card repair before scoring any affected output.

#### Independent secondary prompt

Use the shared system prompt and the complete `PRIMARY_VERIFY` instruction body, changing the role line to `ROLE: INDEPENDENT_VERIFY` and prepending this literal instruction:

```text
Make a complete independent assessment of this task from source evidence and original
candidate content. No earlier assessment is supplied. Apply every required dimension;
do not limit your work to suspected errors or assume that this task was selected
because a pipeline failed. Return the same JudgeResponse contract.
```

The controller compares the full dimension-status vectors, not just aggregate pass/fail. Agreement requires identical applicable dimensions and identical status for each. If both independent assessments support the same dimension vector and all counterexamples validate, finalize it and retain both responses. Any differing vector, a scientific resolved/unresolved disagreement, or incompatible source interpretation goes to the arbiter. Never average categorical verdicts.

#### Arbiter prompt

```text
ROLE: ARBITRATE
INPUT: task card; authoritative source packet; immutable candidate ledger; applicable
rule text; two anonymized independent JudgeResponse objects; response schema.

Decide the scientific task from the original source and candidate content. The two
assessments are claims to inspect, not votes or ground truth. Resolve each differing
dimension and verify the proposed counterexamples against actual source anchors and
output pointers. You may correct either or both assessments. Do not favor an assessment
because of its order, detail, certainty, or assumed role.

Apply the full PRIMARY_VERIFY procedure and all schema allowances. Accept admissible
organizations without choosing a best one. A grouping failure requires SOURCE_SEPARATE
or MUST_TOGETHER evidence. Do not repair the extraction or construct a hybrid output.

Return ASSESSED only if source evidence determines every applicable dimension.
Request an allowed remaining evidence expansion if necessary. Return TARGET_DEFECT
for a materially defective task card. Return SOURCE_UNRESOLVED when the available
source cannot resolve a material question, and TECHNICAL_FAILURE if execution fails.
Do not break a tie by defaulting to the reference pipeline or to a candidate penalty.

Return only the complete JudgeResponse object, including every required dimension
and source-grounded counterexamples for failures. Do not emit scores or confidence.
```

The arbiter's valid `ASSESSED` response is final for that task-card/packet revision. An unresolved or technical final response leaves the affected common task unscored for every pipeline until it can be resolved under the common-hold procedure. Do not substitute another task differently for each pipeline, impute a zero, or rank candidates using unequal task denominators.

## Appendix D. Executable helpers, fixtures, and tests

The helpers specify reproducible algorithm and fixture behavior; their private names and placement are optional. Required expected outcomes remain binding. Application tests use scripted, validated source/judge transcripts. Live scientific qualification is a separate activation gate in §7.

### Optional closure calculation helper

The required closure behavior is specified in §3.4. Prepare the indexes once for a package. Compute or retrieve membership for its validated terminal SCC, then compute each owner's boundary separately. These helpers calculate sets after typed-edge projection; they do not replace SCC/core, parent-cycle, unresolved-edge, or scientific checks. Cache membership only within the same immutable package and graph identity. Helper names and internal storage are optional.

```python
def prepare_closure_indexes(projected_edges, relations):
    reverse, nonclosing, table_for = {}, {}, {}
    for subordinate, coreward in projected_edges:
        reverse.setdefault(coreward, set()).add(subordinate)
    for relation in relations:
        a, b = relation['source_id'], relation['target_id']
        if relation.get('closure_direction') == 'none':
            nonclosing.setdefault(a, set()).add(b)
            nonclosing.setdefault(b, set()).add(a)
        if relation['relation'] == 'table_for':
            table_for.setdefault(b, set()).add(a)
    return reverse, nonclosing, table_for


def closure_members(core_ids, indexes):
    reverse = indexes[0]
    members = set(core_ids)
    pending = list(core_ids)
    while pending:
        node = pending.pop()
        for ancestor in reverse.get(node, ()):
            if ancestor not in members:
                members.add(ancestor)
                pending.append(ancestor)
    return frozenset(members)


def recompute_closure(result_id, members, indexes,
                      named_refs, member_table_refs):
    _, nonclosing, table_for = indexes
    boundary = set(table_for.get(result_id, ()))
    for member in members:
        boundary.update(nonclosing.get(member, ()))
        boundary.update(member_table_refs.get(member, ()))
    for owner in set(members) | {result_id}:
        boundary.update(named_refs.get(owner, ()))
    return sorted(members), sorted(boundary - set(members))
```

### Optional sampling codec helper

The byte codec, ranking, commitment, and audit selection behavior in §4.1 are mandatory. This executable helper is an implementation reference; helper names and module layout are optional. Its `source_sha256` argument receives the complete source-packet hash, not the runner's main-PDF hash.

```python
import hashlib
import hmac

def frame(*parts: str) -> bytes:
    result = bytearray()
    for part in parts:
        raw = part.encode('utf-8')
        result.extend(len(raw).to_bytes(8, 'big'))
        result.extend(raw)
    return bytes(result)

def rank(secret: bytes, scope: tuple[str, str, str, str],
         stream: str, item_id: str) -> bytes:
    assert len(secret) == 32
    return hmac.new(secret, frame(*scope, stream, item_id),
                    hashlib.sha256).digest()

def ordered(ids, secret, scope, stream):
    return sorted(ids, key=lambda x: (rank(secret, scope, stream, x),
                                      x.encode('utf-8')))

def seed_commitment(secret, scope, source_sha256, policy_sha256):
    return hashlib.sha256(frame(*scope, source_sha256, policy_sha256)
                          + secret).hexdigest()

def selected_for_pass_audit(secret, scope, case_fingerprint):
    # Exact 10% threshold; no rounding of an integer percentage.
    return int.from_bytes(rank(secret, scope, 'PASS_AUDIT',
                               case_fingerprint), 'big') * 10 < 2**256
```

### Complete worked fixture and expected behavior

#### Source and required tasks

Use the complete synthetic P005 example embedded in Appendix F, including its actual CSV text. It has one source table, two data rows, two local comparison results and one family. The source is explicitly synthetic. It states:

| Source outcome | Group A, mean ± SE | Group B, mean ± SE | P value |
|---|---|---|---|
| Marker A (mg/L) | `12.0 ± 1.2` | `10.0 ± 1.1` | `0.04*` |
| Marker B (ng/mL) | `3.50 ± 0.20` | `4.10 ± 0.30` | `<0.001*` |

The table note establishes SE. The asterisk means P<0.05 for the A/B comparison. This is one descriptive investigation; it makes no causal claim. There are no supplements. Source-owned scientific content lies in one table tile, so C and T both take a census of its single block and P takes its occupied source bucket. Caption/header/note text is context, not another separately weighted prose result.

Generate two content cards: A comparison and B comparison. Each requires its group-specific means and SEs, units, comparison identity and reported P operator/value. A card can be satisfied through any schema-admissible representation preserving those facts. Generate one archive card for both rows, all four source columns, complete header hierarchy and all notes. Do not count ten F fields as ten quality units.

#### Original outputs and scores

| Fixture | Original-package change | Expected gate/verdict/Q |
|---|---|---|
| `perfect` | Complete schema P005 plus exact supplied CSV. | G=1; A/B cards pass; archive passes; P passes; Q=1. |
| `shared_storage` | Move the four SE F definitions to one C record, retaining IDs, correct field scope, all original relations and computed closures. No second definitions. | G=1; every task passes; Q=1. Physical storage does not establish scientific dependence. |
| `reference_omits_b` | Keep full CSV but omit the B scientific result, its fields/relations and its family-main-result reference. | G=1; A=1, B=0; C=1/2,T=P=1; Q=11/18. CSV presence does not supply omitted scientific F content. |
| `wrong_value` | Marker B group-A structured mean is `35` while CSV/source is `3.50`. | G=1; B content fails numerical fidelity, occupied P bucket fails; T=1; Q=0 for this one-bucket fixture. |
| `wrong_label` | Marker A group-A SE field is labeled SD. | G=1; classification fails A/P; source establishes SE; Q=0. No automatic SD-edge rule is needed to detect it. |
| `wrong_operator` | Marker B structured P operator changed from `<` to `=`. | G=1; B/P fail; Q=0. Matching still finds B. |
| `wrong_csv_precision` | CSV changes printed `12.0 ± 1.2` to `12 ± 1.2`; structured fields remain faithful. | G=1; C=P=1; archive fails literal precision; T=0; Q=7/9. |
| `false_summary` | Add a claim that the descriptive comparison proves a causal intervention effect. | G=1; original scientific sidecar fails audit; Q=0 in the one-bucket fixture. |
| `missing_csv` | Declare the table but omit its delivered file. | G=0; `TABLE_PACKAGE_OR_COVERAGE`; Q=0; no scientific calls required. |
| `stale_closure` | Delete one expected SE member from submitted member_ids. | G=0; `CLOSURE_INCONSISTENT`; Q=0. |
| `unreviewed_link` | Remove one closing relationship's direction while closure remains closed. | G=0; `CLOSURE_INCONSISTENT`; Q=0. |
| `bad_annotation_range` | Applicable annotation names CSV data row 3 although there are two rows. | G=0; `TABLE_REFERENCE`; Q=0. |

For `reference_omits_b` versus `perfect`, both are evaluated on the same source card for B. The complete pipeline earns gain `1−11/18=7/18`. No query asks it to return a reference-miner-named family. No arbitrary organization difference is scored. `shared_storage` and `perfect` tie at full quality without dividing credit between them.

The small fixture has one occupied source bucket, so P takes its census and a wrong asserted result makes P zero. The six-content-tile, four-audit-bucket and two-bucket-census arithmetic examples in §6.2 test their respective means separately.

#### Executable fixture generator

The following standalone Python helper extracts the authoritative schema from this guide and generates complete local packages, a synthetic source, and exact expected outcomes. Expected scientific outcomes are a **test oracle**, not a claim that live model judgments were executed. Mechanical tests run without any provider. Scientific route tests inject validated scripted responses; the activation suite separately exercises approved live routes.

```python
# artifact: tests/source_tasks/generate_fixtures.py
from __future__ import annotations
import copy
import hashlib
import json
import pathlib
import sys

SCHEMA_SHA = '78f5b279dfbf4f2fec5fc83b2219350e43e3fece5c58e94f81fb5d146d10a5e2'

def extract_schema(guide_bytes: bytes) -> bytes:
    marker = b'<!-- BEGIN CLAIMS_EXTRACTION_SCHEMA -->\n```json\n'
    start = guide_bytes.index(marker) + len(marker)
    stop = guide_bytes.index(b'\n```\n<!-- END CLAIMS_EXTRACTION_SCHEMA -->', start)
    body = guide_bytes[start:stop]
    assert hashlib.sha256(body).hexdigest() == SCHEMA_SHA
    return body

def write_json(path, value):
    path.write_text(json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2) + '\n',
                    encoding='utf-8')

def build(guide_path, out_path):
    out = pathlib.Path(out_path)
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    raw_schema = extract_schema(pathlib.Path(guide_path).read_bytes())
    (out / 'claims_extraction.schema.json').write_bytes(raw_schema)
    schema = json.loads(raw_schema)
    original = next(x for x in schema['examples'] if x['paper']['id'] == 'P005')
    csv = next(x['content'] for x in schema['x-table_extraction']['example_csv_files']
               if x['extraction_prefix'] == 'P005.E001')
    source = '\n'.join([
        'Synthetic two-row marker table',
        'One synthetic two-group descriptive investigation; no causal interpretation.',
        'Table 1. Marker concentrations in groups A and B.',
        'Outcome | Concentration: Group A: Mean ± SE | Concentration: Group B: Mean ± SE | P value',
        'Marker A (mg/L) | 12.0 ± 1.2 | 10.0 ± 1.1 | 0.04*',
        'Marker B (ng/mL) | 3.50 ± 0.20 | 4.10 ± 0.30 | <0.001*',
        'Concentrations are reported as mean ± standard error.',
        '* P < 0.05 for the comparison of groups A and B.',
        'Marker A concentrations are in mg/L.',
        'Marker B concentrations are in ng/mL.',
        'No supplementary sources or other numerical tables.',
        ''
    ])
    (out / 'source.txt').write_text(source, encoding='utf-8')
    cases = []

    def case(name, doc, csv_text=csv, expected=None):
        folder = out / name
        folder.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
        write_json(folder / 'output.json', doc)
        if csv_text is not None:
            (folder / 'P005.E001.T001.csv').write_text(csv_text, encoding='utf-8', newline='')
        cases.append({'case_id': name, 'expected': expected})

    case('perfect', original, expected={'G': 1, 'C': '1/1', 'T': '1/1', 'P': '1/1', 'Q': '1/1'})
    doc = copy.deepcopy(original)
    shared_fields = []
    for result in doc['results']:
        kept = []
        for field in result['fields']:
            if field['label'] == 'standard_error':
                field['context'] = copy.deepcopy(result['context'])
                shared_fields.append(field)
            else:
                kept.append(field)
        result['fields'] = kept
    doc['shared_context'] = [{
        'id': 'P005.E001.C001', 'title': 'Addressable source comparison uncertainties',
        'fields': shared_fields,
        'source_snippets': [copy.deepcopy(r['source_snippets'][0]) for r in doc['results']]
    }]
    case('shared_storage', doc, expected={'G': 1, 'Q': '1/1'})
    doc = copy.deepcopy(original)
    removed = doc['results'][1]['id']
    doc['results'] = doc['results'][:1]
    doc['relationships'] = [r for r in doc['relationships']
                            if not r['source_id'].startswith(removed)
                            and not r['target_id'].startswith(removed)]
    doc['result_groups'][0]['core_result_ids'] = [doc['results'][0]['id']]
    case('reference_omits_b', doc, expected={'G': 1, 'C': '1/2', 'T': '1/1', 'P': '1/1', 'Q': '11/18'})
    doc = copy.deepcopy(original)
    doc['results'][1]['fields'][0]['value'] = 35
    case('wrong_value', doc, expected={'G': 1, 'fail': 'NUMERIC_FIDELITY', 'Q': '0/1'})
    doc = copy.deepcopy(original)
    doc['results'][0]['fields'][1]['label'] = 'standard_deviation'
    case('wrong_label', doc, expected={'G': 1, 'fail': 'CLASSIFICATION', 'Q': '0/1'})
    doc = copy.deepcopy(original)
    doc['results'][1]['fields'][4]['value_operator'] = '='
    case('wrong_operator', doc, expected={'G': 1, 'fail': 'NUMERIC_FIDELITY', 'Q': '0/1'})
    case('wrong_csv_precision', original, csv.replace('12.0 ± 1.2', '12 ± 1.2'),
         expected={'G': 1, 'C': '1/1', 'T': '0/1', 'P': '1/1', 'Q': '7/9'})
    doc = copy.deepcopy(original)
    doc['results'][0]['summary'] = 'This comparison proves that an intervention causally reduced Marker A.'
    case('false_summary', doc, expected={'G': 1, 'fail': 'SCOPE_AND_ORIGIN', 'Q': '0/1'})
    case('missing_csv', original, None, expected={'G': 0, 'code': 'TABLE_PACKAGE_OR_COVERAGE', 'Q': '0/1'})
    doc = copy.deepcopy(original)
    doc['results'][0]['closure']['member_ids'].remove('P005.E001.R001.F002')
    case('stale_closure', doc, expected={'G': 0, 'code': 'CLOSURE_INCONSISTENT', 'Q': '0/1'})
    doc = copy.deepcopy(original)
    del doc['relationships'][0]['closure_direction']
    case('unreviewed_link', doc, expected={'G': 0, 'code': 'CLOSURE_INCONSISTENT', 'Q': '0/1'})
    doc = copy.deepcopy(original)
    doc['tables'][0]['annotations'][1]['rows'] = [3]
    case('bad_annotation_range', doc, expected={'G': 0, 'code': 'TABLE_REFERENCE', 'Q': '0/1'})
    write_json(out / 'expected.json', cases)
    return cases

if __name__ == '__main__':
    build(sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2])
```

Each directory is an isolated pipeline output fixture. For a combined pool test, replace `.E001.` IDs and `extraction.id` consistently with assigned distinct E IDs and rename CSV files accordingly; replacement is fixture construction only, never validator repair. The original pointer location changes produced by storage variants must be reflected in expected pointer findings; scientific source ownership and Q stay unchanged.

### Required acceptance cases E01–E50

Create deterministic tests using the fixture generator in this appendix, the embedded schema's illustrative examples for isolated graph/type tests, and the mutations below. The schema's incomplete illustrative real-paper subsets are **not** successful completed-package fixtures. Store scripted model transcripts with precise source-anchor and original-pointer references. Each test asserts both output status/code and absence of forbidden side effects (no output repair, no dropped original objects, no changed competing denominator).

| ID | Test input / mutation | Required result |
|---|---|---|
| E01 | Complete P005 package + exact CSV; schema hash; strict transport instances. | D pass, full scientific oracle pass, Q=1; all refs/hashes replay. |
| E02 | Duplicate physical F ID; dangling typed ref; wrong paper/E prefix; duplicate JSON key. | Appropriate G=0 identity/reference/schema code, no scientific waiver. |
| E03 | Move SE definitions to C retaining IDs; reorder object arrays; consistently relabel allocated IDs in another fixture. | Same source matching and Q=1; no storage-membership inference. |
| E04 | Two primary groups, nonexistent core_result_id, cross-group summary treated as ordinary family result. | G=0 `FAMILY_INTEGRITY` or schema failure. |
| E05 | Parent object/array; path `[0].rho`; key `["a.b"]`; nested bracket escape; bad index; cycle; empty/missing parent path. | Valid paths resolve exact value; invalid/cyclic paths G=0 `PARENT_PATH`; no eval. |
| E06 | Every one of 28 labels on an explicitly source-defined matching object; wrong-label pairs; intelligible `other`; unusual free origin/field names. | Correct/allowed cases PASS; scientifically wrong label FAIL; no advisory-field_group veto. |
| E07 | `p<.05` vs `.05`; 25% vs .25 with/without scale; null vs 0; minus sign; CI vs credible interval; 95% level; high-precision decimal. | Exact faithful forms pass; changed operators/type/scale fail; numeric lexemes preserved. |
| E08 | Two comparison roles and reversed closure projection; wrong evaluated/reference assignment; one role plus other explicit in context. | Correct source orientation passes; incorrect orientation fails; do not universally demand two explicit edges. |
| E09 | SD/SE ambiguity `4.2±.8`; explicitly established SE; diagnostic p-value; paper N vs analysis N. | Faithful unspecified type may pass; invented type/wrong test/count target fails. |
| E10 | One p-value merged across three source rows; independent row-specific copies claiming three tests. | Correct merged scope PASS; replicated independent tests FAIL; T blanks and annotation checked. |
| E11 | Source typo preserved+note; silent correction; explicitly calculated derived value with inputs/rounding; negative half-up tie. | Preserved source/correct identified calculation passes; silent correction fails; Decimal tie behavior exact. |
| E12 | Authentic unrelated quote; false quote; adequate record-level snippet; header quantity with named T ref; calculated value citing input cell as direct. | Entailment/provenance independently checked; inherited support allowed; invalid direct-cell origin fails. |
| E13 | T in core/parent/member; table_for with source/role wrong; R absent from its F-core members; boundary table references. | Forbidden graph T G=0; correct boundary includes T without traversing cells. |
| E14 | Delete member; add storage sibling; remove closure_direction while closed; reverse directed edge. | G=0 `CLOSURE_INCONSISTENT`; no automatic repair. |
| E15 | Single terminal F; meaningful R compound; explained terminal SCC; multiple unrelated terminal cores; support-only sink. | Topology enforced; meaningful scientifically supported core PASS; support-only core FAIL; unexplained invalid structure gated. |
| E16 | review_required with notes; unexplained orphan; named external reference chain; unresolved incident R link. | Honest disclosed context retained; required unresolved result cannot pass; no recursive boundary expansion. |
| E17 | Shared same-ID sample in two closures; two identical numbers for distinct groups; parent and cell views; repeated source report. | Correct identity/overlap allowed; no numeric-only identity, extra scoring mass or forced family merge. |
| E18 | Two admissible broad/narrow organizations with preserved local comparisons and genuine source-supported boundary qualifications recorded in grouping_reason; different IDs/titles. Do not use an arbitrary outcome-only split as the positive fixture. | Equal task acceptance; no canonical partition or separate O score. |
| E19 | Whole-paper family merges independently identified investigations; split drops required comparison; arbitrary one-result families with no linking edges. | Concrete SOURCE_SEPARATE/MUST_TOGETHER witness needed; cross-family descriptor fallback finds relevant result. |
| E20 | Wrong source version; true claim falsely cited elsewhere; unavailable supplement in trusted packet; optional invented scientific summary. | Wrong provenance/summary fails appropriate case; evaluator source unavailability common HOLD; recovery never repairs false quote. |
| E21 | Reference omits B; candidate correct B; target card initially omits B qualification; source-only omission by every pipeline. | Source task finds/updates requirement for all; full candidate Q=1 vs reference 11/18; no agreement-based PASS. |
| E22 | Same printed symbol in two scopes; locally defined coefficient used by another equation; allowed explicit contextual reference. | Separate identities respected; required real dependency/reference checked; no printed-symbol automatic edge. |
| E23 | Main+supplement tables including null/baseline/diagnostic; panel/continuation pages; separate duplicated source publications. | Full inventory required; continuations one T; distinct tables distinct T; source missing common HOLD, pipeline omission G=0. |
| E24 | Empty tables on verified table-free paper; empty tables with actual table; schema-valid incomplete instructional extraction. | Table-free complete accepted with aT=0; missing/incomplete required export G=0. |
| E25 | Missing CSV; traversal/symlink; BOM; mismatched header; CR outside quote; embedded CR/LF inside proper quote; absent final LF. | The CSV validation outcomes in §3; quoted line breaks accepted; invalid delivery/format G=0. |
| E26 | 2 parsed records on 3 physical lines; wrong row_count; duplicated header key; incompatible union-panel columns with structural blanks. | Parsed counts correct; invalid dimensions gated; proper union layout accepted. |
| E27 | Rounding printed CSV precision; dropping inequality/star; swapping source data rows; unsupported extra cell. | T block FAIL; changing raw archival cells does not create scientific field credit. |
| E28 | All nine annotation kinds; scope both/one/neither axes; marker loss; merged numeric fill-down; blank vs NA vs unreported meaning. | Correct scoped interpretation PASS; bounds errors gated; source meaning/layout errors T/P FAIL. |
| E29 | Direct F cell components; all table_for roles; header/caption quantity with fabricated row0; unused cells still present in CSV. | Bounds/source role correctness enforced; no demand for F on every archived cell. |
| E30 | Hash-ranked six C/four T/four P draws; P populations of 0, 1, 2, 3, 4 and 7 occupied buckets; additional UNANCHORED bucket; empty stratum; 100-row vs 5-row table; alternate object order/count. | Exact min(4, population) P selection or census; UNANCHORED is additional; samples/conditional traces reproduce; equal table/stratum target weights; organization cannot increase weight; no outcome-dependent extra draws. |
| E31 | Unanchored genuine claim with valid broad original locator; fabricated claim; false quote recoverable elsewhere; provider lookup outage. | Reassign only compliant provenance; confirmed unsupported/false provenance P=0; evaluator outage common HOLD. |
| E32 | Relevant result at descriptor position 13 and 33; zero lexical overlap; missing qualifier in another referenced record. | Initial12/next32/full fallback finds it; ABSENT only after complete manifest scan. |
| E33 | Contradictory duplicate representations; one-to-many valid match; two source-admissible interpretations. | Do not cherry-pick correct duplicate; valid alternative/compound PASS; unresolved lookup not omission. |
| E34 | Evidence outside tile, footnote expansion, source type ambiguity, >32k indivisible case, missing image-capable route. | Bounded expansion, source uncertainty preserved when appropriate; no truncation; limits HOLD, route CONFIG_INVALID. |
| E35 | Invalid model JSON/pointer; missing dimension; unsupported FAIL without witness; timeout twice. | One identical technical retry; reject bad response; then common HOLD, never scientific zero. |
| E36 | Primary FAIL/secondary PASS; primary PASS selected in 10% audit; arbiter disagrees; source task defective. | Exact independent routing; material coherent final verdict or common correction/HOLD; producer identity never exposed. |
| E37 | G=0 skipped components; no scientific assertions; no C/no T; one failure among four P buckets; one failure in a two-bucket census; numeric cases in §6. | Null versus measured score correct; exact fractions 47/54,3/4,1/2,11/36,5/6 and empty-state rules. |
| E38 | One root defect in C and P; same output repeated twice; shared object reused by several required source tasks. | Only declared formula effect; no extra field/count penalties or divided equivalent credit. |
| E39 | Target/frame correction after scores; candidate-only correction attempt; changed seed/source/output; stale parent update. | Common immutable revision; entire affected pair grid rebuilt; stale/candidate-only mutation rejected. |
| E40 | Worker CSV transfer/cleanup; remote reference format; actual backend blob CAS; replay without provider. | Byte-identical durable round trip and deterministic receipt/score replay. |
| E41 | Code/copy audits on equal perfect outputs and extra lawful F decomposition; incompatible unit-based receipt; incompatible claim-unit parser path. | Only build/context/cap checks; diagnostic is_copy=false; incompatible parser/audit route rejected. |
| E42 | Pending HOLD; terminal common exclusion; package-failed challenger; one-paper SE; crown threshold boundary; commitment tie; incumbent failures 4/20 versus 5/20. | Pending no rows; excluded rows use skipped 0.0 placeholders; pipeline zero remains eligible; infinite SE for n<2; 4/20 no fallback, 5/20 sealed-reference promotion without extraction; unchanged source sampling scope. |
| E43 | Seed revealed before pool sealed; omitted compared pipeline; changed prompt/model/hash; credential in public report. | Freeze/publication rejected; exact protocol/runtime binding and disclosure rules enforced. |
| E44 | Source review ACCEPT, concrete REVISE, pure wording difference, table-layout ambiguity, missing companion manifest. | No needless wording adjudication; corrections source-grounded; common inventory/task review gates enforced. |
| E45 | Strict package/CSV parse with original numeric literals, null/absence, quoted line breaks; shared graph SCCs with owner-specific references; bounded-cache eviction. | Indexed and uncached checks have identical findings, literal spans, member/boundary sets and coordinates; every row/reference remains checked; owner boundaries remain distinct; no quadratic reachability store. |
| E46 | Exact and lexical retrieval with zero-score ties, candidates beyond 12/32, wrong values and contradictory duplicates; cached/uncached packet rendering. | Identical candidate scores/order and complete-search manifests; unresolved correspondence still invokes matching; identical packet bytes and shard boundaries; no scientific acceptance from a lookup. |
| E47 | Concurrent consumers of one identical request; changed role/model/prompt/schema/task/alias/binding/evidence; invalid response followed by retry. | One active provider operation per exact request; changed identity misses cache; independent roles stay distinct; one bounded retry history; failed response is not the retry result. |
| E48 | Restart after completed work or uncertain dispatch; source/task/frame correction before and after freezing; replacement-only descendant and baseline-role fallback. | Recovery retains attempts/deadline and unknown billing; only an authorized post-freeze generation starts its recorded bounded clock; exact cache eligibility controls reuse; every affected pipeline/pair is updated; only the specified lineage exception retains ancestor receipts. |
| E49 | Shared preparation requested by several pair workers; identical blobs; ready queues at concurrency caps; P=0 or an early failed shard followed by TARGET_DEFECT/HOLD. | One common preparation operation and Q per bound pipeline; historical blobs remain retrievable; immutable guest inputs; 16/4/4 limits and deadlines hold; no combined peer context; remaining required cases run; actual usage is counted once. |
| E50 | Four-bucket production sampling and separate ten-paper census/resampling diagnostic; G=0, empty assertions, unanchored zero, incomplete census, census ties and gaps >=0.02. | Fixed production draws and exact fractions; reproducible committed diagnostic selection and 2000 nested shared-rank resamples; incomplete pairs excluded only from explicitly counted diagnostics; no invented bits, live overrides or batch-precision claims. |

For E06/E28, parameterize tests from the actual embedded enums, and assert the case registry covers every enum exactly. For E07/E11, use decimal strings that cannot survive binary floating-point serialization unchanged. For E18, truth fixtures must explicitly state which source-supported grouping alternatives are admissible; the verifier must accept either without inventing a preferred partition. These are source-grounded test decisions, not choices delegated to the implementer.


For E45–E49, use retained/scripted responses to compare cold-cache, warm-cache, concurrent, interrupted and corrected runs. Compare findings, sample IDs, search coverage, packet/request bytes, review routing, exact scores and publication lineage. Operational timing, cache-hit evidence and actual usage may differ. When an implementation revision changes its required policy binding, verify that binding change separately; never normalize production cache keys to reuse an incompatible response.

The implementation may organize the test harness to fit the repository. Each listed input and expected result remains mandatory. Generate a frozen evaluation receipt from complete fixtures and scripted judge/source responses, then replay it with provider access disabled. Required boundary contracts must be validated; no particular internal Python class layout, CLI command family or test-file naming convention is required.

## Appendix E. Engineering completeness map

Each implementation question has a specified answer and required verification. This map establishes specification coverage; successful application tests, live qualification and activation evidence must be produced under §7. It does not designate the application as implemented or deployed. The only open run inputs are the supplied packages, approved runtime/credentials, backend endpoints, frozen budget/policy and signed qualification truth described in Appendix A.

## Appendix F. Authoritative extraction schema

This is the complete supplied Claims Extraction Schema 1.1, including its semantic instructions and examples. Write the block's exact bytes, including its final newline, to `claims_extraction.schema.json`. The fixture helper in Appendix D extracts the block and verifies its SHA-256. Apply both JSON Schema validation and this guide's application/source checks. The schema's incomplete instructional examples cannot pass the completed-package gate.

<!-- BEGIN CLAIMS_EXTRACTION_SCHEMA -->
```json
{
  "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema",
  "title": "Claims scientific result extraction schema 1.1",
  "description": "Self-contained specification for extracting scientific result objects, their relationships, locally closed result sets, result families, and numerical source tables with accompanying CSV files. Includes field definitions, IDs, controlled vocabularies, extraction and export instructions, and validation checks.",
  "type": "object",
  "properties": {
    "schema_version": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "Version of this extraction data format.",
      "const": "1.1"
    },
    "paper": {
      "$ref": "#/$defs/Paper",
      "description": "The paper being extracted and its available bibliographic metadata."
    },
    "extraction": {
      "$ref": "#/$defs/Extraction",
      "description": "Extraction identity, scope, and optional revision metadata."
    },
    "sources": {
      "type": "array",
      "description": "Main paper and any consulted supplements, corrections, or cited sources.",
      "items": {
        "$ref": "#/$defs/Source",
        "description": "One consulted source document."
      },
      "minItems": 1
    },
    "tables": {
      "type": "array",
      "description": "Distinct numerical source tables, each stored once and exported as one CSV. Include descriptive and supplementary tables. An empty array certifies absence only when extraction.table_coverage.status is complete; an incomplete extraction must explain omitted exports.",
      "items": {
        "$ref": "#/$defs/TableObject",
        "description": "One complete numerical source table and its CSV metadata."
      }
    },
    "data_code_availability": {
      "$ref": "#/$defs/DataCodeAvailability",
      "description": "Optional in 1.1. Data and code availability statements and the dataset and code links named by the paper. Recommended for every extraction; omission means not extracted, not that the paper has no data or code."
    },
    "result_groups": {
      "type": "array",
      "description": "Result families formed after local closures have been derived from object relationships. Family membership is not an input to local closure.",
      "items": {
        "$ref": "#/$defs/ResultGroup",
        "description": "One substantive result group."
      }
    },
    "results": {
      "type": "array",
      "description": "Comparisons, estimates, descriptive summaries, equations, and series, each assigned to one primary group.",
      "items": {
        "$ref": "#/$defs/ResultRecord",
        "description": "One ordinary result record."
      }
    },
    "shared_context": {
      "type": "array",
      "description": "Optional reusable samples, parameters, or other context. Omit or use an empty list when none is needed.",
      "items": {
        "$ref": "#/$defs/SharedContext",
        "description": "One shared context record."
      }
    },
    "relationships": {
      "type": "array",
      "description": "Explicit scientific relationships. Review closure_direction before using them for local grouping. A missing closure_direction is unreviewed, not an assertion that the relationship does not participate.",
      "items": {
        "$ref": "#/$defs/Relationship",
        "description": "One explicit scientific connection."
      }
    },
    "cross_group_summaries": {
      "type": "array",
      "description": "Optional summaries across groups, without counting the same evidence as a new independent group.",
      "items": {
        "$ref": "#/$defs/CrossGroupSummary",
        "description": "One summary spanning existing groups."
      }
    },
    "notes": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "Document-level qualifications or extraction explanations."
    },
    "closure_review": {
      "type": "array",
      "description": "Optional object-level explanations for contextual, unassigned, or unresolved material. Use where an object is not already accounted for by a local closure, a reference, or a clear contextual record.",
      "items": {
        "$ref": "#/$defs/ClosureReviewItem",
        "description": "One object and its grouping or coverage disposition."
      }
    }
  },
  "required": [
    "schema_version",
    "paper",
    "extraction",
    "sources",
    "result_groups",
    "results",
    "tables"
  ],
  "additionalProperties": false,
  "allOf": [
    {
      "if": {
        "properties": {
          "tables": {
            "minItems": 1
          }
        },
        "required": [
          "tables"
        ]
      },
      "then": {
        "required": [
          "relationships"
        ],
        "properties": {
          "relationships": {
            "minItems": 1,
            "contains": {
              "properties": {
                "relation": {
                  "const": "table_for"
                }
              },
              "required": [
                "relation"
              ]
            }
          }
        }
      }
    }
  ],
  "$defs": {
    "PaperId": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "The assigned paper key.",
      "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*$"
    },
    "ExtractionId": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "The independently assigned extraction key within a paper.",
      "pattern": "^E(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})$"
    },
    "ResultId": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "A fully qualified identifier following the embedded ID convention.",
      "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\\.E(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.R(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})$"
    },
    "ContextId": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "A fully qualified identifier following the embedded ID convention.",
      "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\\.E(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.C(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})$"
    },
    "GroupId": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "A fully qualified identifier following the embedded ID convention.",
      "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\\.E(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.G(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})$"
    },
    "SourceId": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "A fully qualified identifier following the embedded ID convention.",
      "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\\.E(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.S(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})$"
    },
    "FieldId": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "A fully qualified identifier following the embedded ID convention.",
      "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\\.E(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.[RC](?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.F(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})$"
    },
    "ReferenceId": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "A fully qualified result, shared-context, field, group, or table ID. Source-document IDs use SourceId separately.",
      "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\\.E(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.(?:[GT](?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})|[RC](?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})(?:\\.F(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,}))?)$"
    },
    "Paper": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "Available bibliographic metadata for the paper being extracted. Other source documents are listed in sources.",
      "properties": {
        "id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/PaperId",
          "description": "Project-unique paper key, reused across independent extractions."
        },
        "title": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Paper title as reported.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "authors": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Authors in source order, when available.",
          "items": {
            "type": "string",
            "minLength": 1
          }
        },
        "year": {
          "type": "integer",
          "description": "Publication year when established."
        },
        "journal": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Journal or publication venue when available."
        },
        "doi": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "DOI as reported, without requiring a DOI when none is available."
        },
        "url": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Stable URL for the paper when available."
        },
        "citation": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Additional bibliographic text, such as volume, issue, and pages."
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Bibliographic qualifications or unresolved source-version details."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "id",
        "title"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "Extraction": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "Identity and scope of this extraction. An ordinary revision retains the extraction ID.",
      "properties": {
        "id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/ExtractionId",
          "description": "Assigned E key; independent submissions for the same paper receive different keys."
        },
        "revision": {
          "type": "integer",
          "minimum": 1,
          "description": "Revision of this extraction record; changing it does not change existing field IDs."
        },
        "date": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Date of this extraction or revision, in YYYY-MM-DD form.",
          "format": "date"
        },
        "scope": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "What was actually included: article version, supplements, tables, figures, and any partial coverage. A selected result or supplementary-document subset does not narrow the numerical-table export obligation for the main source version and all associated supplements supplied or identified by that source. Cited works are included only when explicitly part of the extraction scope; disclose unavailable required sources in table_coverage and mark coverage incomplete.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Extraction-level explanations, including whether this is an illustrative subset or a full extraction."
        },
        "table_coverage": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/TableCoverage",
          "description": "Coverage of all numerical tables, independently of which scientific fields were selected for detailed extraction."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "id",
        "scope",
        "table_coverage"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "Source": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "A document actually consulted, such as the main paper, a supplement, a correction, or a cited source.",
      "properties": {
        "id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceId",
          "description": "Unique source-document ID in this extraction, using S001, S002, and so on."
        },
        "title": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Human-readable source title or document description.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "kind": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Ordinary source description, for example main_article, supplement, correction, or cited_source."
        },
        "doi": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Source DOI when available."
        },
        "url": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Source URL when available."
        },
        "version": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Source version, date, or edition needed to distinguish original and corrected material."
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Access, page-numbering, or source-reading qualifications."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "id",
        "title"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "SourceSnippet": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "A verbatim excerpt, including table or equation text, attached where it supplies evidence. Do not duplicate it at every level.",
      "properties": {
        "source_id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceId",
          "description": "ID of a document defined in sources."
        },
        "locator": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Location within that source: page, section, table row, figure panel, equation number, or a useful combination.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "text": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Verbatim source text or table/equation excerpt. Keep paraphrases in summary or description fields.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "note": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Explanation of transcription, OCR, graphical reading, or a source discrepancy; this is not part of the quotation."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "source_id",
        "locator",
        "text"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "Reference": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "A named reference to an existing field or record. It permits reuse without creating a second definition of the same item.",
      "properties": {
        "field": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "The local field or role filled by the referenced item, such as n, sample, w, or comparison.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "target_id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/ReferenceId",
          "description": "ID of a field, result, shared context, group, or numerical table defined in this extraction. A table reference provides provenance or context without importing table cells into local closure."
        },
        "note": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Brief explanation when the field name and target do not make the use clear."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "field",
        "target_id"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "FieldEntry": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "The common entry format. Four fields are required: id, field, label, and value. Optional metadata preserves meaning without requiring an exhaustive classification.",
      "properties": {
        "id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/FieldId",
          "description": "Permanent ID of this particular entry. Its allocation prefix does not change when the entry is moved or relabeled."
        },
        "field": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Existing or plainly descriptive field name, such as cf_mean, estimate, ci_lower, p, n, or equation. Names need not come from a closed vocabulary.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "field_group": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Optional pointer to the most relevant field-vocabulary list. These lists describe typical choices and are not separate mandatory containers.",
          "enum": [
            "result_estimate",
            "descriptive_value",
            "effect_measure",
            "uncertainty_variation",
            "test_result",
            "sample_evidence_count",
            "outcome_field",
            "intervention_exposure_comparator",
            "time",
            "numerical_context",
            "mathematics",
            "other"
          ]
        },
        "label": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Shared object label. Use other when no common label fits or the classification is not established; retain the meaning in the existing value, description, or context.",
          "enum": [
            "mean",
            "count",
            "proportion",
            "probability",
            "sample_size",
            "treatment_effect",
            "association",
            "standard_error",
            "standard_deviation",
            "confidence_interval",
            "confidence_limit",
            "p_value",
            "outcome",
            "intervention",
            "exposure",
            "group",
            "time_point",
            "time_interval",
            "parameter",
            "threshold",
            "increment",
            "equation",
            "assumption",
            "condition",
            "absolute_change",
            "relative_change",
            "odds_ratio",
            "other"
          ]
        },
        "value": {
          "type": [
            "number",
            "string",
            "boolean",
            "array",
            "object",
            "null"
          ],
          "description": "The extracted content. Use a JSON number for an established scalar; a string for text or an equation; an object or array for intervals, curves, or interpreted collections of table values. A collection in value does not replace a required TableObject and complete CSV export. Preserve bounds, signs, and approximations. Explicit null means no supplied value, never zero."
        },
        "units": {
          "type": [
            "string",
            "null"
          ],
          "description": "Reported units or display scale, including percent or the counted entity when useful. Preserve source conventions; this field alone does not establish the statistic."
        },
        "value_operator": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Reported comparison or approximation for a numerical value, for example =, <, <=, >, >=, approximately, or nearly. If omitted, no additional comparison operator is asserted."
        },
        "measure": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Optional numerical form of an estimate, using the effect-measure vocabulary. For example label=treatment_effect and measure=odds_ratio. Other measures can use other with their original meaning retained in description or context.",
          "enum": [
            "absolute_change",
            "relative_change",
            "odds_ratio",
            "proportion",
            "other"
          ]
        },
        "confidence_level": {
          "type": "number",
          "exclusiveMinimum": 0,
          "maximum": 1,
          "description": "Optional confidence probability for a confidence interval or limit, stored here as a fraction such as 0.95. Convert a reported 95% only when the scale is established; retain the original display in the snippet or context."
        },
        "description": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Plain-language explanation of the entry. Especially useful for other, unusual measures, and ambiguous source wording."
        },
        "context": {
          "type": [
            "string",
            "object"
          ],
          "description": "Local context, such as group, outcome, time, test, parameter definition, adjustment, or comparison direction. Use ordinary text or named properties; a full taxonomy is not required.",
          "additionalProperties": true
        },
        "references": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Named references used specifically by this field, such as a sample or parameter stored elsewhere.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/Reference",
            "description": "A named reference to an already defined item or record."
          }
        },
        "parent_field_id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/FieldId",
          "description": "Optional F ID of a whole interval, interpreted collection of table values, curve, or expression containing this independently addressed member. This implies the specified child-field to parent-field closure edge. A source T object is not a parent field; use table_references for its cells. A parent and member are not independent observations."
        },
        "field_path": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Current location of a nested entry. With parent_field_id, this path is relative to the parent's value, for example [0].rho_C_digitized; otherwise it can describe its current extraction location. Use dot-separated keys and zero-based bracket indexes; keys containing dots or brackets can use JSON-quoted bracket notation such as [\"a.b\"]. Paths can change without changing IDs."
        },
        "origin": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "How this content was obtained, using ordinary descriptions such as source_reported, source_calculated, extractor_calculated, or digitized. Distinguish a source result from a calculation added during extraction."
        },
        "value_status": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Relevant precision, approximation, transcription, or source-consistency note. For example, digitized values are approximate; reported_exact denotes transcription fidelity."
        },
        "source_snippets": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "More specific source excerpts when record-level excerpts do not adequately identify this field.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceSnippet",
            "description": "One source excerpt and its location."
          },
          "minItems": 0
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Qualifications, uncertainty about classification, source conflicts, or other explanation that should remain available."
        },
        "table_references": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Cell provenance for separately extracted table entries. Repeated reporting may cite multiple tables or cells under one scientific field ID. Required by semantic validation for directly cell-reported fields when table coverage is complete; omit for quantities supplied only by headers, captions, or footnotes and identify those locations through snippets and named table references.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/TableCellReference",
            "description": "One directly reported source cell, with the relevant component when needed."
          },
          "minItems": 1,
          "uniqueItems": true
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "id",
        "field",
        "label",
        "value"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false,
      "dependentRequired": {
        "parent_field_id": [
          "field_path"
        ]
      }
    },
    "ResultGroup": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "A result family formed from locally closed result records answering one substantive question within one identifiable investigation, review, or generating mechanism. Membership is stored only on results[].group_id. The family is not itself a local closure or an automatic connected component.",
      "properties": {
        "id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/GroupId",
          "description": "Stable group ID, using G001, G002, and so on. Group hierarchy is not encoded in field IDs."
        },
        "title": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Short human-readable name for the result group.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "question": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "The substantive scientific question answered by this group.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "investigation": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "The experiment, observational investigation, evidence review, analytical mechanism, or numerical study at the level used for grouping.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "grouping_reason": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Explain why the already derived local results answer one question in one investigation, and why they are separate from neighboring families. Record boundary judgments without using family membership to determine local closures.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "core_result_ids": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Main local result record or records within this family. Each must belong to the family. This designation follows grouping and is distinct from each record closure.core_ids, which identifies its graph-terminal objects.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/ResultId",
            "description": "ID of an explicitly designated main result record."
          },
          "minItems": 1,
          "uniqueItems": true
        },
        "summary": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Optional plain-language account of the group's result. Preserve the scope and qualifications of the source."
        },
        "context": {
          "type": [
            "string",
            "object"
          ],
          "description": "Context common to this group. Specific result or field context may narrow it; resolve contradictions explicitly rather than silently overwriting meaning.",
          "additionalProperties": true
        },
        "references": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Shared context used by the group, such as a sample, intervention, method description, or parameter record.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/Reference",
            "description": "A named reference to an already defined item or record."
          }
        },
        "related_group_ids": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Optional cross-group associations, such as an analytical group and a numerical study evaluating it. These do not merge group membership.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/GroupId",
            "description": "ID of a scientifically related group."
          },
          "uniqueItems": true
        },
        "source_snippets": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Source excerpts supporting the group-level question or summary, when useful beyond the result excerpts.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceSnippet",
            "description": "One source excerpt and its location."
          },
          "minItems": 0
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Boundary qualifications, unresolved grouping choices, or display notes."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "id",
        "title",
        "question",
        "investigation",
        "grouping_reason",
        "core_result_ids"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "ResultRecord": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "A locally closed comparison, estimate, descriptive result, mathematical result, or series. Its closure is derived from reviewed relationships before it is assigned to a result family. The physical fields array is storage, not evidence of graph membership.",
      "properties": {
        "id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/ResultId",
          "description": "Permanent R-record ID, independent of its current result-group membership."
        },
        "group_id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/GroupId",
          "description": "The one primary result family assigned after local closure. Do not use this value, prior family IDs, or family-membership edges to construct the local closure."
        },
        "title": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Short description of the particular record.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "summary": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Optional paraphrase of what this record establishes. Verbatim wording belongs in source_snippets."
        },
        "fields": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Addressable entries physically stored in this record. Storage and allocation prefixes do not establish closure membership. An entry referenced by several closures has one definition, not duplicated definitions in each record.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/FieldEntry",
            "description": "One addressable field entry."
          },
