          "minItems": 1
        },
        "context": {
          "type": [
            "string",
            "object"
          ],
          "description": "Plain text or an open object containing the needed outcome, population, treatment, comparator, time, method, model, adjustment, assumptions, and other context. Preserve details relevant to the stated result.",
          "additionalProperties": true
        },
        "references": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Named references to already stored shared or reused information. The target retains its ID and label.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/Reference",
            "description": "A named reference to an already defined item or record."
          }
        },
        "source_snippets": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Source excerpts locating and describing this record. Field-specific excerpts can supplement these.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceSnippet",
            "description": "One source excerpt and its location."
          },
          "minItems": 1
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Limitations, source discrepancies, interpretation notes, or extraction explanations."
        },
        "closure": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/LocalClosure",
          "description": "Computed local graph closure and its review status."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "id",
        "group_id",
        "title",
        "fields",
        "source_snippets",
        "closure"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "SharedContext": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "A reusable context record, such as a participant sample or simulation settings. Use one definition and references when the same identified information applies to multiple records.",
      "properties": {
        "id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/ContextId",
          "description": "Permanent C-record ID for shared context."
        },
        "title": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Short description of the particular record.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "summary": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Optional paraphrase of what this record establishes. Verbatim wording belongs in source_snippets."
        },
        "fields": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Addressable entries belonging to or currently stored in this record.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/FieldEntry",
            "description": "One addressable field entry."
          },
          "minItems": 1
        },
        "context": {
          "type": [
            "string",
            "object"
          ],
          "description": "Plain text or an open object containing the needed outcome, population, treatment, comparator, time, method, model, adjustment, assumptions, and other context. Preserve details relevant to the stated result.",
          "additionalProperties": true
        },
        "references": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Named references to already stored shared or reused information. The target retains its ID and label.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/Reference",
            "description": "A named reference to an already defined item or record."
          }
        },
        "source_snippets": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Source excerpts locating and describing this record. Field-specific excerpts can supplement these.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceSnippet",
            "description": "One source excerpt and its location."
          },
          "minItems": 1
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Limitations, source discrepancies, interpretation notes, or extraction explanations."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "id",
        "title",
        "fields",
        "source_snippets"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "CrossGroupSummary": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "An optional summary across existing groups, as in Perel's comparison across six intervention domains. It is not assigned a primary group and does not duplicate the underlying evidence.",
      "properties": {
        "id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/ResultId",
          "description": "R-record ID allocated from the same extraction-wide R sequence as ordinary results."
        },
        "title": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Short description of the particular record.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "summary": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Plain-language synthesis across the named groups, preserving its evidence level and scope.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "fields": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Addressable values in the cross-group summary, such as a reported number of concordant intervention domains. Use an empty array when no addressable values are needed.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/FieldEntry",
            "description": "One addressable field entry."
          },
          "minItems": 0
        },
        "context": {
          "type": [
            "string",
            "object"
          ],
          "description": "Plain text or an open object containing the needed outcome, population, treatment, comparator, time, method, model, adjustment, assumptions, and other context. Preserve details relevant to the stated result.",
          "additionalProperties": true
        },
        "references": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Named references to already stored shared or reused information. The target retains its ID and label.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/Reference",
            "description": "A named reference to an already defined item or record."
          }
        },
        "source_snippets": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Source excerpts locating and describing this record. Field-specific excerpts can supplement these.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceSnippet",
            "description": "One source excerpt and its location."
          },
          "minItems": 1
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Limitations, source discrepancies, interpretation notes, or extraction explanations."
        },
        "group_ids": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Groups explicitly summarized by this record. This summary does not create a new independently generated result group.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/GroupId",
            "description": "ID of a group summarized here."
          },
          "minItems": 2,
          "uniqueItems": true
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "id",
        "title",
        "group_ids",
        "summary",
        "fields",
        "source_snippets"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "Relationship": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "An explicit scientific connection or numerical-table provenance link between defined objects. Scientific relations retain their direction. closure_direction separately records local grouping; table links are always non-closing.",
      "properties": {
        "source_id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/ReferenceId",
          "description": "The source endpoint, following the direction given in the relationship vocabulary."
        },
        "relation": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "A common relationship code, or other with a short explanation.",
          "enum": [
            "uncertainty_of",
            "spread_of",
            "p_value_of",
            "describes",
            "compares",
            "derived_from",
            "contributes_to",
            "applies_under",
            "table_for",
            "other"
          ]
        },
        "target_id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/ReferenceId",
          "description": "The target endpoint, defined in this extraction."
        },
        "role": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Endpoint role. compares requires evaluated or reference. table_for requires reports_result, describes_sample, provides_inputs, or provides_context, identifying how the table relates to the result without asserting evidential strength."
        },
        "context": {
          "type": [
            "string",
            "object"
          ],
          "description": "Additional scope or logical wording needed to interpret this particular connection.",
          "additionalProperties": true
        },
        "basis": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Brief basis for the connection, distinguishing an explicit source association from an extraction interpretation or calculation."
        },
        "note": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Short explanation, required for other so its meaning remains available.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "source_snippets": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Specific source support for the connection when it is not apparent from its endpoints.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceSnippet",
            "description": "One source excerpt and its location."
          },
          "minItems": 0
        },
        "closure_direction": {
          "type": "string",
          "enum": [
            "source_to_target",
            "target_to_source",
            "none"
          ],
          "description": "Reviewed projection into subordinate-to-core direction for local closure. source_to_target makes the source subordinate to the target; target_to_source reverses the scientific edge for this calculation; none retains the connection without expanding local membership. Omission means unreviewed, not none. G/S metadata and family-membership links do not participate in the local graph. Every relationship with a T endpoint requires none."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "source_id",
        "relation",
        "target_id"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false,
      "allOf": [
        {
          "if": {
            "properties": {
              "relation": {
                "const": "compares"
              }
            },
            "required": [
              "relation"
            ]
          },
          "then": {
            "required": [
              "role"
            ],
            "properties": {
              "role": {
                "enum": [
                  "evaluated",
                  "reference"
                ]
              }
            }
          }
        },
        {
          "if": {
            "properties": {
              "relation": {
                "const": "other"
              }
            },
            "required": [
              "relation"
            ]
          },
          "then": {
            "required": [
              "note"
            ]
          }
        },
        {
          "if": {
            "properties": {
              "relation": {
                "const": "table_for"
              }
            },
            "required": [
              "relation"
            ]
          },
          "then": {
            "required": [
              "role",
              "closure_direction"
            ],
            "properties": {
              "source_id": {
                "$ref": "#/$defs/TableId"
              },
              "target_id": {
                "$ref": "#/$defs/ResultId"
              },
              "role": {
                "enum": [
                  "reports_result",
                  "describes_sample",
                  "provides_inputs",
                  "provides_context"
                ]
              },
              "closure_direction": {
                "const": "none"
              }
            }
          }
        },
        {
          "if": {
            "anyOf": [
              {
                "properties": {
                  "source_id": {
                    "$ref": "#/$defs/TableId"
                  }
                },
                "required": [
                  "source_id"
                ]
              },
              {
                "properties": {
                  "target_id": {
                    "$ref": "#/$defs/TableId"
                  }
                },
                "required": [
                  "target_id"
                ]
              }
            ]
          },
          "then": {
            "required": [
              "closure_direction"
            ],
            "properties": {
              "closure_direction": {
                "const": "none"
              }
            }
          }
        }
      ]
    },
    "LocalClosure": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "The computed closure of a local result under selected subordinate-to-core relationships and parent-field inclusion. It is complete for the represented local inputs, not isolated from all other scientific relationships. Several closures may contain the same existing ID; the object remains stored once.",
      "properties": {
        "core_ids": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "The eligible terminal field, known comparison anchor, or explained compound core from which this closure is calculated. These are local core objects, distinct from family-level core_result_ids. G/S metadata and T source-table objects cannot serve as local cores.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/ClosureObjectId",
            "description": "One existing R/C record or F field eligible for local closure; never a G, S, or T object."
          },
          "uniqueItems": true,
          "minItems": 1
        },
        "member_ids": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Exactly the core IDs and every object that can reach them along the reviewed subordinate-to-core graph, including implicit child-field to parent-field edges. Compute this set; do not fill it from stored field ownership or family membership. Shared same-ID members may occur in more than one closure without merging those closures. T source containers and CSV coordinates never create membership or parent-field edges.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/ClosureObjectId",
            "description": "One existing R/C record or F field eligible for local closure; never a G, S, or T object."
          },
          "uniqueItems": true,
          "minItems": 1
        },
        "reference_ids": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Existing external inputs, context, related results, and source tables at this boundary. Include external endpoints of reviewed non-closing relationships incident on members, named references attached to the result or its members, table_for source IDs whose target is this result record (even when the R ID is not a member), and table_ids in member fields' table_references. Exclude member IDs and deduplicate. References never import their own descendants or ancestors.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/ReferenceId",
            "description": "One existing field or record ID."
          },
          "uniqueItems": true,
          "minItems": 0
        },
        "status": {
          "type": "string",
          "enum": [
            "closed",
            "review_required"
          ],
          "description": "closed means the represented local graph has a reviewed core, computed members, resolved references, and no unresolved incident relationship or boundary issue. It does not certify complete paper coverage. Use review_required while any such issue remains."
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Explain a known comparison anchor, accepted compound core or cycle, overlap, unresolved link, or other local-boundary judgment. Required by the semantic instructions when status is review_required or a nontrivial cycle is accepted."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "core_ids",
        "member_ids",
        "reference_ids",
        "status"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false,
      "allOf": [
        {
          "if": {
            "properties": {
              "status": {
                "const": "review_required"
              }
            },
            "required": [
              "status"
            ]
          },
          "then": {
            "required": [
              "notes"
            ],
            "properties": {
              "notes": {
                "minLength": 1
              }
            }
          }
        }
      ]
    },
    "ClosureReviewItem": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "A brief disposition of an object requiring an explicit coverage or grouping explanation.",
      "properties": {
        "object_id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/ReferenceId",
          "description": "The existing field or record requiring a disposition."
        },
        "reason": {
          "type": "string",
          "minLength": 1,
          "description": "Why the object needs a review or separate contextual disposition."
        },
        "disposition": {
          "type": "string",
          "minLength": 1,
          "description": "Ordinary text stating how the object is handled, for example shared context, unresolved attachment, or awaiting source review. This is not a new scientific object taxonomy."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "object_id",
        "reason",
        "disposition"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "TableId": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "Permanent ID of one source table in this extraction. T has its own positive counter; a table is independent of result allocation and family membership.",
      "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\\.E(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.T(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})$"
    },
    "ClosureObjectId": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "ID eligible for local scientific closure: an R or C record, or an F field allocated under R or C. G, S, and T identifiers are excluded.",
      "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\\.E(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.[RC](?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})(?:\\.F(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,}))?$"
    },
    "TableColumn": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "One CSV column, in CSV order. The key is a flattened machine-readable name; header_path retains the source headings and their hierarchy.",
      "properties": {
        "name": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Unique column key appearing exactly in the CSV header and in this table's columns[].name.",
          "pattern": "^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$"
        },
        "header_path": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Original heading hierarchy, including units and markers printed in headings. Use [] only for an originally unheaded or extractor-added descriptor column.",
          "items": {
            "type": "string",
            "description": "One source heading or subheading, transcribed as printed, from outermost to innermost."
          }
        },
        "role": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Layout role of this column. data contains source values, including any retained text data; label identifies source rows; context carries panel or inherited group labels.",
          "enum": [
            "data",
            "label",
            "context"
          ]
        },
        "units": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Units or display scale applicable to the entire column, when stated. Use scoped annotations when units vary by row."
        },
        "description": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Explanation of an unheaded column, flattened hierarchy, or extractor-added descriptor. Do not present an invented heading as source text."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "name",
        "header_path",
        "role"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "TableAnnotation": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "An interpretive qualification or layout fact preserved outside the CSV. rows and columns delimit its scope: both select their Cartesian product; one selects those rows or columns; neither means table-wide.",
      "properties": {
        "kind": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Type of qualification or layout information.",
          "enum": [
            "footnote",
            "unit",
            "statistical_convention",
            "panel",
            "merged_cell",
            "missing_value",
            "structural_blank",
            "transcription",
            "other"
          ]
        },
        "text": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Full source wording for a reported note, or an explicitly identified explanation of layout/transcription. Preserve uncertainty and missing-value meanings; do not infer them from typography alone.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "marker": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Source footnote or significance marker when present, for example *, a, or †. Preserve the same marker in its source-display CSV cell or, for source headings, in the applicable column header_path."
        },
        "rows": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Affected CSV data-record positions; omit for all rows.",
          "items": {
            "type": "integer",
            "minimum": 1,
            "description": "One-based CSV data-record position, excluding the header. Count parsed records, not physical text lines."
          },
          "minItems": 1,
          "uniqueItems": true
        },
        "columns": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Affected CSV column keys; omit for all columns.",
          "items": {
            "type": "string",
            "description": "Unique column key appearing exactly in the CSV header and in this table's columns[].name.",
            "pattern": "^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$"
          },
          "minItems": 1,
          "uniqueItems": true
        },
        "source_locator": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "More specific source location for the note or layout feature, when helpful.",
          "minLength": 1
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "kind",
        "text"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false,
      "allOf": [
        {
          "if": {
            "properties": {
              "kind": {
                "const": "merged_cell"
              }
            },
            "required": [
              "kind"
            ]
          },
          "then": {
            "required": [
              "rows",
              "columns"
            ]
          }
        }
      ]
    },
    "TableObject": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "One complete numerical source table, represented independently of scientific result boundaries. Its CSV holds the source-reported cells; metadata preserves their interpretation. Multiple panels and page continuations belong to the same object.",
      "properties": {
        "id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/TableId",
          "description": "Permanent table ID, allocated once and retained through corrections or changes in linked results."
        },
        "source_id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceId",
          "description": "Document containing the table; resolves in sources."
        },
        "locator": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Source table number or an unambiguous locator including page/panel locations when needed. Include every page of a continued table.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "title": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Source title or a plainly descriptive title if the source has none. Do not present an extractor-supplied title as a quotation.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "caption": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Complete source caption, transcribed as printed, when present. Footnotes and scoped qualifications belong in annotations."
        },
        "csv_file": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Basename of the accompanying CSV, exactly id + \".csv\". It is relative to the extraction JSON directory and must resolve to a delivered file; a filename alone is not an export.",
          "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\\.E(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.T(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.csv$"
        },
        "columns": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "All exported columns. Names must be unique within the table; preserve original heading hierarchies in header_path.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/TableColumn",
            "description": "One column in the exact order of the CSV header."
          },
          "minItems": 1
        },
        "row_count": {
          "type": "integer",
          "minimum": 1,
          "description": "Number of parsed CSV data records, excluding the single header record. Count records rather than physical lines, since quoted cells may contain line breaks."
        },
        "annotations": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Complete applicable footnotes, units, statistical conventions, missing-value explanations, and layout qualifications. Use [] only when none are present or needed.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/TableAnnotation",
            "description": "One scoped qualification or layout annotation."
          }
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Source discrepancies, disambiguation of repeated labels, or explicit transcription decisions. Never use a note to claim that an incomplete table export is complete."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "id",
        "source_id",
        "locator",
        "title",
        "csv_file",
        "columns",
        "row_count",
        "annotations"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "TableCellReference": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "Provenance for a field directly reported in a numerical table cell. It identifies a location in the delivered CSV, not an additional independent observation or a local grouping edge.",
      "properties": {
        "table_id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/TableId",
          "description": "Existing table object whose CSV contains the source-reported entry."
        },
        "row": {
          "type": "integer",
          "minimum": 1,
          "description": "One-based CSV data-record position, excluding the header. Count parsed records, not physical text lines."
        },
        "column": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Unique column key appearing exactly in the CSV header and in this table's columns[].name.",
          "pattern": "^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$"
        },
        "component": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Part of a composite source cell represented by this field, such as mean, standard_error, lower confidence limit, count, or percentage. Omit when the whole cell supplies the value.",
          "minLength": 1
        },
        "note": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Explanation needed to reconcile source display, field representation, or a reported discrepancy. Do not use this to substitute a calculated value for a source-reported value."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "table_id",
        "row",
        "column"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "TableCoverage": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "Explicit completeness declaration for the mandatory numerical-table exports. Structural validity permits an incomplete working record; completed miner delivery requires status=complete and successful source and file checks.",
      "properties": {
        "status": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "complete means all qualifying tables in the required source scope have complete, delivered CSVs; incomplete means coverage or any export remains unresolved.",
          "enum": [
            "complete",
            "incomplete"
          ]
        },
        "reviewed_source_ids": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Documents reviewed for table coverage, including the main source version and all associated supplements supplied or identified by that source. This list must not imply that an unavailable document was read.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceId",
            "description": "A source document actually inspected for numerical-table coverage."
          },
          "uniqueItems": true
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "For incomplete coverage, identify missing documents, tables, unreadable regions, or deliberately omitted instructional exports. For complete coverage with no tables, record that no qualifying tables were found.",
          "minLength": 1
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "status",
        "reviewed_source_ids"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false,
      "allOf": [
        {
          "if": {
            "properties": {
              "status": {
                "const": "incomplete"
              }
            },
            "required": [
              "status"
            ]
          },
          "then": {
            "required": [
              "notes"
            ]
          }
        },
        {
          "if": {
            "properties": {
              "status": {
                "const": "complete"
              }
            },
            "required": [
              "status"
            ]
          },
          "then": {
            "properties": {
              "reviewed_source_ids": {
                "minItems": 1
              }
            }
          }
        }
      ]
    },
    "ArtifactId": {
      "type": "string",
      "description": "Permanent ID of one data or code artifact named by the paper. A has its own positive counter per extraction; it is independent of result, table, and family allocation.",
      "pattern": "^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]*\\.E(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})\\.A(?:00[1-9]|0[1-9][0-9]|[1-9][0-9]{2,})$"
    },
    "AvailabilityStatement": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "What the paper says about the availability of one kind of material (data or code). Record the paper's statement; do not test access.",
      "properties": {
        "status": {
          "type": "string",
          "enum": [
            "available",
            "available_on_request",
            "restricted",
            "not_available",
            "not_stated",
            "not_applicable"
          ],
          "description": "available: the paper states the material is publicly accessible or names where it is. available_on_request: access requires contacting authors or a committee. restricted: access requires an application, licence, or controlled-access agreement, or is embargoed. not_available: the paper states it cannot be shared. not_stated: the paper and supplied supplements say nothing. not_applicable: the paper generates and uses no such material, for example code for a purely analytical paper."
        },
        "source_snippets": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Verbatim text of the availability statement or the passage that names the material. Required unless status is not_stated or not_applicable.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceSnippet"
          },
          "minItems": 0
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Qualifications, such as partial availability or conflicting statements between the article and supplements."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "status"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false,
      "allOf": [
        {
          "if": {
            "properties": {
              "status": {
                "enum": [
                  "available",
                  "available_on_request",
                  "restricted",
                  "not_available"
                ]
              }
            },
            "required": [
              "status"
            ]
          },
          "then": {
            "required": [
              "source_snippets"
            ],
            "properties": {
              "source_snippets": {
                "minItems": 1
              }
            }
          }
        }
      ]
    },
    "ArtifactLink": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "One dataset, code repository, or other research artifact that the paper names with a link or persistent identifier. Record it as written in the source; never fetch, open, or run it during extraction.",
      "properties": {
        "id": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/ArtifactId",
          "description": "Permanent A ID, using A001, A002, and so on."
        },
        "artifact_type": {
          "type": "string",
          "enum": [
            "dataset",
            "code",
            "other"
          ],
          "description": "dataset: data files, databases, or accession records. code: source code, scripts, notebooks, or software repositories. other: for example a preregistration or protocol; explain it in description."
        },
        "url": {
          "type": "string",
          "minLength": 1,
          "description": "URL exactly as printed in the source, including any version or commit path."
        },
        "identifier": {
          "type": "string",
          "minLength": 1,
          "description": "Persistent identifier as printed, such as a DOI, accession number (GEO GSE…, dbGaP phs…, PDB ID), or software archive ID."
        },
        "identifier_type": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Open description of the identifier scheme, for example doi, accession, rrid, or swhid."
        },
        "repository": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Hosting service or archive as named or evident from the link, for example GitHub, Zenodo, Figshare, OSF, Dryad, GEO, or UK Biobank."
        },
        "description": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "What the artifact contains, in plain language."
        },
        "provenance": {
          "type": "string",
          "enum": [
            "generated_by_this_paper",
            "reused",
            "unclear"
          ],
          "description": "generated_by_this_paper: data or code produced for this paper. reused: a third-party or previously published resource the paper analysed or built on. unclear: the source does not establish which."
        },
        "access": {
          "type": "string",
          "enum": [
            "open",
            "on_request",
            "restricted",
            "unclear"
          ],
          "description": "Access condition as stated in the source, not as tested."
        },
        "source_snippets": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Verbatim source text that contains the URL or identifier. At least one snippet must contain the url or identifier exactly as recorded.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceSnippet"
          },
          "minItems": 1
        },
        "related_ids": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Optional results, result groups, shared context, or tables that the source says use this artifact. These are provenance pointers; they never participate in local closure.",
          "items": {
            "anyOf": [
              {
                "$ref": "#/$defs/ReferenceId"
              },
              {
                "$ref": "#/$defs/TableId"
              }
            ]
          },
          "uniqueItems": true
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Qualifications, such as a broken-looking link as printed or a version mismatch between article and supplement."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "id",
        "artifact_type",
        "provenance",
        "access",
        "source_snippets"
      ],
      "anyOf": [
        {
          "required": [
            "url"
          ]
        },
        {
          "required": [
            "identifier"
          ]
        }
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    },
    "DataCodeAvailability": {
      "type": "object",
      "description": "The paper's data and code availability: its statements and every data or code artifact it names with a link or identifier. Records what the paper says; it does not certify that a link works or contains what is claimed.",
      "properties": {
        "data": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/AvailabilityStatement",
          "description": "Availability of the data the paper analyses or generates."
        },
        "code": {
          "$ref": "#/$defs/AvailabilityStatement",
          "description": "Availability of the analysis code or software the paper uses or generates."
        },
        "artifacts": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Each distinct dataset, code repository, or other artifact named with a URL or identifier, in the main article and supplied supplements. One entry per artifact, even when it is named several times. Use an empty list when none is named.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/ArtifactLink"
          }
        },
        "reviewed_source_ids": {
          "type": "array",
          "description": "Source documents inspected for availability statements and links.",
          "items": {
            "$ref": "#/$defs/SourceId"
          },
          "minItems": 1,
          "uniqueItems": true
        },
        "notes": {
          "type": "string",
          "description": "Coverage gaps, such as an unavailable supplement that may hold the statement."
        }
      },
      "required": [
        "data",
        "code",
        "artifacts",
        "reviewed_source_ids"
      ],
      "additionalProperties": false
    }
  },
  "x-how_to_use": [
    "Use this document alone as the extraction specification. It defines the JSON format, numerical-table CSV package, and application-level instructions for graph closure, family formation, and validation.",
    "This file is both the JSON Schema and its instruction package. Ordinary validation uses properties and $defs; the x- sections define graph closure, grouping, vocabulary, and semantic checks.",
    "Create an extraction data file with the top-level sections defined under properties. Do not copy these instruction and vocabulary sections into every extraction record.",
    "A field entry requires only id, field, label, and value. Scientific content remains flexible through value, context, description, notes, and other labels.",
    "Deliver the extraction JSON together with one CSV per numerical table, following x-table_extraction. Include tables[] and extraction.table_coverage even when no numerical tables are present. A completed submission requires complete table coverage; an incomplete example or working record is not a completed submission.",
    "Review explicit scientific relationships before computing local closures. A plainly named field association can justify an edge, but it must be materialized as a reviewed relationship when the closure calculation uses it. parent_field_id is the one specified implicit grouping link.",
    "Compute local member sets from relationships and parent-field links, then assign result families. Stored arrays, record allocation prefixes, and family-membership edges are not graph inputs.",
    "The required closure is a computed record of the local grouping. Shared objects retain one definition and can occur by ID in several member or reference lists.",
    "Validate the JSON structure, then apply the semantic checks. A missing closure_direction is permitted while extracting, but affected results must remain review_required until its relevance is resolved. CSV presence, source coverage, and source fidelity are application checks; JSON Schema alone cannot perform them."
  ],
  "x-extraction_instructions": [
    "Read the source context and retained snippets alongside the structured fields. The objective is reliable identification and connection of common inference-relevant objects, not exhaustive classification of every detail.",
    "Follow x-result_grouping in order: objects, reviewed scientific relationships, local closures, then result families. Use one generic fields[] format for scientific entries; the 11 vocabulary lists are guides, not mandatory containers.",
    "Give substantive populated entries their field IDs using x-id_convention. Keep labels, units, names, and context outside IDs. Labels and source details can be corrected without renumbering the same entry.",
    "Use the embedded shared label codes consistently. Other is valid in every field group. Explain an unusual item through its existing field name, value, description, or context; a new narrow object category is unnecessary.",
    "Preserve a source value and its meaning. In particular retain units/display scale, inequality operators, reference conditions, exposure increments, adjustment/model information, time scope, and uncertainty type where they matter.",
    "Use measure on the relevant estimate when the numerical form needs to be stated, for example treatment_effect with odds_ratio or relative_change. A proportion of positive studies stays proportion.",
    "Keep standard deviation distinct from standard error and confidence intervals. Keep lower/upper confidence-limit meaning in the field name or parent interval. Confidence levels stored in the optional confidence_level property use probability scale.",
    "Identify the actual target of each SE, CI, p-value, count, and contrast. Named fields and source context can establish the interpretation; materialize the corresponding scientific relationship with closure_direction if it participates in closure. A heterogeneity or bias p-value must stay with that diagnostic, not the treatment-effect estimate.",
    "Use source_snippets for verbatim source text with document and locator. Use summary, description, or notes for paraphrases and extraction reasoning. A record-level excerpt can support several fields; duplicate excerpts are unnecessary.",
    "Store each shared sample, parameter, or input once and refer to its ID. Shared inputs may occur in several local member sets without merging them. A non-closing context link instead appears in reference_ids. Equal values do not establish identity, and a group-level count must not replace a result-specific count.",
    "Keep equations and interpreted arrays in useful existing representations. Every numerical source table also requires its distinct T object and complete CSV under x-table_extraction, regardless of which cells become F fields. Address individual scientific terms or cells as F fields when needed, and record table_references for independently extracted source cells. parent_field_id connects only F collections and members; it never links a field to a T object. A source table, its interpreted collection, and its addressed cells are not additional independent observations.",
    "Export all numerical tables, including baseline characteristics, other descriptive summaries, diagnostic tables, null findings, and supplementary tables. Link each table to the ordinary result records it reports or contextualizes with table_for and closure_direction=none. Do not invent a treatment effect, comparison, or main finding solely to attach a table; an appropriate descriptive result is sufficient.",
    "Distinguish reported, source-calculated, extractor-calculated, and digitized content in origin or context. A separate calculated or converted entry gets a new ID and a derived_from connection when useful; preserve the reported entry.",
    "When context is stored at several levels, local details may narrow broader context. Conflicting bindings require an explicit note or corrected record; do not silently choose a convenient interpretation.",
    "Do not turn every graph sink into a result. Review support-only sinks, unreviewed relationships, cycles, and disconnected quantities. Keep unresolved objects visible in closure_review or a result with closure.status=review_required; do not silently discard them.",
    "Record data and code availability in data_code_availability. Read the article's data availability, code availability, and software sections, the methods, acknowledgements, and supplied supplements. Classify the data and code statements separately, use not_stated when the paper says nothing, and create one ArtifactLink per distinct dataset or code repository named with a URL or persistent identifier. Copy URLs and identifiers exactly as printed and support each with a verbatim snippet. Do not fetch, open, clone, or execute any link during extraction, and do not add links that the source does not print.",
    "Validate the JSON structure with this JSON Schema, then perform x-validation_checks for identity, references, local closure, families, scientific attachment, numerical-table coverage, and actual CSV files. Structural validity alone does not establish scientific correctness or delivery completeness."
  ],
  "x-table_extraction": {
    "rule": "Every numerical source table in the main source version and its associated supplementary material must be represented as a distinct TableObject, linked appropriately to ordinary scientific results, and delivered as a separate CSV whose filename is recorded in that object. Descriptive tables are included regardless of whether they establish a main scientific conclusion.",
    "qualification": [
      "A numerical table has a substantive data region consisting of numbers, numerical expressions, or reported missing-value markers. Textual row and column labels, study identifiers, model names, panel labels, units, categorical row qualifiers, significance symbols, and footnotes do not disqualify it.",
      "Counts, percentages, means, estimates, uncertainty intervals, thresholds, inequalities, and composite cells such as 156.8 ± 8.2 or 1.32 (1.07–1.63) all qualify. Regression, baseline-characteristic, diagnostic, parameter, and supplementary numerical tables are included.",
      "If a mixed table has a clearly delimited numerical data region, it qualifies and the whole source table is exported, including accompanying text data columns. A purely narrative table without such a region is outside this mandatory numerical-table rule.",
      "Tables embedded as images and tables continued across pages qualify. A graph, equation, prose list, or extractor-created numerical array is not a source table merely because it contains numbers."
    ],
    "identity_and_scope": [
      "Use one T object and one CSV per source table. Combine all source panels and continuation pages. Table identity follows the source, not the number of results, closures, or families using its values.",
      "Export the entire table once it qualifies, including numerical entries that do not receive separate F IDs. Do not omit descriptive rows, non-significant results, alternative models, totals, denominators, or uncertainty columns.",
      "For repeated source publication of the same entry, reuse its scientific F ID when identity is established, while preserving distinct T objects for distinct source tables. A repeated page header or continuation is not a second table. Distinct source versions use separately identified Source records and table IDs.",
      "The required table scope includes the main source version and all associated supplementary documents supplied or identified by that source. Do not narrow this obligation by selecting only certain results or supplements. If a required supplement is unavailable, disclose it and mark table coverage incomplete. Cited papers are covered when expressly included in the extraction scope."
    ],
    "result_links": [
      "Add a table_for relationship from every T object to at least one appropriate ordinary R result. Its role is reports_result, describes_sample, provides_inputs, or provides_context; its closure_direction is always none. Link every directly reported result, and identify contextual uses that the extraction relies on.",
      "A numerical table may support several results and several families. Reuse its T ID and CSV. Its layout does not decide local closures or family boundaries.",
      "A purely descriptive table may link to a descriptive result or, when appropriate, to a result whose sample or context it describes. When a new descriptive result is needed, derive it through the ordinary field and relationship procedure. Do not invent a comparison, hypothesis test, effect, or main finding just to attach the table.",
      "All T objects remain outside the local closure graph. The reference calculation explicitly includes table_for sources targeting an R record even when that R ID is not itself in member_ids. Table IDs in member-field cell references are also boundary references."
    ],
    "csv_format": {
      "encoding": "UTF-8 without a byte-order mark (BOM).",
      "delimiter": "Comma.",
      "quote_character": "Double quote. Double an internal double quote to escape it. Quote cells containing commas, double quotes, carriage returns, or newlines; other cells may also be quoted.",
      "record_separator": "LF (newline). Embedded cell line breaks remain inside a quoted cell. A final record separator is required.",
      "header": "Exactly one header record. Keys must be unique, nonempty, lower-case snake_case, and match columns[].name in order. Derive descriptive flattened keys from source headers; add a numeric suffix if necessary for uniqueness. Store every original hierarchical heading in header_path. For a genuinely unheaded or added descriptor column, use an intelligible key and explain it in description.",
      "location": "Each CSV is in the same directory as the extraction JSON. csv_file is exactly the full table ID followed by .csv. Deliver the actual file, not only its name, URL, or an inline string in an extraction record.",
      "cell_type": "CSV values are source-display strings. Do not apply automatic type inference, date conversion, numerical normalization, or spreadsheet formula interpretation to the archival transcription."
    },
    "flattening_and_fidelity": [
      "Retain source data-row order and every data column. Each source data row contributes one CSV data record. Pure separator rows, repeated continuation headers, caption rows, and footnote blocks are not data records; preserve their meaning through descriptor columns, caption, and annotations.",
      "Preserve textual row labels as explicit columns. Carry hierarchical row and panel labels into separate descriptor columns so every data row is identifiable. Repeating an inherited textual label is permitted; do not fill down numerical data or statistical tests.",
      "Flatten multi-level column headings into unique keys while retaining the complete source heading hierarchy in each column's header_path. If panels have incompatible columns, use the union of columns and explicit panel/row-label columns. Record layout-induced empty cells with structural_blank annotations so they are not mistaken for source-reported missing data.",
      "Preserve the printed numerical string, precision, signs, inequalities, percentage symbols, significance markers, and composite expressions. Keep 156.8 ± 8.2 and 1.32 (1.07–1.63) as single CSV cells when printed as single cells. Scientific decomposition into means, uncertainty measures, and interval endpoints belongs in F entries with component-specific table_references.",
      "Preserve reported missing markers exactly, for example NA, a dash, or a blank. Do not map all markers to an empty string, convert them to zero, or infer that a blank means zero or non-significance. Retain the source explanation of each marker in annotations; if no meaning is supplied, state that it is not specified.",
      "For a merged numerical cell spanning several source rows or columns, place its source string once at the first covered CSV row and leftmost covered exported column, leave the remaining covered cells blank, and record the span and its meaning with a merged_cell annotation. The annotation scope lists the covered rows and columns. This is particularly important for one joint p-value spanning several categories.",
      "Keep the complete caption and all interpretive footnotes, units, statistical conventions, and scoped qualifications in TableObject metadata. Annotation row and column lists refer to CSV positions/keys, not printed row numbers. A table-wide note omits both lists.",
      "Transcribe source characters from the readable source rather than accepting an obvious text-extraction substitution; document nontrivial transcription decisions. Preserve genuinely printed inconsistencies and flag them in notes. Do not silently recompute a printed percentage or replace a source-reported estimate."
    ],
    "cell_provenance": [
      "Separately extracted F entries reported in table data cells must identify their table_references when coverage is complete. Each reference names the T object, one-based CSV data-record position excluding the header, and exact column key. Coordinates count parsed CSV records, not physical lines.",
      "Use component to identify the relevant part of a composite cell. Several F fields can reference different components of the same cell. A field clearly repeated in several tables may list several references while retaining one F ID.",
      "Quantities printed only in a header, caption, or footnote use an exact source snippet and a named Reference to the T object; do not fabricate a data-row coordinate such as row 0. Extractor-calculated values instead identify input fields through derived_from and preserve the calculation basis.",
      "For interpreted F collections, use a named table reference for the source collection and cell references on independently addressed constituent fields. parent_field_id and field_path refer only to the interpreted F representation. They never establish containment in a T object.",
      "Update CSV coordinates after a legitimate table-transcription correction without changing scientific F IDs or the table T ID. Do not reorder the canonical CSV merely to facilitate analysis; create downstream analytical views separately."
    ],
    "completion": [
      "Set extraction.table_coverage.status to complete only after reviewing the required sources, exporting every qualifying table in full, resolving each table's result links and field-cell references, and validating the actual CSV package.",
      "If a required document or table region is unavailable, unreadable, or unresolved, use incomplete and describe the specific gap in table_coverage.notes. Never invent missing cells or describe a selected excerpt as a complete source-table export. TableObjects in tables[] describe complete exports; unfinished exports remain disclosed work rather than accepted tables.",
      "When no qualifying table exists, use tables:[] with complete coverage, list the reviewed sources, and state in table_coverage.notes that no numerical tables were found. An empty tables array in an incomplete record makes no such claim.",
      "A schema-valid incomplete record may be stored for review or instruction but must not be accepted as a completed miner submission. Local closure.status=closed only certifies the represented local scientific graph and does not override incomplete table coverage."
    ],
    "validation_boundary": "JSON Schema enforces the object shapes, required metadata, identifier patterns, table-link role/direction restrictions, and exclusion of T IDs from local cores and members. The application must additionally verify reference resolution, all-table coverage, per-table result links, filename equality, actual CSV files, dimensions, cell provenance, and faithful source reconstruction using V20–V27.",
    "instructional_examples": "The four real-paper examples are deliberately incomplete instructional subsets and omit full table exports; their table_coverage is incomplete. They must not be treated as completed submissions. The synthetic numerical-table example demonstrates the entire table-export contract; its accompanying CSV text is supplied below for instruction and must be written as an actual CSV file when constructing that example package.",
    "example_csv_files": [
      {
        "extraction_prefix": "P005.E001",
        "table_id": "P005.E001.T001",
        "csv_file": "P005.E001.T001.csv",
        "content": "outcome,group_a_mean_se,group_b_mean_se,p_value\nMarker A (mg/L),12.0 ± 1.2,10.0 ± 1.1,0.04*\nMarker B (ng/mL),3.50 ± 0.20,4.10 ± 0.30,<0.001*\n",
        "instruction": "Write this exact UTF-8 text to the named CSV alongside the synthetic extraction JSON. Inline content is provided only to make this schema example self-contained; actual miner submissions deliver the file."
      }
    ]
  },
  "x-result_grouping": {
    "definition": "First derive each local result as a core plus its closed set of represented local inputs. Then form result families from those local results using the substantive question and generating investigation.",
    "local_closure_definition": "Normalize selected scientific relationships to subordinate -> core. For a terminal core K, its members are K and all nodes that can reach K along these edges. The set is closed under selected local inputs. Non-closing scientific connections and external context remain references; closure does not mean isolation in the full graph.",
    "family_definition": "A family answers one substantive question within one identifiable investigation, evidence synthesis, or generating mechanism. It can contain several local closures and several main results.",
    "steps": [
      {
        "step": 1,
        "name": "Fix scope and inventory",
        "instruction": "Record the source version and included supplements. Identify scientific numbers, equations, arrays, and the context required to interpret them. Administrative numbers remain metadata unless scientifically used. Preserve the object inventory during regrouping so omitted objects remain detectable. Inventory every numerical source table in the main paper and included supplements, including descriptive tables, and its complete CSV export. A source table remains one T object even when its scientific entries belong to several local results or families."
      },
      {
        "step": 2,
        "name": "Assign object IDs and common labels",
        "instruction": "Assign permanent neutral IDs and the accepted common labels. Use other when appropriate. Existing comparison or compound-result anchors may be retained when needed to identify what a joint test or set of measurements concerns. State that those anchors are supplied scientific interpretations, not findings inferred from family membership. IDs may be allocated in a staging record before closure is known. Their allocation prefix describes permanent identity, not the local membership later computed from relations. Assign T IDs to source tables separately from the R/C/F scientific-entry sequence; table identity is not a scientific core designation."
      },
      {
        "step": 3,
        "name": "Build and review scientific links",
        "instruction": "Record the typed object relationships, using source context and existing field associations. Review closure_direction on every relationship relevant to a candidate result. Omission is unreviewed. Mark cross-result, shared-context, family-level, and other non-closing links none. Do not use prior family labels, group_id values, storage membership, or family-membership edges as closure evidence. Record table_for links from each T object to every ordinary result it directly reports or contextualizes, with the appropriate role and closure_direction=none."
      },
      {
        "step": 4,
        "name": "Project the local graph",
        "instruction": "Create a directed subordinate-to-core graph: source_to_target adds source -> target; target_to_source adds target -> source; none adds no local edge. Every parent_field_id adds child field -> parent field. Named references add boundary references, not grouping edges. Exclude G/S metadata nodes, T source-table nodes, table_for links, CSV cell coordinates, and family-membership links. Table provenance never supplies an implicit child-to-parent edge. A self-loop is unnecessary and does not establish core status."
      },
      {
        "step": 5,
        "name": "Resolve cycles and candidate cores",
        "instruction": "Find strongly connected components and inspect every nontrivial cycle. Correct mistaken directions or accept a scientifically meaningful compound component with an explanation. Until resolved, affected results are review_required. Condense reviewed components and find terminal components with no outgoing local edge. Check that each candidate is a meaningful reported output, comparison, or descriptive result. A support-only or context sink is a review item, not automatically a result."
      },
      {
        "step": 6,
        "name": "Compute local closed sets",
        "instruction": "For each eligible terminal core or explained compound core, compute all reverse-reachable ancestors, including the core. Store the resulting IDs in closure.member_ids and the terminal IDs in closure.core_ids. Do not add all fields of a record merely because they share a storage container. Distinct terminal cores remain separate candidates unless an explicitly supported compound anchor connects their objects."
      },
      {
        "step": 7,
        "name": "Retain overlaps and boundary references",
        "instruction": "If an object feeds several terminal cores, retain its same ID in each applicable member set. Do not union the closures or duplicate the stored definition. Compute reference_ids as the union of: external endpoints of reviewed none relationships incident on members; named references attached to the result or members; T source IDs of table_for relationships targeting this result, whether or not its R ID belongs to member_ids; and table_ids in member fields' table_references. Remove member IDs and duplicates. A reference never imports a neighboring result or other table cells. Keep all incident scientific links available to explain the boundary."
      },
      {
        "step": 8,
        "name": "Check local closure",
        "instruction": "Check endpoint resolution, recomputed membership, eligible terminal cores, overlap, cycle explanations, and unreviewed incident links. Mark a closure closed only when its represented local boundary is resolved; otherwise use review_required and explain. Account for every in-scope object as a member, reference, clearly contextual item, cross-group summary, or explicit closure_review item. Closed status does not certify complete extraction of the paper. Account separately for all source tables and CSVs through table_coverage; closed local results do not certify table-export completeness."
      },
      {
        "step": 9,
        "name": "Form result families",
        "instruction": "Only after local closure, identify the question and generating investigation for each local result. Combine local results answering that question within that investigation; retain distinct questions, generating mechanisms, reviews, and separately identified numerical investigations. Assign one group_id per ordinary result and write grouping_reason. Family decisions may use source context and scientific relationships, but must not retroactively supply unrecorded local attachment edges."
      },
      {
        "step": 10,
        "name": "Designate main results and review families",
        "instruction": "Choose the family core_result_ids from its member result records. These are presentation and scientific-emphasis designations, distinct from local graph-terminal core_ids. Check unnecessary family splitting and merging. Cross-group summaries can describe several families without creating another independent evidence group. Record unresolved boundary judgments openly."
      }
    ],
    "direction_policy": [
      {
        "relation": "uncertainty_of",
        "typical_closure_direction": "source_to_target",
        "qualification": "Attach to the particular estimate whose uncertainty is reported."
      },
      {
        "relation": "spread_of",
        "typical_closure_direction": "source_to_target",
        "qualification": "Use locally for a particular distribution or group value; shared group identities may instead require none."
      },
      {
        "relation": "p_value_of",
        "typical_closure_direction": "source_to_target",
        "qualification": "Use the actual test, estimate, or known comparison anchor. Do not attach a joint or diagnostic test to an arbitrary individual estimate."
      },
      {
        "relation": "describes",
        "typical_closure_direction": "source_to_target",
        "qualification": "Use locally for result-specific characterization. Shared sample or contextual information can instead use none; its scientific connection remains recorded."
      },
      {
        "relation": "compares",
        "typical_closure_direction": "target_to_source",
        "qualification": "Specific numeric comparison inputs feed the comparison core. Shared treatment or exposure identities may instead use none. Evaluated/reference role is still required and is independent of closure direction."
      },
      {
        "relation": "derived_from",
        "typical_closure_direction": "target_to_source",
        "qualification": "Inputs feed an output within one local derivation. Use none when intentionally retaining an independently reported preceding result as a separate local result, and explain that boundary."
      },
      {
        "relation": "contributes_to",
        "typical_closure_direction": "source_to_target",
        "qualification": "Use only when contributors belong to the intended local pooled component. Use none to retain separately addressed contributing results without collapsing them into the pool."
      },
      {
        "relation": "applies_under",
        "typical_closure_direction": "none",
        "qualification": "Conditions and settings normally remain references. If a source-specific local inclusion is needed, explicitly review and explain the direction."
      },
      {
        "relation": "table_for",
        "typical_closure_direction": "none",
        "qualification": "Mandatory non-closing provenance/context link from a T source table to an ordinary R result. The role states whether the table reports the result, describes its sample, provides inputs, or supplies context. It never establishes local membership or family boundaries."
      },
      {
        "relation": "other",
        "typical_closure_direction": null,
        "qualification": "No automatic projection. Review source_to_target, target_to_source, or none and explain the scientific relationship in note."
      }
    ],
    "direction_policy_note": "The table guides a recorded decision; it does not turn an omitted closure_direction into a default. Missing means unreviewed. Scientific predicate directions remain unchanged. For table_for and every T endpoint, none is mandatory and must be written explicitly.",
    "overlap_rule": "Local member sets need not form a partition. One stored object may belong by ID to several closures. Shared inputs alone do not justify merging either local results or families. Shared source tables and CSV files also do not merge local results or families.",
    "cycle_rule": "Parent-field links must be acyclic. Nontrivial cycles among scientific grouping edges require a source-supported compound interpretation and notes, or remain review_required. Condensation is a graph operation, not automatic scientific acceptance. Multiple core_ids must identify one reviewed terminal strongly connected component. For a compound result with no mutual dependency, use an explicitly supported comparison or compound anchor and link its inputs; do not join unrelated terminal cores by listing them together.",
    "anchor_rule": "An identified comparison, joint test, or compound result may use its R-record ID as a core anchor. Its scientific role and incoming links must be explicit. A record container, component ID, or family label does not create an anchor merely by existing. A T object is a source container and cannot serve as a scientific anchor. A descriptive result may summarize table data only through its own scientifically justified fields and relationships.",
    "usually_keep_together": [
      "Different outcomes or measurement times within the same investigated relationship.",
      "Alternative adjustment specifications, doses, or subgroups within an investigation.",
      "Special cases, approximations, and supporting derivation steps within an analytical mechanism.",
      "Graph degree, population size, or graph-class variations within an identified simulation study.",
      "Pooled estimates, their study-level contributors, and supporting quality results within an intervention-specific evidence review."
    ],
    "usually_split": [
      "A distinct substantive research question.",
      "An independently identifiable investigation at the level being grouped.",
      "A different generating analytical mechanism, such as a different updating process.",
      "A separate intervention-specific evidence review.",
      "An independently identifiable numerical evaluation of an analytical result."
    ],
    "boundary_note": "The keep/split guidance applies to families after local closure. Source context resolves scientific boundaries that graph structure alone cannot establish. State the decision in grouping_reason or closure.notes at the level where it is made.",
    "cross_group_summaries": "Use cross_group_summaries for a synthesis across existing families. Such a synthesis references the underlying groups rather than being counted automatically as another independent evidence group.",
    "family_boundary_resolution": [
      "Keep local results in one family when they address a common substantive question within the same identifiable investigation. Split when source context establishes a distinct substantive question, an independently identifiable investigation, or a different generating mechanism.",
      "Differences in outcomes, measurement times, subgroups, adjustments, doses, or parameter values do not by themselves justify splitting. Shared data, inputs, equations, or broad subject matter do not by themselves justify merging.",
      "If broad and narrow boundaries remain plausible within a supported common investigation, prefer keeping the results together. Briefly record the alternative boundary and the reason for the chosen boundary in grouping_reason."
    ],
    "family_boundary_examples": {
      "keep_together": [
        "Unadjusted, adjusted, and subgroup estimates of the same association within one investigation: these are variations of the same analysis.",
        "Several outcomes and follow-up times used to assess the same treatment comparison in one experiment: these are different measurements within the investigation.",
        "An analytical result, its special cases, and supporting derivation steps under the same mechanism: these develop the same result."
      ],
      "split": [
        "The same dataset is used to investigate two distinct substantive questions with separately stated objectives: sharing data does not make them one family.",
        "Two analytical results arise from different updating processes: the generating mechanisms establish separate families even if the notation and outcomes are similar.",
        "A paper contains two independently identified simulation studies using the same analytical framework: sharing a framework does not make them one investigation."
      ]
    }
  },
  "x-id_convention": {
    "title": "Claims result-field ID convention",
    "purpose": "A short, repeatable convention for identifying result entries while leaving their names, labels, and context flexible.",
    "format": {
      "field_id": "{paper_id}.{extraction_id}.{record_key}.{field_key}",
      "record_id": "{paper_id}.{extraction_id}.{record_key}",
      "paper_id": "An assigned project-unique paper key. Use letters, digits, underscores, or hyphens; no dots or spaces. Preserve the assigned spelling and case.",
      "extraction_id": "E followed by a positive number assigned uniquely within that paper for an independently produced extraction.",
      "record_key": "R followed by a positive local result number, or C followed by a positive local shared-context number.",
      "field_key": "F followed by a positive number allocated within the record.",
      "number_format": "Use three digits, padded on the left with zeros, for E/R/C/F/G/S/T counters below 1000. Use all digits for larger numbers: F999, F1000. Counters start at 1, not 0.",
      "counter_scope": "E is allocated per paper; R, C, G, S, and T are separate sequences per extraction; F is allocated separately within each R or C record. T objects do not allocate F fields.",
      "case": "Case-sensitive; copy IDs exactly. E, R, C, F, G, S, and T prefixes are uppercase.",
      "path_convention": "Example paths use dot notation and zero-based square-bracket indexes, such as rows[0].groups[1].count. A path records current location and is not a permanent ID.",
      "examples": [
        "P001.E001.R002",
        "P001.E001.R002.F003",
        "P001.E001.C001.F001",
        "P001.E001.T001"
      ]
    },
    "instructions": [
      {
        "number": 1,
        "title": "Use an assigned paper and extraction prefix",
        "instruction": "Reuse the project's unique paper_id. Give each independently produced extraction or submission for that paper an assigned extraction_id, E001, E002, and so on. Obtain the prefix before numbering local records; different workers must not independently claim the same prefix. Corrections and continued work on the same extraction retain its extraction_id.",
        "example": "P001.E001 and P001.E002 are two independently produced extractions of paper P001."
      },
      {
        "number": 2,
        "title": "Number result records once",
        "instruction": "Use R001, R002, and so on for the result records already defined by the extraction procedure. Follow the supplied result-list order. If there is no supplied order, use first source appearance in the paper, then supplements in their stated order. Read tables from top to bottom and left to right. A result record can be a comparison, estimate, equation result, or series; the ID convention does not redefine its grouping. Result-family names or membership are not included in the ID.",
        "example": "P001.E001.R002 identifies a result record, not a new object category."
      },
      {
        "number": 3,
        "title": "Number the entries that need addresses",
        "instruction": "Assign F001, F002, and so on within a record to its result entries. An entry can be a number, text value, equation, interval, or collection. Follow the extraction template's field order. For extra fields, use initial source reading order; use alphabetical field names only as a tie-breaker if the source gives no order. The default is one ID per populated substantive field. Labels, units, source locations, and similar descriptive metadata do not need their own IDs unless they must be referenced separately. Skip empty fields unless the task explicitly requires an address for the empty entry.",
        "example": "P001.E001.R002.F001 is one entry allocated under result R002."
      },
      {
        "number": 4,
        "title": "Give different entries different IDs",
        "instruction": "Separate means, standard errors, confidence limits, tests, time points, or group values receive different IDs even when their values or labels are identical. A named field repeated across rows has a separate ID for each distinct addressed entry. A reference to an already identified shared item reuses that item's ID.",
        "example": "Two arm standard deviations of 0.4 still receive different IDs because they describe different arms."
      },
      {
        "number": 5,
        "title": "Keep meaning and location alongside the ID",
        "instruction": "Retain the existing field name, object label, value, units, and context. Record the current field path when a name alone would not identify a nested entry. Use neutral F-numbers in the permanent ID; do not include object labels, numerical values, treatment names, time values, source page numbers, or source equation numbers. An ID is assigned to the entry, not computed from its content.",
        "example": "The field can be named cf_sem and labeled standard_error while its ID remains P001.E001.R002.F002."
      },
      {
        "number": 6,
        "title": "Give shared context its own address",
        "instruction": "Use C001, C002, and so on for shared context records within the extraction. Number their entries with the same F001 convention. A sample size, parameter, or other context item known to be shared by several results can be stored once and referenced wherever used. Reuse requires the same identified sample or context item, not merely the same value.",
        "example": "P001.E001.C001.F001 can identify the participant sample size shared by several comparisons."
      },
      {
        "number": 7,
        "title": "Address collections only as finely as needed",
        "instruction": "An interval, curve, equation, or interpreted collection of table values can have one F field ID as a whole. Every numerical source table independently requires a T ID and complete CSV; a whole-table F value is not a substitute. Give a row, cell component, confidence limit, or equation term an additional F number when it needs independent scientific reference. Retain its parent F field and current field_path when appropriate; identify source cells separately through table_references. F numbers are permanent allocations, not current list indexes. Reordering changes locations, not IDs. A table, collection, and addressed members are different representations of the same source material, not independent observations.",
        "example": "A curve can be F001 and an addressed probability at [0].rho_C_digitized within that field's value can be F002. Store F001 as parent_field_id and [0].rho_C_digitized as field_path. If that point moves, retain F002 and update the path."
      },
      {
        "number": 8,
        "title": "Reuse IDs for clear repeated reporting",
        "instruction": "If a table, figure, and passage clearly report the same entry, retain one field ID with the relevant source locations.
