If the reported values conflict, preserve the distinct reported entries for resolution. When identity is uncertain, keep separate IDs and the existing explanation rather than deduplicating by value. When the same scientific entry appears in several exported tables, retain one F ID and list its source cells. Distinct source tables retain distinct T IDs even when some values repeat.",
        "example": "An estimate repeated in an abstract and table can share one ID; two different estimates equal to 0.58 cannot be merged on that basis."
      },
      {
        "number": 9,
        "title": "Preserve IDs when editing",
        "instruction": "Keep an ID when correcting the transcription, object label, field name, units, or description of the same entry. Keep it when reordering records or changing result-family membership. Store ordinary revision history with the data, not in the ID. If the same field is moved to another result record, it can be referenced there using its existing ID; the prefix records its original allocation, not a requirement about current placement.",
        "example": "Correcting an entry's label from standard_error to standard_deviation leaves its ID unchanged."
      },
      {
        "number": 10,
        "title": "Append new IDs and never recycle old ones",
        "instruction": "A newly discovered result or genuinely additional entry receives the next number never used in its applicable sequence. Preserve earlier IDs and gaps. Do not regenerate IDs from the current row order or reuse a retired ID. A separately stored calculated or converted quantity receives a new field ID and can be linked to its source with derived_from; correcting or changing the presentation of the same stored entry preserves its ID. If an entry is replaced by several genuinely distinct entries, assign new IDs and record which new entries replace the original. During record splits or merges, preserve existing field IDs where they still identify the same entries and record new group membership separately.",
        "example": "After F001 through F008 have been allocated, a new field receives F009 even if F004 was removed."
      },
      {
        "number": 11,
        "title": "Use full IDs in relationships",
        "instruction": "A relationship points to the full ID of the intended entry, known comparison anchor, result record, shared context, or source table. Table relationships always carry closure_direction=none. Named field associations can establish the scientific interpretation, but a link used by local closure must be recorded with reviewed closure_direction; parent_field_id has its specified implicit inclusion rule. Abbreviated IDs are for display only when the full prefix is unambiguous.",
        "example": "uncertainty_of(P001.E001.R002.F002, P001.E001.R002.F001) identifies the particular SE and mean."
      },
      {
        "number": 12,
        "title": "Check uniqueness and references before delivery",
        "instruction": "Ensure that each defined full ID identifies one stored entry or record, every relationship or shared-item reference resolves, and the used counters are retained for future additions. Repeated references to one ID are allowed; competing definitions under one ID are not. IDs in different extraction prefixes do not establish scientific equivalence; compare their content and sources when matching extractions.",
        "example": "P001.E001.R002.F001 and P001.E002.R002.F001 may identify different entries despite matching local numbers."
      },
      {
        "number": 13,
        "title": "Assign one ID and CSV filename per source table",
        "instruction": "Allocate T001, T002, and so on in first source-appearance order, then included supplements in source order. Continue the same T sequence across documents. Multi-panel and multi-page continuations retain one ID. Use the exact full T ID followed by .csv as the filename. Corrections and regrouping preserve that ID and filename; new tables take the next unused T number. Distinct tables in separately identified source versions retain distinct T IDs; document their relationship in notes.",
        "example": "P001.E001.T001 is exported as P001.E001.T001.csv and may link to several R records."
      }
    ],
    "supporting_notes": [
      "A field ID identifies a particular stored entry, not a field name or vocabulary category. Several entries labeled mean therefore receive different IDs.",
      "The pattern uses a record key so the same rule covers ordinary result records (R) and optional shared context records (C).",
      "IDs are permanent allocations. Their apparent numeric order is not a claim about scientific order, priority, or current array position.",
      "The convention can be implemented through existing per-object IDs, per-field metadata, or a lookup table from ID to current field path. It does not require restructuring the extraction.",
      "Independent extractors can follow the same rules while assigning different local numbers if their result grouping differs. The extraction prefix prevents collision; identical scientific interpretation is assessed using content and source locations.",
      "Local closure does not change ID identity. Member and reference lists reuse existing IDs; an object shared by closures is defined once. Group IDs and record allocation prefixes must not be used to derive closure membership."
    ],
    "organizational_extensions": {
      "group_id": "{paper_id}.{extraction_id}.G001",
      "source_id": "{paper_id}.{extraction_id}.S001",
      "instruction": "G, S, T, and A are separate positive counters per extraction for result groups, source documents, source tables, and data or code artifacts. They do not change the R/C/F field-ID convention. Cross-group summaries use the same R sequence as ordinary results.",
      "nested_location": "A field_path accompanying parent_field_id is relative to the parent F value and uses zero-based indexes. table_references.row instead uses one-based parsed CSV data-record positions, excluding the header; table_references.column uses an exact column key. Both describe current location, not permanent identity.",
      "table_id": "{paper_id}.{extraction_id}.T001",
      "artifact_id": "{paper_id}.{extraction_id}.A001"
    }
  },
  "x-controlled_vocabularies": {
    "title": "Claims: simple controlled vocabularies",
    "purpose": "Consistent, compact labels for common inference-relevant objects and scientific relationships. Other is an acceptable classification.",
    "usage": [
      "Field lists give typical choices, not an exhaustive classification of everything in a paper. Other is a normal, valid choice.",
      "Use the same code for the same kind of object wherever it appears. Codes use lowercase words separated by underscores; readable labels are supplied.",
      "Field groups collect equivalent existing fields for reference. They are not new required fields or instructions to rename the extraction.",
      "The existing description, value, units, context, and source snippets remain available. A generic value field takes the label of the object it represents.",
      "Use existing measure fields for numerical form when available: for example, treatment_effect with measure odds_ratio. No new field is required solely for this vocabulary.",
      "A clear field association is sufficient to represent a relationship. Add an explicit link only when useful to identify its target.",
      "Illustrative examples show label use, not a required record format. Unusual objects and relationships can retain other with their existing descriptions."
    ],
    "object_labels": {
      "mean": {
        "label": "Mean",
        "definition": "A reported mean. The existing group, outcome, and condition information identifies what is averaged."
      },
      "count": {
        "label": "Count",
        "definition": "A number of units, events, reports, studies, comparisons, or other items. The field and context identify what is counted."
      },
      "proportion": {
        "label": "Proportion",
        "definition": "A share of a specified group or set, expressed as a fraction or percentage."
      },
      "probability": {
        "label": "Probability",
        "definition": "A probability or estimate of a probability, such as fixation probability. The event remains identified in context."
      },
      "sample_size": {
        "label": "Sample size",
        "definition": "The number of observational or experimental units in the relevant sample, group, or analysis."
      },
      "treatment_effect": {
        "label": "Treatment effect",
        "definition": "A reported outcome contrast between intervention and reference conditions. Its interpretation follows the study design and existing context."
      },
      "association": {
        "label": "Association",
        "definition": "An estimated relationship between an exposure or predictor and an outcome, including observational regression estimates."
      },
      "standard_error": {
        "label": "Standard error",
        "definition": "The reported standard error of an estimate, including the standard error of a mean."
      },
      "standard_deviation": {
        "label": "Standard deviation",
        "definition": "The reported standard deviation of observations or values in a group or distribution. Keep distinct from standard error."
      },
      "confidence_interval": {
        "label": "Confidence interval",
        "definition": "An interval reported as a confidence interval for an estimate."
      },
      "confidence_limit": {
        "label": "Confidence limit",
        "definition": "An individual lower or upper endpoint of a confidence interval. Its existing field identifies which endpoint it is."
      },
      "p_value": {
        "label": "P-value",
        "definition": "A p-value for the identified test or comparison."
      },
      "outcome": {
        "label": "Outcome",
        "definition": "The measured, modeled, or calculated outcome addressed by a result. Its substantive name remains in the existing field."
      },
      "intervention": {
        "label": "Intervention",
        "definition": "A treatment, action, or experimental condition, whether it is the evaluated intervention or the comparator."
      },
      "exposure": {
        "label": "Exposure",
        "definition": "An exposure or predictor whose relationship with an outcome is studied."
      },
      "group": {
        "label": "Group",
        "definition": "A sample, population, or group referred to by a result or comparison."
      },
      "time_point": {
        "label": "Time point",
        "definition": "A single time at which a measurement or event is located, using its existing reference event and units."
      },
      "time_interval": {
        "label": "Time interval",
        "definition": "A duration or time window, including a measurement, integration, or recall window."
      },
      "parameter": {
        "label": "Parameter",
        "definition": "A parameter or numerical setting used in an equation, model, or simulation."
      },
      "threshold": {
        "label": "Threshold",
        "definition": "A cutoff used to classify cases or identify when a condition is met."
      },
      "increment": {
        "label": "Increment",
        "definition": "The change in an exposure or input over which a reported effect or association is expressed."
      },
      "equation": {
        "label": "Equation or formula",
        "definition": "A mathematical equation, formula, or inequality. The stored expression retains its particular form."
      },
      "assumption": {
        "label": "Assumption",
        "definition": "A stated premise under which a result is derived or applied."
      },
      "condition": {
        "label": "Condition",
        "definition": "A stated requirement attached to a result or formula."
      },
      "absolute_change": {
        "label": "Absolute change",
        "definition": "A difference expressed in the outcome's units. The existing definition and context retain its direction and reference."
      },
      "relative_change": {
        "label": "Relative change",
        "definition": "A change relative to a reference quantity, often expressed as a percentage. Keep the reference and direction in existing fields."
      },
      "odds_ratio": {
        "label": "Odds ratio",
        "definition": "An odds ratio for the stated comparison or exposure increment. Keep the model and outcome coding in context."
      },
      "other": {
        "label": "Other",
        "definition": "An entry that does not fit the typical labels, or whose classification is not established. Retain its existing description, value, units, and context."
      }
    },
    "field_vocabularies": [
      {
        "code": "result_estimate",
        "name": "Main result or estimate",
        "existing_field_examples": [
          "estimate",
          "change_pct",
          "rho_C_digitized"
        ],
        "typical_labels": [
          "treatment_effect",
          "association",
          "proportion",
          "probability",
          "other"
        ],
        "note": "Choose from the actual meaning. An estimate of the fraction of positive studies is a proportion."
      },
      {
        "code": "descriptive_value",
        "name": "Group values and descriptive statistics",
        "existing_field_examples": [
          "cf_mean",
          "cl_mean",
          "normal_mean",
          "hearing_loss_mean",
          "treated_value",
          "control_value",
          "count",
          "percent"
        ],
        "typical_labels": [
          "mean",
          "count",
          "proportion",
          "other"
        ],
        "note": "A field explicitly reporting a mean uses mean. A generic treated_value or control_value can remain other if its statistic is unclear."
      },
      {
        "code": "effect_measure",
        "name": "Numerical form of an effect or estimate",
        "existing_field_examples": [
          "measure",
          "estimate_type"
        ],
        "typical_labels": [
          "absolute_change",
          "relative_change",
          "odds_ratio",
          "proportion",
          "other"
        ],
        "note": "This uses existing measure fields. For example, the result can be treatment_effect and its measure odds_ratio. A label change does not change numerical signs or reference conventions."
      },
      {
        "code": "uncertainty_variation",
        "name": "Uncertainty and variation",
        "existing_field_examples": [
          "cf_sem",
          "cl_sem",
          "treated_sd",
          "control_sd",
          "ci95",
          "ci_lower",
          "ci_upper",
          "normal_ci_lower",
          "normal_ci_upper",
          "hearing_loss_ci_lower",
          "hearing_loss_ci_upper"
        ],
        "typical_labels": [
          "standard_error",
          "standard_deviation",
          "confidence_interval",
          "confidence_limit",
          "other"
        ],
        "note": "SEM fields use standard_error; SD fields use standard_deviation. Use confidence_interval for the whole interval and confidence_limit for a separate endpoint. Printed and figure-read values share these labels."
      },
      {
        "code": "test_result",
        "name": "Statistical test results",
        "existing_field_examples": [
          "p",
          "test_p",
          "heterogeneity_p",
          "test.value"
        ],
        "typical_labels": [
          "p_value",
          "other"
        ],
        "note": "Use the same p_value label for different tests. Existing test identity or a link states what was tested."
      },
      {
        "code": "sample_evidence_count",
        "name": "Sample and evidence counts",
        "existing_field_examples": [
          "n",
          "treated_n",
          "control_n",
          "n_treated",
          "n_control",
          "n_animals",
          "n_reports",
          "n_experiments",
          "n_comparisons",
          "positive_studies",
          "available_studies",
          "treated_events",
          "control_events"
        ],
        "typical_labels": [
          "sample_size",
          "count",
          "other"
        ],
        "note": "Use sample_size for units in the relevant sample or analysis. Other counts stay count; retain what is counted and which result or group it concerns."
      },
      {
        "code": "outcome_field",
        "name": "Outcome",
        "existing_field_examples": [
          "outcome",
          "outcome_id",
          "endpoint",
          "measurement"
        ],
        "typical_labels": [
          "outcome",
          "other"
        ],
        "note": "Use outcome when the field names the studied outcome. Some measurement fields instead describe an operation such as iAUC; retain that wording rather than automatically relabeling it."
      },
      {
        "code": "intervention_exposure_comparator",
        "name": "Treatment, exposure, and comparator",
        "existing_field_examples": [
          "treatment",
          "comparator",
          "exposure_id",
          "condition_id",
          "hearing_groups"
        ],
        "typical_labels": [
          "intervention",
          "exposure",
          "group",
          "other"
        ],
        "note": "An intervention keeps the same label in treatment and comparator fields. A numeric comparator such as >= is an operator, not a comparison object; keep it as recorded."
      },
      {
        "code": "time",
        "name": "Time",
        "existing_field_examples": [
          "time_label",
          "start_min",
          "end_or_point_min",
          "time_window",
          "time_scope_id"
        ],
        "typical_labels": [
          "time_point",
          "time_interval",
          "other"
        ],
        "note": "The existing time fields and context retain the reference event, units, and interval endpoints."
      },
      {
        "code": "numerical_context",
        "name": "Numerical context and settings",
        "existing_field_examples": [
          "context",
          "exposure_increment",
          "increment",
          "parameter_id",
          "N",
          "k",
          "w"
        ],
        "typical_labels": [
          "parameter",
          "threshold",
          "increment",
          "count",
          "other"
        ],
        "note": "Use threshold for a classification cutoff and increment for an exposure scale, even when both have the same value. Other contextual entries may remain other."
      },
      {
        "code": "mathematics",
        "name": "Mathematical results and assumptions",
        "existing_field_examples": [
          "equations",
          "expression",
          "assumption_ids",
          "domain_condition",
          "clauses"
        ],
        "typical_labels": [
          "equation",
          "parameter",
          "assumption",
          "condition",
          "other"
        ],
        "note": "Keep mathematical details in the stored expression and context. These labels do not require a detailed mathematical taxonomy."
      }
    ],
    "relationship_labels": [
      {
        "code": "uncertainty_of",
        "label": "Uncertainty of",
        "meaning": "Links a standard error, confidence interval, or confidence limit to its estimate.",
        "direction": "Uncertainty measure -> estimate",
        "note": "A clearly named field or interval target already supplies the link. Standard deviations use spread_of."
      },
      {
        "code": "spread_of",
        "label": "Spread of",
        "meaning": "Links a standard deviation to the group, distribution, or reported group value it describes.",
        "direction": "Standard deviation -> described group or group value",
        "note": "The field or context can identify the relevant treatment or control group."
      },
      {
        "code": "p_value_of",
        "label": "P-value for",
        "meaning": "Links a p-value to its particular test or comparison.",
        "direction": "P-value -> test or comparison",
        "note": "Keep treatment, association, heterogeneity, and other test targets clear; they share the p_value object label."
      },
      {
        "code": "describes",
        "label": "Describes",
        "meaning": "Links a mean, proportion, count, sample size, or other value to the sample, group, outcome, or result it describes.",
        "direction": "Value -> described sample, group, outcome, or result",
        "note": "A sample or evidence count must remain attached to the result or set it actually counts."
      },
      {
        "code": "compares",
        "label": "Compares",
        "meaning": "Identifies the evaluated condition or exposure and the reference for an effect or comparison.",
        "direction": "Effect or comparison -> named target and reference",
        "note": "Existing treatment/exposure and comparator fields are sufficient. If explicit links are used, identify which target is evaluated and which is the reference; two unqualified links are insufficient."
      },
      {
        "code": "derived_from",
        "label": "Derived from",
        "meaning": "Identifies the numerical inputs or preceding results used to calculate or derive an output.",
        "direction": "Output or result -> input quantity, equation, or preceding result",
        "note": "Retain the existing formula or explanation of the calculation."
      },
      {
        "code": "contributes_to",
        "label": "Contributes to",
        "meaning": "Identifies an individual result included in a pooled or combined result.",
        "direction": "Contributing result -> pooled or combined result",
        "note": "The relationship denotes contribution, not independent support for every conclusion of the synthesis."
      },
      {
        "code": "applies_under",
        "label": "Applies under",
        "meaning": "Associates a result or equation with its relevant time, parameter setting, assumption, or condition.",
        "direction": "Result or equation -> time, setting, parameter, assumption, or condition",
        "note": "Existing fields can carry this association. Preserve the stated condition and any logical wording in context."
      },
      {
        "code": "table_for",
        "label": "Table for result",
        "meaning": "Links one source table to an ordinary result that it reports, whose sample it describes, to which it supplies inputs, or for which it provides context.",
        "direction": "Source table T -> ordinary result R",
        "note": "role is reports_result, describes_sample, provides_inputs, or provides_context. closure_direction must be none. Link a table to every relevant ordinary result without making the table an independent result, evidential-strength assessment, or family boundary."
      },
      {
        "code": "other",
        "label": "Other",
        "meaning": "A relationship outside the short common list.",
        "direction": "As described in the existing record",
        "note": "Retain its description and endpoints; there is no requirement to force it into a common label."
      }
    ]
  },
  "x-validation_checks": [
    {
      "id": "V01",
      "kind": "reference",
      "check": "Every source, group, table, record, and field ID has one physical definition in the extraction and the correct paper/extraction prefix. The same ID may occur in many closure member/reference lists without becoming a duplicate definition."
    },
    {
      "id": "V02",
      "kind": "reference",
      "check": "Every relationship endpoint, named reference, parent_field_id, snippet source_id, family membership, family main-result ID, closure core/member/reference ID, and closure_review object_id resolves to an appropriate defined item. Table source_id, coverage reviewed_source_ids, table_for targets, and field table_references.table_id also resolve to their specified object types; table_for targets must be ordinary results, not cross-group summaries."
    },
    {
      "id": "V03",
      "kind": "grouping",
      "check": "Each ordinary result has one primary family through group_id; each family has results and its core_result_ids belong to it. Assign these after local closure. Cross-group summaries reference existing families without owning their evidence."
    },
    {
      "id": "V04",
      "kind": "identity",
      "check": "Moved fields retain their IDs and may have an earlier R/C allocation prefix. Neither ID prefix nor current storage container determines local membership or family membership."
    },
    {
      "id": "V05",
      "kind": "reference",
      "check": "Parent-field links are acyclic, field_path resolves relative to parent.value, and the addressed value agrees. Include the specified child -> parent edge when recomputing local closure; update paths after reordering without changing IDs."
    },
    {
      "id": "V06",
      "kind": "vocabulary",
      "check": "Object and relation codes occur in the embedded controlled vocabulary libraries. Typical field-vocabulary lists are advisory, and other is valid."
    },
    {
      "id": "V07",
      "kind": "scientific_attachment",
      "check": "Explicit compares relationships retain evaluated/reference roles, with the other role present or explicit in context. Scientific comparison orientation and closure_direction are different properties; neither can be inferred merely from raw endpoint order."
    },
    {
      "id": "V08",
      "kind": "scientific_attachment",
      "check": "Uncertainty and p-values target their actual estimates or tests. Counts retain their counted set. A joint test is not copied into independent category tests, and a shared sample is not substituted for a result-specific analysis count. Standard deviations ordinarily use spread_of; standard errors and confidence intervals use uncertainty_of. Preserve diagnostic-test targets separately from treatment-effect tests."
    },
    {
      "id": "V09",
      "kind": "source",
      "check": "Values, units, signs, bounds, operators, contrast orientation, and approximations agree with the source or an explicitly marked calculation. Other retains an intelligible description in value, context, or notes."
    },
    {
      "id": "V10",
      "kind": "grouping",
      "check": "Family boundaries use the question and generating investigation only after local closed sets have been derived. Shared samples, assumptions, sources, and cross-result dependencies do not automatically merge families."
    },
    {
      "id": "V11",
      "kind": "source",
      "check": "Verbatim snippets remain distinguishable from summaries. Source-reported records trace to a source and locator; calculations identify their inputs or basis."
    },
    {
      "id": "V12",
      "kind": "closure",
      "check": "Recompute each member_ids set from core_ids using reviewed closure_direction projections and implicit parent links. It must exactly equal the reverse-reachable ancestor set including the core. Do not add storage siblings, family members, or unrestricted graph neighbors."
    },
    {
      "id": "V13",
      "kind": "closure",
      "check": "Core IDs belong to eligible terminal components of the projected local graph. G/S metadata and T source containers cannot be local cores or members. Support-only sinks do not automatically become cores. Compound cores and nontrivial cycles require a scientific explanation or review_required status; family core_result_ids do not substitute for local core_ids. Multiple core IDs must represent one reviewed terminal strongly connected component; otherwise use an explicitly linked compound anchor."
    },
    {
      "id": "V14",
      "kind": "closure",
      "check": "A missing closure_direction is unreviewed, not none. A closure with an unresolved incident relationship or boundary cannot be marked closed. Known local links have a recorded direction; non-closing connections retain none and their appropriate references."
    },
    {
      "id": "V15",
      "kind": "closure",
      "check": "Recompute reference_ids from external endpoints of reviewed none links incident on members, named references attached to the result or members, T source IDs of table_for links targeting the result record, and table_ids in member fields' table_references. Remove member IDs and duplicates. Include linked T objects even when the R record is not itself a closure member. Do not traverse references into neighboring closures or other table cells."
    },
    {
      "id": "V16",
      "kind": "closure",
      "check": "Overlap is permitted: shared inputs keep one ID in all applicable closures and one physical definition. Repeated numeric values alone never establish a shared object. Parent/member views are not additional independent evidence."
    },
    {
      "id": "V17",
      "kind": "coverage",
      "check": "Every in-scope scientific object is a closure member, an external reference, clearly contextual information, cross-group summary content, or an explicit closure_review item. Preserve unresolved objects and explain review_required results; do not silently drop unsupported sinks. Account for source tables separately through tables[] and extraction.table_coverage. Complete local closures do not imply complete numerical-table export."
    },
    {
      "id": "V18",
      "kind": "comparison",
      "check": "When computing grouping, use the object inventory and source scope, not existing group labels or family-membership edges. Preserve identifiable comparison anchors and source-supported links; check memberships, attachment targets, coverage, and unresolved judgments."
    },
    {
      "id": "V19",
      "kind": "scientific_attachment",
      "check": "Review terminal definitions and locally bound symbols used in structured equations. A locally defined coefficient used by another equation needs an appropriate derived_from link or an explicit contextual reference. Resolve scoped symbol identities before adding a dependency; identical printed symbols alone are insufficient."
    },
    {
      "id": "V20",
      "kind": "coverage",
      "check": "Review the actual main source version and all associated supplements supplied or identified by that source and compare their numerical-table inventory with tables[]. Include descriptive, baseline, diagnostic, and supplementary numerical tables regardless of significance or importance. A partial result or supplementary-document selection does not excuse incomplete table export. A completed submission requires table_coverage.status=complete, all required sources reviewed, no missing or unreadable required table regions, and all exports delivered. Unavailable required supplements make coverage incomplete. An incomplete instructional example is not a completed submission."
    },
    {
      "id": "V21",
      "kind": "identity",
      "check": "There is exactly one T object and one CSV per qualifying source table. Merge page continuations and panels into that export; do not create copies per result or family. Distinct source tables remain distinct. csv_file equals id + \".csv\" exactly and is a basename in the extraction JSON directory; no absolute paths, subdirectories, or traversal components are permitted."
    },
    {
      "id": "V22",
      "kind": "file",
      "check": "Open every recorded CSV file from the delivered package. It must exist, decode as UTF-8 without BOM, parse under x-table_extraction.csv_format, and contain one header whose unique keys exactly equal columns[].name in order. Every parsed data record has that many cells and the total equals row_count. A syntactically valid filename does not prove the file exists."
    },
    {
      "id": "V23",
      "kind": "source",
      "check": "Reconstruct every numerical entry and its interpretation from the CSV, column metadata, caption, and scoped annotations. Check source row order, row and panel identity, complete header hierarchy, units, footnotes, numerical precision, signs, inequalities, composite expressions, significance markers, and missing-value meanings. Do not silently correct source errors, round values, discard cells not individually extracted, or treat layout-induced blanks as reported missing values."
    },
    {
      "id": "V24",
      "kind": "reference",
      "check": "Every table has at least one table_for relationship to an appropriate ordinary result. Link all directly reported results and any explicitly used sample, input, or context roles. Check role against the source. A descriptive result is valid; no invented effect or main finding is required. Each such link has source T, target R, and closure_direction=none; every other T relationship also has none."
    },
    {
      "id": "V25",
      "kind": "reference",
      "check": "For every table_references entry, resolve the table and CSV, verify 1 <= row <= row_count, and resolve column to exactly one header key. Check the selected cell or component against the field value, units, operators, and source context. Directly cell-reported independent fields require these references when table coverage is complete. Header/caption/footnote quantities instead use a precise source snippet and a named table reference. Derived quantities identify their source fields and calculation rather than claiming to be reported in an input cell."
    },
    {
      "id": "V26",
      "kind": "source",
      "check": "All annotation rows and columns resolve within the table; scopes agree with the source. Merged numeric cells are transcribed once at the first covered row and leftmost covered exported column, with the whole span recorded as merged_cell and the other covered cells left blank. Shared tests and notes must not be represented as independent observations for each row."
    },
    {
      "id": "V27",
      "kind": "closure",
      "check": "T objects and CSV coordinates never enter core_ids, member_ids, parent_field_id, or implicit local edges. Cell provenance and source-table identity do not create scientific dependence, expand local closure to adjacent cells, or merge families. An interpreted F collection and its source T object are not independent evidence."
    },
    {
      "id": "V28",
      "kind": "availability",
      "check": "Every ArtifactLink url or identifier appears verbatim in at least one of its source_snippets, and each snippet traces to a listed source and locator. Links that the source does not print are fabricated and invalid."
    },
    {
      "id": "V29",
      "kind": "availability",
      "check": "Statuses agree with the source: a data or code statement marked available, available_on_request, restricted, or not_available quotes the statement; not_stated is used only when the reviewed sources contain none; an artifact of type dataset or code is consistent with the corresponding statement (for example, a printed GitHub link for the analysis code is not compatible with code not_applicable)."
    },
    {
      "id": "V30",
      "kind": "availability",
      "check": "Artifacts are not duplicated: the same URL or identifier repeated across the article and supplements is one entry with several snippets. related_ids resolve to existing objects and never enter core_ids, member_ids, or local edges."
    }
  ],
  "x-context_guidance": {
    "description": "Context is ordinary text or an open object. These are useful familiar names, not compulsory fields or a new controlled taxonomy.",
    "suggested_properties": {
      "outcome": "What was measured or modeled, including any operation such as iAUC.",
      "treatment": "The evaluated intervention or condition.",
      "comparator": "The reference intervention or condition; numeric inequalities belong in value_operator.",
      "exposure": "The exposure or predictor for an association.",
      "population": "Species, participants, cohort, or other units studied.",
      "sample": "Which sample or analysis subset applies.",
      "time": "Time point or window and its reference event.",
      "method": "The measurement, calculation, or test procedure.",
      "model": "The statistical or theoretical model relevant to this result.",
      "adjustment": "Covariates or other adjustment information.",
      "weighting": "Whether and how values are weighted.",
      "assumptions": "Conditions under which the result is derived or applied.",
      "direction": "What is compared with what, including the meaning of positive or negative changes.",
      "counted_entity": "What a count or proportion counts and its relevant denominator set."
    }
  },
  "x-value_guidance": [
    {
      "case": "Bounded p-value",
      "example": {
        "label": "p_value",
        "value": 0.001,
        "value_operator": "<"
      },
      "instruction": "Preserve the inequality instead of treating the reported boundary as exact."
    },
    {
      "case": "Whole confidence interval",
      "example": {
        "label": "confidence_interval",
        "value": {
          "lower": 0.41,
          "upper": 0.83
        },
        "confidence_level": 0.95
      },
      "instruction": "Keep its estimate target clear. Separate endpoints may instead use confidence_limit."
    },
    {
      "case": "Equation",
      "example": {
        "label": "equation",
        "value": "b/c > k"
      },
      "instruction": "Keep variable meanings and assumptions in context or references. A detailed expression taxonomy is optional."
    },
    {
      "case": "Unusual entry",
      "example": {
        "label": "other",
        "value": "A source-specific description"
      },
      "instruction": "Other is a normal choice. Keep sufficient existing description or context to interpret the entry."
    }
  ],
  "x-technical_reference": {
    "format": "JSON Schema Draft 2020-12",
    "url": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/json-schema-core",
    "note": "The x- sections are application instructions. Ordinary JSON Schema validation checks structure; graph reachability, terminal-core eligibility, cross-record references, source fidelity, family boundaries, numerical-table coverage, filename-to-ID equality, and actual CSV presence/content require the documented semantic checks."
  },

Source-task consolidation (21 November 2026): Before targets and sampling weights
freeze, a source-only pass consolidates restatements of the same scientific
quantity (§4.1). It preserves distinct analyses, values, uncertainty, conditions
and all original cited anchors. The independent reviewer compares both complete
drafts with the source and may correct lost information or unjustified merging.
Both drafts, the consolidation request and the review remain replayable. This
step never consults a miner output or changes a submitted extraction.

Caption/reporting-convention clarification (21 November 2026): A caption that
identifies a table/cohort and says how values are reported is SOURCE_CONTEXT.
It does not make every referenced table result one scientific target. Findings
explicitly reported in captions/notes remain SCIENTIFIC_RESULT, and independently
owned numerical table rows remain eligible. Mixed spans retain both kinds.

## Owner-approved diagnostic recovery extension — 23 November 2026

A released round may have a separately identified diagnostic recovery evaluation.
Keep its original status, receipts, scores, sample and miner outputs unchanged.
Freeze the recovery's target configuration, original source/output-pool hashes,
actor and reason in an immutable manifest. Preserve the original sampling secret;
commit it against the target policy. Source interpretation changes apply commonly
to all entrants. A changed policy requires re-evaluation, while only an identical
policy may retain FROZEN scores. Recovery paper heads and clocks are isolated;
ordinary resumes do not reset elapsed time or provider-attempt bounds. Retain
previous generations and report recovery progress separately from original progress.
Diagnostic recovery cannot select a competition winner or issue on-chain weights.

Docling source classification covers numerical-table blocks as well as prose.
Navigation tables and contents lists are retained evidence but are not qualifying
scientific numerical tables. OCR fragments without a coherent stated result are
not independently sampled prose results; their anchors remain available for
context and output-support checks. A higher-resolution Docling OCR profile may be
frozen before recovery; no model image crops or invented plot values are required.

Preserve unknown billing. Provider generation metadata may resolve a captured
generation ID with a retained proof; an absent ID or rate-limit message alone
does not prove zero cost. A request proven not to have reached dispatch has known
zero cost. Diagnostic budgets retain frozen per-request reservations for unobserved
charges across crashes and block additional requests when committed spend leaves
insufficient reserve. Reservations and measured spend are reported separately.
